# Deep audit, October 16, 2026

## Scope

This audit covered the working tree of `main` at `47b5285` (the merge of PR #120) together with the uncommitted work in 19 files (`ablation.py`, `train_vfe3.py`, `vfe3/config.py`, `vfe3/train.py`, `paper_arms.py`, `multiseed_analysis.py`, `train_baseline.py`, `vfe3/viz/figure_worker.py`, several tests and daily logs), which was audited as it stands. Since the last full audit (`docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12-round2.md`, taken at `822f3d1`), 66 commits in PRs #99 to #120 added the covariance ceiling and its gradient-route fix, the fp64 escalation of failing sequences, the selectable moment-matched floor, divergence-spike attribution, the `fully_gauge_covariant` switch, the LR width-transfer sweep, the block_glmc local basis, the SU(N) irrep towers (M1 and M2), and the block_sl, block_borel and block_unipotent groups. That diff and the uncommitted edits were the first priority of one investigator; the others swept the whole source tree by domain.

The active configuration was the `train_vfe3.py` dictionary as it stands: K = 160 with five heads, four layers, N = 128, `gaussian_diagonal`, `projected_predictive` with the moment-matched covariance and the kernel readout, the inverse receiver measurement, block_glk per-head frames, comparison-invariant RoPE at base 100, the causal ALiBi prior, the coordinate block MLP behind a LayerNorm with covariance pushforward, the `diagonal_chunked` decoder with the Triton cross-entropy and bf16 logits, the Fisher table M-step, AdamW frames and group-scope clipping. The theory references were `GL(K)_attention_v2.tex`, its supplement, `minimal_agent_model.tex`, `edge_latent_links.tex` and `dynamic_links.tex`.

## Method

Seventeen domain-expert investigators ran in parallel on assigned areas (table below). After the first round a coverage pass compared the files each investigator reported reading against the 125 non-test source files. 122 had been read, `paper_arms.py` had only been skimmed, and the two unread files were package `__init__` stubs. A completeness critic then added four gap investigations for scope that no single investigator owned: the live Triton decode cross-entropy, the paper-arm table and multiseed aggregation, checkpoint and resume exactness, and the opt-in readout and link routes. All 21 investigators returned. A merge agent grouped their 149 raw findings by shared root cause into the 128 entries below; it was told not to judge correctness and could not raise a severity above the highest one a member reported. Writer agents rendered the entries from the investigators' structured output without re-investigating. Every agent ran on the session model (Claude Opus 5.5).

All probes ran on CPU torch, single-threaded, on tiny models, one script at a time per investigator; the decode gap investigator ran the Triton kernels in interpreter mode on CPU. No GPU was used, no full test suite was run, and no repository file was edited. Old payloads came from `git show` copies of earlier trees in the session scratchpad. Following the standing rule, no adjudication ran: there was no skeptic, defender or verifier panel, so each entry is the evidence of the investigators who reported it, with executed probes distinguished from source reading, and none is closed. The coordinator checked four claims against the source. The uncommitted `block_norm_covariance` default flip to `"pushforward"` at `vfe3/config.py:648` has no row in `_HISTORICAL_DEFAULT_PINS` (`vfe3/config.py:3045-3055`), although the same edit annotates the `decode_logits_amp` and `decode_ce_triton` rows. `_table_fisher_step` ends with `p.grad = None` (`vfe3/table_optim.py:193`). `_spike_attribution` skips every parameter whose gradient is `None` (`vfe3/train.py:3943`). The LayerNorm trace cap rescales by `clamp(K * max(gamma^2) / trace, max=1)` (`vfe3/model/block_mlp.py:496-499`).

## Verdict

No investigator rated a finding critical, and four findings are rated high. The uncommitted default flip of `block_norm_covariance` to `"pushforward"` was made without a historical pin, so every config serialized before that field existed, including the 36.41 base of `paper_arms.py`, reloads with a different forward; the same reload gap applies to the moment-matched floor, which now resolves to `'prior'` for configs saved before 2026-10-12 that ran the reference-ridge floor (item 1). On the full family the capped LayerNorm pushforward is near rank one and singular in float32, which broke the first forward in about 10 percent of accepted tiny full-family LayerNorm configurations and saturates the router clamp on the pure path that `fully_gauge_covariant` selects (item 2). On the diagonal family at the live operating point the capped pushforward has the right trace but the wrong shape: per-coordinate variance tracks the squared normalized mean rather than the Monte Carlo law, and covariance changes pass downstream about 33 times more weakly than in the valid regime. This was measured at initialization only (item 3). Spike attribution and the parameter-motion report cannot see `mu_embed` and `sigma_log_embed` on the Fisher table route, or phi under the group updates, because those optimizers clear `.grad` inside `step()`; the spike records therefore omit the tables that the 2026-10-15 instability audit named as amplifiers (item 4).

The 26 medium findings cluster in a few places. Two concern configuration: the new pushforward default is refused under every non-LayerNorm norm, so `parameter_optimization.py` and two paper arms no longer build (item 5), and the strict pullback closure certificate rejects valid so_n towers with N of 4 or more (item 6). In the covariance numerics, the float32 storage of the attended covariance before the diagonal sandwich and the unscreened minimal-agent posterior covariance both give positive but inaccurate variances that the sign-only escalation misses (items 7 and 8). On routing, `cholesky_qr2_certified` disables the closed-form diagonal comparison route on the live config, and the metrics of the current K = 160 run show one layer whose every causal pair cost sits at the 1280-nat cap at steps 1200 and 2400, which zeroes that layer's router gradient (items 9 and 10). The variational, decoder and training items cover the so-called M-step self-coupling term, which back-propagates through the converged belief rather than acting on the priors, the missing monitor for the untied decode variance table, a per-group update metric divided by an inert AdamW rate, and a hard-coded tolerance that, not the trust radius, bounds the group frame step on the chart retraction (items 11 to 14). The experiment drivers discard seeded rows in the width-transfer fit, drop diverged seeds from across-seed aggregates, compare a greedy GPT-2 control against sampled VFE continuations, ship a `grow_K_mup` that is not muP, cannot start the TIER_B launch of `paper_arms.py`, and widen the compute-matched baseline eightfold at its base-width learning rate (items 15 to 20). Several reported figures and contracts misstate what they show, including the weight-decay reference of the paper's sigma-schedule panel, the optimization field of the methods contract, the training-loss figure on resumed runs, the layer mix of the numerical-trust figure, and the channel names of the beta decomposition (items 21 to 25). Four performance items on the active path concern tile budgets, a materialized copy of T, dense certificate work and dense float64 residual checks (items 26 to 29), and the last medium item records that no test checks the capped pushforward against Monte Carlo or builds a model under `fully_gauge_covariant` (item 30). The 83 low findings and 15 improvements follow, and the appendix lists what the investigators checked and cleared.

Coverage has known limits. No GPU or compiled Triton path was executed, so CUDA behavior and every wall-clock estimate in the performance items rest on reading, op counts and the interpreter. The `pullback_group` and `fisher_group` direction solvers were probed piecemeal but never run end to end; the critic left them out because they are inactive on the launcher. Whether the capped-pushforward regime of items 2 and 3 persists past initialization needs a training run, which was not made.

## High-severity findings

### 1. Uncommitted default flips (block_norm_covariance to pushforward, relative floor now a separate mode) lack _HISTORICAL_DEFAULT_PINS rows, so older configs, including the paper_arms k128_alibi 36.41 base, silently reload with a different forward (high)

The defect sits at `vfe3/config.py:648` (the default), `vfe3/config.py:3031-3055` (the `_HISTORICAL_DEFAULT_PINS` table and its rule), `vfe3/config.py:3157-3162` (the `payload.setdefault(name, historical)` migration), `vfe3/config.py:535-540` and `vfe3/config.py:1777-1789`, with consumers at `paper_arms.py:8-14`, `paper_arms.py:146`, `paper_arms.py:161-164`, `paper_arms.py:387-395`, `paper_arms.py:557-561`, `parameter_optimization.py:250`, `scaling.py:122-386` and `scaling.py:833-878`, and the floor code at `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:934-978`. The working-tree diff changes `block_norm_covariance: str = "passthrough"` to `"pushforward"`, but no pin row was added, although the table's own comment at `config.py:3032` says "Add a row whenever a default flips"; the same WIP did add rows for `decode_logits_amp` and `decode_ce_triton`. The field was introduced at 89eedfb (2026-10-10) with default `"passthrough"` and a commit message calling pushforward opt-in, so any config serialized before then that lacks the key reloads with today's default. A scan over all 99 commits of `vfe3/config.py` found this is the only field introduced after 2026-09-23 whose default changed without a pin. The experiments investigator reports further unpinned flips in the same diff: `attention_residual` True to False, `layernorm_affine` False to True, and `lambda_alpha_mode` per_coord to state_dependent. A second, related reload gap concerns the covariance floor: before 5f5d484 (2026-10-12) the moment-matched floor was the reference ridge `C_att + f*tr(C_i)/K*I` (in `git show 5f5d484^:vfe3/model/relational_operators.py`, blockwise lines 1187-1189, dense line 1283, minimal agent line 940), `relative_floor_term` reproduces it exactly in exact arithmetic, and docs/2026-10-16.md:5 says `'relative'` "restores the floor that ran before then", yet 322843b (2026-10-16) added `predictive_covariance_floor_mode` with default `'prior'` and no migration. The evidence was executed. A scratch probe serializing a tiny layernorm config with `'passthrough'`, dropping the key and reloading through `config_from_serialized` gives `'pushforward'`. Of 7 `vfe3_runs/*/config.json`, exactly one lacks the field: the 36.41 run (timestamp 2026-10-09, trained at 7bfdf27, an ancestor of 89eedfb, with `norm_type_block='layernorm'`, gaussian_diagonal, moment_matched). `config_from_serialized`, `lenient_config_from_serialized` and `run_artifacts._config_from_run_serialized` all return `block_norm_covariance='pushforward'`, the field is not in `inactive_fields()`, and `pa.load_base('k128_alibi', ...)` lists it only under `defaulted`, with `pinned` equal to `{'relational_receiver_measurement': 'adjoint', 'decode_ce_triton': False}`; the same load gives `predictive_covariance_floor_mode='prior'`, also under `defaulted`. Override probes show both fields are live on this route: `block_mlp_covariance_floor`, read only by the pushforward, changes parameters after 3 steps (maxdiff 3.3e-3), and relative against prior gives a parameter maxdiff of 7.8e-5 after 3 tiny steps. A tiny replay of the 36.41 config (V=13, K=8, H=2, L=2, N=8, identical weights) gives CE 2.44436 under pushforward against 2.44654 under passthrough, and a second tiny CPU model (K=8, L=2, V=7, N=4) loads its state dict strictly (`<All keys matched successfully>`) while the loss moves from 1.945386 (passthrough and relative, as run) to 1.945857 (as reloaded) on the same weights and batch, even at initialization. Pushforward adds no parameters, so `best_model.pt` loads with no error, and the 2026-10-15 log showed passthrough and pushforward diverging in opposite directions (inflation against collapse). The reuse and homogeneity checks are blind: in the reuse probe the reloaded base record and the plan both serialize `block_norm_covariance=pushforward, floor_mode=prior`, so apart from `checkpoint_interval` no field differs, and `reuse_mismatch` compares two migrated configs, so a reused (6,3) replicate would pass beside retrained pushforward replicates. Likewise `scaling.py:_cell_resume_status` would accept a pre-10-10 passthrough cell as equal to a current pushforward cell. The dry plan resolves two arms as invalid (`block_norm_covariance='pushforward' needs block_coordinates='local'` for the reference-coordinate LayerNorm and MLP arm, and `needs norm_type_block='layernorm' (got 'mahalanobis')` for the intertwiner-constrained arm); with a passthrough pin monkeypatched in, both resolve `ready` with only their declared diffs. A pre-2026-10-10 config with `norm_type_block` set to `'none'` or `'mahalanobis'` now fails to load with that same error, and `parameter_optimization.generate_candidates(SEARCH_CONFIG)` raises `ValueError` because its config sets `norm_type_block='none'` at `:250` and never sets `block_norm_covariance`; patched in memory, its `attention_residual` resolves to False where HEAD gave True. The scaling width cell K20 also resolves to `'pushforward'`, since `scaling.py` never sets the field.

This is reachable on every reload of a config saved before 89eedfb through `config_from_serialized` or `lenient_config_from_serialized`: resume, eval, posthoc, `viz/run_loading`, semantics, `vfe3.paper` and multiseed, and the active `paper_arms.py` CONFIG with `bases=['k128_alibi']` and `dry_run=False` whenever a VFE arm is selected (TIER_B, TIER_AB, REST or None). Because `run_plan` refuses any launch with an invalid selected arm (`paper_arms.py:1541-1544`), a REST or None launch stops, and the paper_arms docstring (lines 10-13) promises that "the arms never inherit a default flip". The floor gap reaches any moment-matched config saved before 5f5d484, again including the 36.41 base; docs/2026-10-15.md:31 replays that run on current code while noting that the kernel "differs from the archived source only in its floor", a difference that is now selectable but not selected on reload. All investigators propose adding `"block_norm_covariance": "passthrough"` to `_HISTORICAL_DEFAULT_PINS` with a comment row naming 89eedfb (2026-10-10). The experiments investigator would also pin `attention_residual=True`, `layernorm_affine=False` and `lambda_alpha_mode='state_dependent_per_coord'` at their introduction-time values and set these keys explicitly in `parameter_optimization.config` and `scaling.config`, and gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed would have the two reference-coordinate arms declare `block_norm_covariance='passthrough'` or report themselves inapplicable on a pushforward base. For the floor, a plain key-absence pin is wrong because configs saved from 2026-10-12 to 2026-10-16 also lack the key and ran `'prior'`, and the investigators differ on the era marker: recent-diff and gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed would set `'relative'` when the payload lacks both `predictive_covariance_floor_mode` and `phi_frame_condition_max` (introduced at 5f5d484), while config-wiring would key on any pre-2026-10-12 marker from `_PROJECTED_FACTORIZATION_FIELDS`, `_ROPE_FIELDS` or `_RUNTIME_POLICY_FIELDS` (the 36.41 payload carries `projected_cov_condition_recovery`, `projected_factor_route`, `pos_rotation`, `rope_space`, `train_window_offset`, `fill_uninitialized_memory` and `relational_receiver_law`, all retired or merged on 2026-10-12), and recent-diff asks paper_arms to list the result under `pinned`. The finding is falsified if `config_from_serialized` on `vfe3_runs/36.41_wikitext-103_K128_alibi-exp-chunk/config.json['config']` returns `'passthrough'` or the payload carries the key, if the code at 7bfdf27 already pushed Sigma through the block LayerNorm, or if `parameter_optimization.generate_candidates` completes on the shipped `SEARCH_CONFIG`; the floor part is falsified if a pre-5f5d484 checkpoint evaluated under `'prior'` reproduces its logged validation CE as closely as under `'relative'`, if runs saved before 2026-10-12 computed the `f*Sigma_p` floor, or if the user decides archived runs should reload with the current floor. The pushforward flip was rated high by all four investigators; recent-diff and config-wiring each rated the floor-mode reload gap medium as a separate finding. Reported by recent-diff, config-wiring, experiments and gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; the `block_norm_covariance` flip is new (docs/audit-results_2026-10-15-instability.md:36 and its finding 8 covered only the `decode_logits_amp`, `decode_ce_triton` and `routing_kl_max_reference` flips and the paper_arms `attention_residual` fallback), the `layernorm_affine` and `lambda_alpha_mode` pins and the parameter_optimization crash are new, and the floor difference was noted for the 36.41 replay at docs/2026-10-15.md:31 (the replacement itself is in docs/2026-10-12.md:21) without the migration being addressed.

### 2. Full-family LayerNorm pushforward collapses covariance to near rank one and is fp32-singular: first-forward failures in ~10% of accepted tiny configs, kl_max saturation with zero score-map gradient on projected_gradient (high)

The defect is in `vfe3/model/block_mlp.py:543-620` (`layernorm_full_pushforward`) and `vfe3/model/block_mlp.py:469-504` (the trace cap), with failures surfacing at `vfe3/relational_scoring.py:845`, `vfe3/gradients/relational.py:253` and `vfe3/families/base.py:61-62`, and the configuration path through `vfe3/config.py:1776-1789` and `vfe3/gauge_covariant.py:33-87`. Since `P = I - WW^T` annihilates span{1, z}, only the `f*D*Sigma*D` floor fills that span, and the capped pushforward is dominated by the radial term `rho*g*g^T`, so the output covariance is near rank one. All evidence was executed. Random sweeps over accepted tiny live-derived configs (K=8, heads of 4, L=2) produced 26 failures among 3,137 valid configs and 23 among 2,007, all with `family='gaussian_full'`, `norm_type_block='layernorm'` and `block_norm_covariance='pushforward'`; with full family and LN fixed and other toggles random, pushforward failed 100 of 999 (10.0%) and passthrough 0 of 1174, mostly with "projected covariance is not positive definite: zero-jitter Cholesky failed". In float64, the pushforward of random SPD Sigma (scale 5) has min over median eigenvalue of 1.2e-4 to 1.3e-4 at K=8 and K=160, which is the 1e-4 floor alone; at the live `mu_init_std=0.03` the median condition number is 6e7 at K=8 and 6.4e7 at K=160 (6e4 at mu_std 1), so `eps_32*cond` is about 4. The layer-1 input covariance measured in float32 (eigenvalues in float64, `probe_full_layers.py`) ranges from -1.15e-8 to 8.0 (cond 4e7, indefinite as stored) on the live dict with gaussian_full at K=8, from -1.11e-8 to 8.6 (cond 4.75e7) under the pure-path overrides at K=8, and from 1.1e-6 to 166.6 (about the whole trace in one direction, median cond 8.9e7, `u32*cond` about 5) under the pure-path overrides at K=160, H=5, against 4.08 to 5.95 (cond 1.2) with passthrough. A directional central-FD sweep in float64 (`fd_variants.py`) with `attention_mode='projected_gradient'`, `relational_receiver_update='closed_form'`, `rope_mode='none'`, `family='gaussian_full'` and `decode_mode='full_chunked'` gives an all-zero `.grad` on `relational_operators_extra.0.raw_score_q` and `.raw_score_k` (the layer-1 router), also at `n_layers=3` and with euler or adjoint; `kl_max=inf`, `routing_kl_max_reference='row_min'` or passthrough each restore a nonzero gradient, so every layer-1 pair cost exceeds `kl_max = 8K = 64` and `safe_kl_clamp` (`kl.clamp(min=0.0, max=kl_max)`) routes those rows uniformly with zero gradient. At launcher width (K=160, H=5, L=4, N=16), the pure path passed 20 of 20 first forwards and trained 12 steps with finite, decreasing loss, so the first-forward failure is not shown at that scale.

The defect is reachable on the pure path, since `fully_gauge_covariant=True` on the live dict switches to gaussian_full and `block_coordinates='reference'` while keeping `block_norm_covariance='pushforward'`; on `family='gaussian_full'` with the live dict; on projected_gradient with gaussian_full and local coordinates; and in the single-digit-dimension CPU tests the project mandates. The proposed fix is to floor the annihilated span{1, z} relative to the pushed spectrum in `layernorm_full_pushforward` (for example adding `f*tr(P Sigma P)/K` on `W W^T`) instead of relying on `f*D*Sigma*D`, which leaves the condition number near `1/f` times the radial inflation, and until then to have `fully_gauge_covariant_overrides` refuse or warn on pushforward with the full family at a capped initialization. It is falsified by a randomized sweep showing equal first-forward failure rates for pushforward and passthrough, a pushforward output whose smallest eigenvalue is not set by the 1e-4 floor, a pure-path layer-1 input covariance with cond below 1e4 at the launcher's `sigma_init` and `mu_init_std`, or a nonzero layer-1 `raw_score` gradient on that projected_gradient configuration at absolute `kl_max = 8K`. The investigators disagree on severity: config-wiring rated it medium and gradients-tests high. Reported by config-wiring and gradients-tests; the symptom is recorded without diagnosis at docs/2026-10-13.md:7 ("pushforward was tried on the same seed and failed at step 1", at K=128 at both precisions), and the diagnosis is new.

### 3. Capped LayerNorm covariance pushforward has the wrong shape at the launcher operating point, and the covariance channel through the norm is nearly gradient-frozen (high)

The defect is at `vfe3/model/block_mlp.py:276-312`, `:367-386`, `:430-447` and `:469-504`, with call sites at `vfe3/model/block.py:206` and `vfe3/model/block.py:224`. The diagonal pushforward is `d2 * (bracket + covariance_floor * sigma) + rho * g2` with `g2 = weight.square() * z2` (`:386`), then scaled by `lam = torch.clamp(bound / trace, max=1.0)` (`:499`) with `bound = K * weight.square().amax()`. When `Sigma/s^2 >> 1` the radial term `rho*(gamma o z)^2`, of order `(Sigma/s^2)^2`, dominates the first-order term, and the cap fixes the trace at K while keeping that shape, so per-coordinate variance becomes proportional to `z_k^2`. Executed CPU probes with float64 Monte Carlo of 1e5 to 2e5 samples show the following. Standalone at K=160, mu std 0.03 and `Sigma=5I`, with cap lambda 9.3e-6, the traces agree (MC 159.97, pushforward 160), but the radial direction carries 94.7% of the pushforward trace against 0.6% in the MC, and the diagonal variances are 0.053 to 7.81 against MC 0.992 to 1.008, with correlation 1.000 to `z^2`. In the live model at K=160, H=5, L=4, N=8, spying on `norm_covariance_pushforward` inputs, layer 0 (`Sigma/s^2` of 1.3e4 to 2.3e4) gives pushforward variances 0.013 to 10.3 against MC 0.84 to 1.21, correlations +0.08, +0.03 and -0.13, and trace ratio 1.000; layers 1 to 3 (`Sigma/s^2` of 1.0 to 1.9) give correlation 1.000 but a pushforward trace 1.52x to 1.97x the MC trace. `layernorm_trace_cap_elements()` reports 54 of 64 token-layer norm calls capped at init (K=8: 20 of 32; K=40 with one layer: 16 of 16). The output changes by 2.97e-2 per unit relative change in input variance in the capped regime against 0.986 in the valid regime (K=160, mu std 1, var 0.01), so `sigma_log_embed` and the layer-0 moment-matched `C_att` reach downstream layers about 33x more weakly; in the valid regime the pushforward matches MC (trace 0.403 against 0.398). The capped covariance feeds the next router's KL, the moment-matched update and the belief decoder (`decode_covariance='belief'`).

This is reachable on the active config (`block_norm_covariance='pushforward'`, `norm_type_block='layernorm'`, gaussian_diagonal, `sigma_init=5`, `mu_init_std=0.03`), measured at initialization only; whether it persists during training was not measured. The proposed fix is to replace the rescaled delta-method shape, where the cap binds or `Sigma/s^2` is not small, with a moment estimate valid off the small-noise regime, such as an O(K)-point unscented transform of the LN map or the large-variance sphere limit `diag(gamma^2)(1 - 1/K)` for the diagonal family, and to add an MC regression test at `Sigma/s^2 >= 1e3`. It is falsified by an MC of `LN(u)`, `u ~ N(mu, diag sigma)`, at the captured layer-0 inputs whose per-coordinate variances correlate with the pushforward above 0.9, or by a short training run in which `layernorm_trace_cap_elements` stays near zero after the first few hundred steps. Reported by gradients-tests; new, extending docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12.md item 1, whose trace-cap remediation fixed the trace but not the shape (docs/audit-results_2026-10-15-instability.md finding 2 covers magnitude collapse, not shape).

### 4. Spike attribution and parameter-motion checks are blind to parameters whose optimizer clears .grad in step() (mu_embed, sigma_log_embed on the fisher route; phi under pullback/fisher_group) (high)

The defect spans `vfe3/table_optim.py:193`, `vfe3/gauge_optim.py:1744-1752`, and the readers at `vfe3/train.py:3928`, `vfe3/train.py:3941-3946`, `vfe3/train.py:4791-4797`, `vfe3/train.py:4815-4818` and `vfe3/train.py:5248-5251`. `_TableFisherMixin._table_fisher_step` ends each table update with `p.grad = None`, and `GaugeManifoldAdamW` does the same for every pullback_group phi parameter, but two `train()` readers run after the step and assume the gradients survive: `_spike_attribution`, documented as reading "the live gradients after the step", skips `if parameter.grad is None or role is None: continue`, and `motion_known_dead` records every `p.grad is None` after the first accepted step. Detection reads `role_grad_norms` computed before the step, so detection includes these tables and attribution does not; the shares are renormalized over surviving parameters, so they still sum to 1 and the record looks complete. Both investigators executed probes. On a tiny config from `tests/test_audit2_20261011_training._cfg` (diagonal_chunked, `untie_decode_bank=True`), adamw gives role mu detected and attributed 1.05153 with `mu_embed` among the groups, fisher gives detected 1.05153 and attributed 0.76757 with groups relational_score, relational_content, decode, norm and block_mlp and `mu_embed.grad` None, and fisher plus pullback_group gives role phi detected 0.008595, attributed None, groups `[]`. On `tests.profiles.live_config()`, status role norms were `{'mu': 1.8932, 'sigma': 2.5677, 'phi': 0.0598}` while attribution reported mu 1.7689 with shares `{'decode': 0.996, ...}` and sigma `{'decode': 1.0}`, omitting `mu_embed` and `sigma_log_embed` from shares, top parameters and table rows; the post-step grad-None list was `['prior_bank.mu_embed', 'prior_bank.sigma_log_embed']`, so both live tables are written to `parameter_motion.json` as known dead, which moves a frozen Fisher-stepped table from the warned `frozen` list to the silent `frozen_known_dead` list. Forced spike rows take `grad_norm_group_*` from `report["group_norms"]` (`train.py:5248`), so `grad_norm_group_mu_embed` and `grad_norm_group_sigma_log_embed` are NaN there and a "10x its last logged norm" check can never fire for them, although the 2026-10-15 instability audit named count-1 rows of `mu_embed`, `sigma_log_embed` and `phi_embed` as the amplifier. `tests/test_divergence_spike_attribution_20261015.py` only runs the adamw table route. No training value changes; the spike records and motion report misattribute.

This is reachable on the active config, where `m_table_update_mode='fisher'` (`train_vfe3.py:278`) builds `TableFisherAdamW`, and on any `m_phi_update_mode='pullback_group'` or `'fisher_group'` run. recent-diff proposes keeping references (no copy, no host sync) to the `.grad` tensors of the table_fisher and pullback_group parameters in `status_out` before `optimizer.step()` and reading them in `_spike_attribution` and the `motion_known_dead` capture; training proposes recording per-parameter pre-clip norms and table-row squared norms before `_scaler.step`, or having the table and manifold steps stash the consumed gradient. It is falsified if, with `m_table_update_mode='fisher'`, `report['roles']['mu']['pre_clip_norm']` matches `status_out['role_grad_norms']['mu']` to 1e-5 and the attribution lists `prior_bank.mu_embed` and `sigma_log_embed` in the group shares. Reported by recent-diff and training; new (docs/2026-10-15.md:53 records the symptom "the decode table topped every sigma spike" but attributes it to missing decode-row reporting).

## Medium-severity findings

### 5. New 'pushforward' default is refused under every non-LayerNorm norm while inactive_fields calls it inert; parameter_optimization.py and two paper arms no longer build (medium)

The defect sits at `vfe3/config.py:1776-1789`, `vfe3/config.py:2270-2273`, `parameter_optimization.py:250`, `parameter_optimization.py:506` and `paper_arms.py:386-394`. The validator refuses `block_norm_covariance == "pushforward"` whenever `norm_type_block != "layernorm"` (`problems.append(...)` and then a raise, lines 1777-1789), while `inactive_fields` marks the same state inert with the note that the field is "only read by a 'layernorm' block norm" (lines 2270-2273). Under the old `'passthrough'` default the refusal fired only when a user asked for pushforward; under the working-tree default it fires on configs that never name the field. Executed probes showed that `VFE3Config(norm_type_block='none')` raises `ValueError`, as do `'mahalanobis'` and `block_coordinates='reference'` on the diagonal family. `parameter_optimization.config` (with `norm_type_block='none'` and no `block_norm_covariance` key) raises in `VFE3Config(**config)` at `parameter_optimization.py:506`. `paper_arms.rebuild` on the k128_alibi base fails for the arms "LayerNorm and MLP in reference coordinates" and "Intertwiner-constrained nonlinear block" (mahalanobis), and both build once the base carries the HEAD default `passthrough`. A `tiny_config` (norm `'none'`, test baseline `passthrough`) also emits a `ConfigNotice` saying `block_norm_covariance='passthrough'` is inert and that "changing them will have no effect", although the only other value raises. Separately, `scaling.py`'s `BASELINE` (layernorm, no `block_norm_covariance` key) silently switches from passthrough to pushforward between HEAD and the working tree.

It is reachable from any config that sets `norm_type_block` to `'none'` or `'mahalanobis'`, or sets `block_coordinates='reference'` on `gaussian_diagonal`, without spelling out `block_norm_covariance`; this covers `parameter_optimization.py`'s config dict and `paper_arms` arms built from the k128_alibi base (36.41) or from the live launcher base. The proposed fix is to mark pushforward inert under a non-`'layernorm'` norm instead of raising (keeping the reference-coordinate refusal on `gaussian_diagonal`), or to resolve a `None` default to `'pushforward'` only when `norm_type_block='layernorm'`. The finding would be retracted if `VFE3Config(norm_type_block='none')` constructs without error on the working tree, or if `parameter_optimization.config` builds. Reported by config-wiring; new.

### 6. Strict pullback closure certificate falsely rejects valid so_n towers with N >= 4 tensor-power irreps, crashing pullback_group at the first M-step (medium)

The defect is at `vfe3/geometry/phi_preconditioner.py:304-311` and `vfe3/geometry/irreps.py:237-250`. `_strict_structure_constants` computes `relative = residual / (bracket_norm + torch.finfo(torch.float64).eps)` and raises if `relative.max() > closure_tol` (1e-4). Brackets that vanish in exact arithmetic, such as those of commuting so(N) generators like `L_01` and `L_23`, carry about 4e-16 of rounding in the Gram-Schmidt tensor-irrep basis, and that noise has no component in the span, so the ratio is O(1). Executed probes: so_n N=4 `[('l2',1)]` raises with residual 6.432e-01 (6 noisy zero brackets, max norm 4.0e-16), N=5 `[('l2',1)]` gives 6.432e-01, and N=4 `[('l3',1)]` gives 9.102e-01, although the bases are closed (build-time homomorphism residual at most 6e-15). A full tiny `train_step` (`tiny_config`, K=13, `n_heads=2`, so_n N=4 `[('l1',1),('l2',1)]`, `pullback_group`) raises "strict phi group direction requires a bracket-closed generator span; relative reconstruction residual=1.000e+00" under both `tying='shared'` and `'per_head'`. Controls that pass are SO(3) l1+l2 (closed-form ladder, exact zeros), SO(5) l1+w2 and SO(6) l1+w3 (integer wedge recipe), the block-local groups, and shared `block_glk`.

It is reachable with `m_phi_update_mode='pullback_group'`, `gauge_group='so_n'`, `group_n >= 4` and any `'l<p>'` block with p >= 2 (explicit spec, or `irrep_spec='random'` when it draws one), under either `gauge_tying`; the config accepts it because `_pullback_structure_problem` passes. It is not the active config (adamw, `block_glk`). The proposed fix is to measure closure on the bilinear scale, `relative = residual / (||G_a||_F ||G_b||_F)`, or to floor the denominator at a `closure_tol`-scaled `||G_a||_F ||G_b||_F`, so a zero bracket's rounding no longer reads as an out-of-span component. It would be falsified by a `pullback_group` train_step on so_n `group_n=4`, `irrep_spec [('l1',1),('l2',1)]`, K=13 that completes and moves `phi_embed` on the current tree, or by `_strict_structure_constants(get_group('so_n')(9, group_n=4, irrep_spec=[('l2',1)], dtype=torch.float64).generators)` returning without raising. Reported by gauge-groups; new.

### 7. Moment-matched diagonal sandwich diag(U C_att U^T) stores C_att in fp32, producing positive-but-wrong variances the sign-only fp64 escalation misses (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:1428-1429`, `:1481`, `:1259` and `:1286-1290`. The diagonal readout forms the variances as `((U @ C) * U).sum(-1)` from a `C_att` stored in fp32, and the error that results comes from that storage, not from the arithmetic of the sandwich. An executed synthetic test (torch 2.11.0+cpu) used d=32, n=4000 frames with prescribed kappa(U), true local variances log-uniform over the live belief range [4e-4, 340], and `C = U^-1 diag(sigma) U^-T` stored once in fp32. At kappa(U)=50, the live `vertex_cond_max` of 50.7, the median relative error in the smallest decade was 1.6e-2 and 0.01% of entries were positive but more than 50% wrong. At kappa(U)=200, 0.67% were nonpositive (0.21% still nonpositive after adding `f Sigma_p = 5e-4`) and 2.6% were positive but more than 50% wrong. At kappa(U)=1000, 7.5% were nonpositive and 12% positive and wrong. Evaluating only the final sandwich in float64 from the same fp32-stored C still leaves relative errors of 8.1e-3, 7.7e-2 and 1.23 at kappa 50, 200 and 1000, so a precision island around the sandwich alone cannot fix it. The existing escalation recomputes `C_att` itself in float64, which is the right kind of remedy, but its trigger is `sigma_plus > 0` only. The live run logged `covariance_escalation_elements = 1920` by step 786, so the fp32 error reaches the floor scale in practice.

This is on the active config (`gaussian_diagonal` on `block_glk` block frames, `moment_matched` kernel readout, `predictive_covariance_fp64_escalation=True`, floor_mode `'prior'`). The proposed fix is to base the escalation on an error bound that includes `C_att`'s storage rounding, for example `u * (|U||C_att||U|^T)_kk` against the floored variance, or to keep `C_att` and its sandwich in float64 for the d x d head blocks; promoting only the final product does not help. It would be falsified by a probe at the live kappa(U) <= 50 in which positive-but-wrong variances do not occur once `C_att` is accumulated in fp32 by the real blockwise update, or by a fix that promotes only the sandwich and removes the error. Reported by numerics; previously reported as `docs/audit-results_2026-10-15-instability.md` finding 5 (open; the error-bound escalation was backed out per `docs/2026-10-15.md:51`), with the storage-versus-compute split and the kappa(U) crossover new here.

### 8. Minimal-agent fp32 posterior covariance is computed by cancellation and never screened; fp64 recompute fires only on factorization failure (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:1059-1086`, `vfe3/model/minimal_agent.py:1512-1516`, `vfe3/model/minimal_agent.py:1527-1580` and `vfe3/config.py:2171-2173`. `minimal_agent_update` reads only the readout status, `failed, ill_conditioned = host_read(status)` (`relational_operators.py:1059`), and recomputes in float64 only when those flags are set (`:1062`). The status holds the zero-jitter factorization flags of `A^h`, `W_ij`, `B_j`, `Lambda` and `E` (`minimal_agent.py:1512-1514`). The returned local covariance `sigma - downdate + floor`, with `downdate = sum_h rowquad(U K^h, M^h)` (`minimal_agent.py:1571-1575`), is a float32 Kalman downdate that nothing factors or sign-checks before the ceiling (`:1085`). `config.py:2172-2173` still marks `predictive_covariance_fp64_escalation` inert under the minimal agent because "the minimal agent escalates its own failed readout". An executed probe called `minimal_agent_readout` and `local_posterior_covariance` directly in float32 and float64 from identical float64-built inputs with floor 0, with N=8, H=2, d=2, K=4, block frames with kappa(U) in [10, 1e4], channel noise 1e-3 and a causal prior, over 200 instances and both inference modes, skipping every case with `failed=True`. With per-coordinate local variances `exp(4 z)`, z ~ N(0,1), float32 gave 16 of 12160 variances <= 0 and 20 positive variances wrong by more than 50 percent; one example is float32 -124.9 against float64 1.41 at sigma 9.1e5 and kappa = 80, another is -144.9 against 0.041 at kappa = 260. Several failures occur at kappa < 1700, where the launcher's `frame_pair_escalation_tol = 1e-4` does not move the frame to float64. With `exp(2 z)` variances there were no sign flips, but the worst relative error was still 0.31. The prototype agents use a cancellation-free form for the same mixture covariance (`C Psi + C R C`, `prototype_agents.py:26-30, 444-446`); the minimal agent does not.

It is reachable with `predictive_readout='minimal_agent'` under `attention_mode='projected_predictive'` with `predictive_covariance='moment_matched'` or `'spread_corrected'` and float32 beliefs; it is inactive on the launcher, which uses `predictive_readout='kernel'`. The proposed fix is to fold a check of the floored float32 output (diagonal: finite and positive; full or block: `cholesky_ex`) into the readout status so the existing whole-call float64 recompute fires, and to correct the inert rule at `config.py:2172`, or alternatively to assemble `C^+` in a Joseph or information form as `prototype_agents` does. It would be falsified by the same probe routed through `RelationalOperators.minimal_agent_update` returning only positive variances or triggering the float64 recompute, or by finding a cancellation-free covariance assembly on this path. Reported by gap-opt-in-readout-and-link-routes; new for the minimal-agent route, while the kernel-route analog is `docs/audit-results_2026-10-15-instability.md` and `docs/2026-10-15.md:9`, which exempts the minimal agent on the assumption that its own escalation covers the output.

### 9. projected_factorization='cholesky_qr2_certified' disables the closed-form diagonal comparison route on the live config, forcing the dense r x r Gram route plus a certificate (and extra host sync) that does no work on a diagonal P(G) (medium)

The defect is at `vfe3/gradients/relational.py:17-28` and `:134`, `vfe3/relational_scoring.py:1037-1041`, `:557-564` and `:853-872`, `vfe3/model/model.py:264-266`, `vfe3/config.py:440-443` and `:869-873`, `vfe3/config.py:2145-2152`, and `train_vfe3.py:333`. `_diagonal_comparison_route` returns `rope_commutant_is_diagonal(r, n_pairs) and operators.routing == 'gaussian_kl' and operators.pair_kl_gemm_tol is None and not projected_condition_recovery()`, and `model.py:264` sets recovery to `cfg.projected_factorization != 'gram'`. On the live dict (`rope_mode='comparison_invariant'`, `rope_fraction=1`, r = d_head = 32, so n_pairs=16 and P(G) is structurally diagonal) the last clause is False, and because `commutant_pairs` is set the factor route is not taken either (`factored=False` at `relational_scoring.py:1037`). Every layer therefore scores an exactly diagonal P(G) through the dense path: a per-token r x r Cholesky, the kappa_F certificate (`cholesky_inverse` plus norms), an r^2+r = 1056-wide pair GEMM in place of 2r = 64, a mandatory `host_read` where the diagonal route's `host_check` is deferred, and, for any token whose diagonal P(G) has kappa_F > 838.9, a shifted-CholeskyQR3 rebuild with per-pair triangular-solve tiles. The investigators' shared argument is that the certificate eps||G||_F||G^-1||_F <= 1e-4 is a backward-error proxy for dense Cholesky, whereas Cholesky and the inverse of a diagonal matrix are componentwise exact to O(u) whatever the condition number and diag(XX^T) is a sum of squares with relative error of order r eps, so the rebuild cannot improve the score. The numerics investigator adds that the rebuild is not in float64 on this path either (`float64_rebuild = factored and ...` is False, and `_commutant_root_factor(shift=True)` with m == r returns the square root of the plane mean of squared root rows, the same sum of squares the Gram diagonal already holds), so the gate docstring's reason, "whose float64 rescoring it would bypass", is false for a diagonal commutant.

All evidence was executed on CPU. A route probe on `live_config` (K=8, H=2) counted `_projected_diagonal_kl: 0, _GramPrecision.apply: 2` under `cholesky_qr2_certified` against `diag: 2, gram: 0` under `'gram'`, while the model's own warning in the same run says P(G) is diagonal; a separate tiny live-route model (`probe_syncs.py`) likewise called `_projected_kl_gemm` twice and `_projected_diagonal_kl` 0 times. On the live tiny route (K=32, H=2, N=16, B=4, L=2, 3 training steps) `'gram'` made 16 diagonal calls at 21.6/22.8 ms per step, `'gram_certified'` 16 dense calls at 29.4 ms, and `'cholesky_qr2_certified'` 16 dense calls at 27.2/28.1 ms; the config-wiring investigator found `gram_certified` bitwise identical to the live `cholesky_qr2_certified` over 3 train steps and `'gram'` differing by 2.8e-5, so the "cholesky_qr2" part of the setting is inert here. Timing of `projected_gaussian_kl` forward plus backward at the live head shape (B=1, N=128, H=5, r=32, fp32) gave 0.031-0.033 s for the dense route with recovery off, 0.001 s for the diagonal route, and 0.89-0.91 s for the dense route with recovery on when 0.6-1.4% of entries fail the certificate (kappa_F > 839, which a diagonal matrix reaches at a variance ratio of about 150), with max relative error against float64 of 7.4e-8 to 3.7e-7 (dense) and 1.8e-7 to 3.6e-7 (diagonal). A scorer probe at B=1, N=128, H=5, r=32, K=160 measured 108.8 ms against 38.1 ms (2.85x), 582+312 against 176+213 dispatched ops, and, extrapolated to B=32, 13.4 GFLOP per layer forward for G = X X^T and 5.5 GFLOP against 0.33 for the pair GEMM; outputs differ by 4.9e-7 relative in score and 2.8e-7 in grad mu and sit equally close to float64 (7.2e-7 and 8.5e-7). A tiny probe (B=1, N=8, H=2, K=8, r=4, variances from e^-6.9 to e^6.9) gave off-diagonal max 0.0, kappa_F median 46.8 and max 2640, 8 of 32 entries recovered, and max relative error 1.65e-05 (dense+recovery) against 9.30e-06 (diagonal) at 0.0020 s against 0.0002 s. With anisotropic comparison maps (row scales logspace(0,2), B=4, N=32, H=2, d=8), recovery rebuilt 503 of 512 entries per call with max relative error 1.08e-5 against 1.10e-5 without recovery, at 8.3 ms against 1.4 ms. The live run `vfe3_runs/20261016-201802_.../metrics.csv` shows `source_recovery_elements = 1` at step 2400, and the 2026-10-15 instability bisection counted 883k factorization recoveries in the group containing the certified factorization. Documentation drift: `config.py:442` says the comparison is scored "in closed form with no factorization when P(G) is diagonal", true only under `'gram'`; `docs/2026-10-11.md:48` still describes the diagonal route as the active-config route (it measured 0.072 s against 0.130 s); `config.py:869-870` records the route switch for `gram_certified` only; the `cholesky_qr2_certified` text at `config.py:871-873` says comparison_invariant "pass[es] no source root", but `relational.py:196-197` does pass `R_iS` source tuples and the factor route is skipped because `commutant_pairs` is set; and `inactive_fields` marks `projected_factorization` only under `mean_distance`. GPU cost was not measured.

The proposed fixes differ in scope. Numerics, info-geometry and attention propose dropping `not projected_condition_recovery()` from `_diagonal_comparison_route` whenever `rope_commutant_is_diagonal` holds (keeping the routing and gemm-tol conditions), on the ground that the diagonal route's finite-and-positive variance check already covers what factorization and certificate check, or skipping the kappa_F screen for structurally diagonal inputs, and correcting the config comments. Performance proposes letting the diagonal route run under recovery with an exact diagonal certificate (kappa = max_k g_k / min_k g_k per entry) and an O(r) direct-difference diagonal tile for flagged entries, gated behind a toggle because float32 rounding changes by about 5e-7. Config-wiring proposes only that `inactive_fields` or a `ConfigNotice` state that `cholesky_qr2_certified` equals `gram_certified` on this route and both bypass the diagonal scorer. The finding would be falsified by a float32 case, or a float64 comparison on trained comparison maps and frames, in which the dense certified route on a diagonal P(G) scores a pair measurably closer to float64 than the diagonal route (beyond about 1e-6 relative), by a route trace showing CholeskyQR2 executing or `_projected_diagonal_kl` being called on the live dict, or by a 5090 profile or CUDA-event timing at B=32, N=128, H=5, r=32 showing no cost difference between the routes. Reported by numerics, info-geometry, attention, config-wiring and performance; severities differ (medium from numerics, info-geometry, attention and performance; info from config-wiring, which treats it as an inert-mark gap). Prior record: config-wiring and info-geometry note that the `gram_certified` exclusion is documented at `config.py:865-873` and the `relational.py:17` docstring (audit 2026-10-10 round 2, item 30), and `docs/2026-10-12.md:45` notes that `cholesky_qr2_certified` "runs as gram_certified without a notice" under rotation_invariant; the disabled diagonal route on the live `cholesky_qr2_certified` config, the recovery-rate, accuracy and timing evidence, and the inert-mark gap are new.

### 10. Live kl_max=1280 router clamp covers every causal entry of one layer, zeroing its router gradient, and NaN/inf costs are indistinguishable from capped ones (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/gradients/relational.py:221-253`, `vfe3/families/base.py:54-62`, `vfe3/config.py:1004-1005` and `vfe3/model/model.py:1705-1707`. The evidence was read from `vfe3_runs/20261016-201802_wikitext-103_K160_block_glk_mlp_s6/metrics.csv`. At step 1200, `relational_score_klmax_frac` is 0.0155 for the last layer while `relational_score_klmax_frac_layer_max` is 1.0 at argmax_layer 2, with `relational_score_klmax_frac_head_max` 1.0, `relational_clamped_row_frac_head_max` 1.0 and `attn_entropy_collapsed_heads` 1.0; at step 2400 `layer_max` is 1.0 at argmax_layer 1, with head_max and clamped_row_frac_head_max both 1.0. `_layer_guard_maxima` computes the fraction per layer as `((score >= cfg.kl_max) & support).sum()/support.sum()` on the captured clamped cost, so in one layer every causal pair cost is at least 1280 nats. Under the absolute reference such rows route by the ALiBi prior alone and the cost carries zero gradient (the clamp derivative is 0 above the cap), so that layer's `S_Q`, `S_K` and routing beliefs get no router signal. The same rows show `relational_projected_cov_min_eigenvalue_layer_min` of 5.7e-9 and 3.2e-8 against `relational_projected_cov_scale` of 42-76, which points to near-zero comparison variances in some tokens, but the cause was not established. `safe_kl_clamp` maps NaN and +inf to `kl_max` before capture, so the metric cannot separate a non-finite score from a saturated finite one.

This is on the active config (`routing_kl_max_reference='absolute'`, `kl_max=None`, resolving to 8K = 1280 per the run's config.json); the pure paths are `row_min` or `kl_max=inf`. The proposed fix is to count non-finite pre-clamp router costs separately from finite costs at or above `kl_max` in the capture (on-device, before `safe_kl_clamp`) and to log the per-layer capped fraction so the saturated layer can be attributed to a cause. It would be falsified by a per-layer capture at these steps showing that the layer's pre-clamp costs are below 1280 for most pairs, which would make the `layer_max` reading a metric artifact. Reported by numerics; new (`audit-results_2026-10-15-instability.md:40` found `kl_max=1280` never bound under `row_min`, and the live dict now uses absolute).

### 11. 'M-step' self-coupling term back-propagates through the converged belief q* into the readout; it is not an M-step on the priors and no toggle gives the pure M-step (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/model/model.py:1315-1356`, `vfe3/model/block.py:113-119`, `vfe3/model/stack.py:126-127,164-166` and `ablation.py:928-933`. The code takes `q_conv = cap["converged"]` (`model.py:1343`), computes `self_div = self_divergence_for_alpha(fam(q_conv.mu, q_conv.sigma), fam(mu_p, sigma_p), ...)` with `q_conv` not detached, and adds `coupling = alpha_sc.detach() * self_div` (`:1352`). The comment at `:1330-1336` justifies this by the envelope argument and says the term "backprops to the learned prior tables"; `block.py:114` calls q* "what the E-step's F was minimized over", but `free_energy.py:282-283` says no supported route minimizes F. With `prior_handoff_rho = 1` (the default), the final block's prior mean is the penultimate layer's output (`stack.py:165`), so the prior is itself a function of the readout. An executed probe on the tiny live route (`tests.profiles.live_config`, K=8, H=2, L=2, V=7) at weight 1.0 found a gradient norm of 10.63 on `prior_bank` plus 1.157 on non-prior parameters (relational O 0.874, block_norm 0.491, layer-1 O 0.430, block_mlps 0.267, and the score maps). With q* detached by hand the `prior_bank` gradient fell to 1.006, so about 90% of this "M-step" gradient on the tables arrives through q*, not through p; even then gradient still reached layer-0 `raw_score_q/k`, T, O, block_norm and fc1/fc2 through the handed-off prior mean. By contrast the prototype EM term detaches its posterior, and the same probe found its gradient only on `nu`, `log_lambda` and `log_pi` (other parameters at most 1.3e-8). Under Neal and Hinton 1998 (Sec. 2, coordinate ascent on F(q, theta)) the M-step holds q fixed, and the envelope theorem (Danskin 1967) would equate total and partial derivatives only for the full functional at its minimizer, not for the self sector alone.

It is reachable on the live route (projected_predictive kernel) with `mstep_self_coupling_weight > 0`; `ablation.py:928` ships an uncommented sweep over it (log_range 1e-4 to 2), and the default of 0 is off. The proposed fix is to detach `cap['converged']` (and the handed-off `final_block_prior` mean when rho > 0, or read the encoded prior instead) inside the term so that only prior parameters receive `alpha* dD/dp`, or else to rename it a belief-to-prior regularizer and drop the M-step wording from the code, config and sweep descriptions. It would be falsified if, with `mstep_self_coupling_weight > 0`, the gradient difference against weight 0 is zero on every `relational_operators*`, `block_norm` and `block_mlps` parameter and the `prior_bank` gradient is unchanged when q* is detached. Reported by variational; new.

### 12. Untied decode variance table has no training-time monitor: the decode_sigma_v_min gauge config.py tells the user to watch is never computed or logged (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/model/prior_bank.py:976-989`, `vfe3/train.py:2395-2419`, `vfe3/train.py:5253`, `vfe3/config.py:338-341` and `scaling.py:360-367`. `PriorBank.decode_sigma_v_min` returns `float(bounded_variance_from_log(self._decode_sigma_log_table(), eps=self.eps).min())`, and its docstring calls this "the one number that says how much headroom is left ... it has to be watched". A search for `decode_sigma_v_min` over `vfe3/`, `tests/` and the root scripts finds only the definition and two comments, `config.py:340` ("watch decode_sigma_v_min") and `scaling.py:367`; no test references it. The gap-live-decode-triton-kernels investigator traced the removal to commit 2f2772f, which deleted the only consumer (`d["decode_sigma_v_min"] = self.prior_bank.decode_sigma_v_min()` in the model diagnostics) as a dead diagnostic. The only logged variance statistic, `_sigma_log_statistics` (`train.py:2409-2419`, written at `:5253`), reads `bank.sigma_log_embed`, the encoder table, while under `untie_decode_bank=True` the decoder reads `decode_sigma_log_embed` (`prior_bank.py:997-999`). An executed probe on `tests/profiles.live_config` (K=8, H=2, L=2, N=4, V=7) set half of `decode_sigma_log_embed` to log(1e-5): `_sigma_log_statistics` stayed at `{'sigma_log_mean': 1.6094, 'sigma_log_sd': 0.0}` while `decode_sigma_v_min` fell from 5.0 to 1e-5, and at that table the fp32 and fp64 `a_v` logits differed by up to 1.35 (1.02 after per-row centering). A second executed measurement (torch 2.11.0+cpu, real `decode_ce_diagonal_chunked` against float64, clustered vocabulary V=256, tau=0.1) at sigma_v near 1, 1e-1, 1e-2 and 1e-3 gave, at K=128, fp32 `a_v` CE errors of 1.6e-5, 4.2e-5, 5.1e-4 and 3.9e-3 and relative `d_mu` gradient errors of 8.5e-5, 4.3e-4, 3.8e-3 and 3.1e-2; the fp64 option gives 8.7e-8 to 2.7e-5 CE error and 1.1e-6 to 1.3e-4 gradient error, and at K=20 the fp32 errors are 3e-7 to 3e-4, so K=128 is 5 to 50 times worse, with the gradient error crossing 1e-2 between sigma_v = 1e-2 and 1e-3. Two finished K=64 runs (`vfe3_runs/47.67_*`, `51.22_*`) have trained decode sigma_v minima of 1.61 and 0.975 (median about 4.1), where the fp32 per-row CE error is 4e-5 to 1.3e-4, so the regime has not been reached in recorded runs, but nothing would show it if a run got there.

It applies on the active config (`decode_mode='diagonal_chunked'`, `untie_decode_bank=True`, `decode_av_precision='fp32'`) and every untied prior-bank KL decode; the fp64 option is the documented opt-in and the toggle itself is not the defect. The proposed fix is to log `decode_sigma_v_min` (and the decode table's log-variance mean and SD) in the metrics row at the existing logging cadence when the decode bank is untied, as one off-graph reduction, or else to delete the "watch decode_sigma_v_min" advice from `config.py` and the docstring. It would be falsified by a metrics or summary column that already reports the minimum or statistics of `decode_sigma_log_embed` under `untie_decode_bank=True`, a production call of `decode_sigma_v_min`, or a K=128 measurement showing fp32 CE and gradient error below 1e-3 down to sigma_v = 1e-3. Reported by dead-code and gap-live-decode-triton-kernels, who disagree on severity (medium and low respectively); new (the `a_v` precision itself is audit 2026-08-06 F32 and round-2 H4, but the monitor removal is not recorded).

### 13. update_rms_per_lr_group_{mu_embed,sigma_log_embed} divides the Fisher table step by an AdamW rate the config marks inert (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/train.py:2319-2326`, `:2374-2381` and `:4718-4719`. `_parameter_learning_rates` returns `group['lr']`, the AdamW rate, but under the Fisher table M-step that rate only sets `lr_t/lr_0`, and the table moves by `eta*(lr_t/lr_0)*trust*d`. An executed one-step probe on the tiny `live_config` gave, at `m_p_mu_lr=0.025`, `||delta mu_embed||=0.0147585` and `update_rms_per_lr_group_mu_embed=7.88875`, and at `m_p_mu_lr=0.25` the same `||delta||=0.0147585` but a column value of 0.788875. In both cases `cfg.inactive_fields()` marks `m_p_mu_lr` inactive and `_table_fisher_learning_rates` reports `{'mu': 0.1, 'sigma': 0.1}`. The column, introduced by audit item 26 as an O(1) trainability measure for AdamW tables, therefore scales with an inert setting on this route. It is reached on the active config (`m_table_update_mode='fisher'`) in every persisted log and eval row. The proposed fix is to divide by the realized step size `_table_fisher_learning_rates` already computes (`eta*lr_t/lr_0`) for groups marked `table_fisher`, or to emit NaN for them; the finding would be falsified if the column is unchanged when `m_p_mu_lr` is rescaled under `m_table_update_mode='fisher'`. Reported by training; new (refines audit-2026-10-09-round2 item 26, which concerned AdamW log-scale tables).

### 14. On the chart retraction the hard-coded 1e-6 product-residual tolerance, not m_phi_group_trust_radius, bounds the pullback/fisher_group frame step (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/gauge_optim.py:63`, `:478-505` and `:553-556`, and `vfe3/config.py:759`. `stage_pullback_group_candidate` accepts a row only if `||exp(phi - eta s v) - exp(phi)exp(-eta s xi)||/||.|| <= _GROUP_PRODUCT_RESIDUAL_MAX = 1e-6` and halves s otherwise; because this is a second-order discrepancy, the test binds well before the trust radius. An executed probe (block_glk, d=8, H=2, 64 rows, trust_radius 0.1) gave, at phi_std 0.035, lr 0.003 and a large gradient, a chart median realized step of 2.28e-2 (2.02 halvings) against 9.16e-2 for group_log (0 halvings); at phi_std 0.3, lr 0.015 (the library `m_phi_lr`) and unit gradient, 8.85e-3 (1.75 halvings) against 3.10e-2; and at phi_std 0.3, lr 0.003 and a large gradient, 1.18e-2 (3.05) against 9.50e-2. At frame row norms of 3.4 to 7.9 the chart step stayed at 1.2e-2 to 2.0e-2 with up to 8 halvings. The config comment calls `m_phi_group_trust_radius` the "step bound", but on the chart route the effective bound is about 4 to 8 times smaller, depends on `||phi||`, and is set by the tolerance; it is visible only through `phi_group_trust_scale_mean`, which folds in the halvings.

It is reachable on the pure frame path, `m_phi_update_mode='pullback_group'` or `'fisher_group'` with `m_phi_group_retraction='chart'` (the default retraction and the only one for groups other than untied `block_glk`), not on the active adamw route. The proposed fix is to document that the trust radius is reachable only under group_log, or to state the chart tolerance relative to the second-order term and expose it, and to log the mean halving count as its own column. It would be falsified by the chart route realizing a step near 0.1 for the same phi, gradient and lr inputs where group_log does. Reported by training; new (audit-2026-10-08 covered off-strip rows raising, not the binding tolerance).

### 15. LR width-transfer fit discards seeded rows (multi-seed sweeps report every cell diverged), and the exponent is grid-quantized, single-seed and interval-free (medium)

The locations are `ablation.py:294-303`, `:4547`, `:4550-4606` (including `:4559` and `:4564`) and `:5497`. The label regex `^K=(\d+),H=(\d+),([A-Za-z0-9_]+)=x([0-9.eE+-]+)$` is anchored and its multiplier class cannot match the `__s<seed>` suffix that `run_sweep` appends on the multiseed path (`ablation.py:4000-4001`), so `match is None`, the row is skipped, and the unsuffixed `requested` labels then fill every cell with inf, contrary to the docstring's "Seeds of one cell are averaged". Both investigators executed this: a scratch probe with 3 widths x 3 multipliers x 2 seeds of finite PPL rows labeled `__s{s}` gave `interior_widths 0`, no exponent, and K=64 `ppl_by_multiplier {'0.5': None, '1': None, '2': None}` with all multipliers diverged, while the same rows without the suffix gave PPLs 60.5/55.5/57.5 and a fitted exponent; labels such as `'K=64,H=2,m_phi_lr=x0.5__s6'` likewise left [0.5,1,2,4] diverged at every width. The experiments investigator adds that the exponent itself is quantized: with multipliers [0.5,1,2,4] and the edge rule, the only interior best multipliers are 1 and 2, so each width's `best_lr` takes one of two values. Executed on synthetic exact optima, best `{64:2,96:2,128:1}` gives exponent -0.934 and eta(160)=0.00285, `{64:2,96:1,128:1}` gives -1.047, and all x1 gives 0.000. The exponent can thus take only a handful of values about 1 apart, yet it is extrapolated to K=160 and 256 with no uncertainty, from one seed per cell at a 1500-step cosine horizon (`max_steps` replaced at `:5497`), although `docs/2026-10-15.md` records six identical x1 baseline cells at K=80 spanning PPL 231 to 5526.

The seeded-label defect needs a `'seeds'` list on the `lr_width_transfer` SWEEPS entry, and the default single-seed path is unaffected by it; the quantization applies whenever `LR_WIDTH_TRANSFER['enabled']=True` (the 2026-10-15 instability workflow). Both investigators propose stripping the seed suffix with the existing `_base_label` before matching (`_LR_WIDTH_TRANSFER_LABEL.match(_base_label(...))`); experiments also proposes locating each width's optimum by a quadratic fit of log PPL against log multiplier through the best point and its neighbors and reporting a seed-bootstrap interval for b alongside `eta_at`. It would be falsified by `fit_lr_width_transfer` returning finite `ppl_by_multiplier` entries and an exponent for `__s<seed>` labels such as `'K=64,H=2,m_phi_lr=x1__s0'`, or by an exponent strictly between the grid-induced values on data whose true optimum lies between grid points. Reported by recent-diff and experiments, who disagree on severity (low and medium respectively, recent-diff scoring only the seed-label bug); new.

### 16. Across-seed ablation aggregates silently drop diverged seeds, so an unstable arm can rank first labelled 'single seed' (medium)

The locations are `ablation.py:4482-4499`, `:4502-4524`, `:4879-4908`, `:4996-5001` and `:5093-5095`. `_seed_aggregate` keeps only rows with `ppl < inf` (`if ppl < float("inf"): groups...append(ppl)`), and `_plot_gauge_transport` and `_plot_mu_precond` skip inf rows before `_aggregate_cells`. In an executed probe with rows `armA__s6=inf`, `armA__s23=inf`, `armA__s54=90.0` and `armB__s{6,23,54}=95,96,97`, `_seed_aggregate` ranked armA first with n=1 and mean 90.0 against armB with n=3 and mean 96.0, `_aggregate_cells` over A=[inf,inf,90] returned ppl 90.0, and `_plot_seed_aggregate` drew armA with the legend "n = 1: single seed, SD unknown" although three seeds ran. `parameter_optimization._leaderboard_rows` handles the same case correctly by marking the candidate incomplete and unranked. It is reachable from any sweep that declares `'seeds'` (new_attention_features, relational_features, parameter-grid or user sweeps) where some seed diverges or errors, including the headline gauge_transport and mu_precond figures. The proposed fix is to carry `n_requested` and `n_failed` per base label, rank or plot an arm only when every requested seed finished (or count failures as diverged, as `fit_lr_width_transfer` does), and label partial arms "k of n seeds finite". It would be falsified by a multi-seed sweep CSV in which an arm with failed seeds is reported with its failure count and not ranked above complete arms. Reported by experiments; new (audit-2026-09-23 item 7 concerned SD=0 for single-seed arms, since fixed).

### 17. generate_text GPT-2 control decodes greedily while the VFE continuation is sampled, so self-CE deltas compare different samplers (medium)

The locations are `generate_text.py:425-432`, `:509-513`, `:63-67` and `:468-477`. Read from the code: `HuggingFaceBaseline.generate` takes `logits.argmax(dim=-1, keepdim=True)` at every step, and its docstring calls this "the same protocol VFEModel.generate uses", but `main` calls `baseline.generate(ids, MAX_NEW_TOKENS)` with no sampler arguments while the VFE continuation comes from `generate_for_prompts(greedy=GREEDY, temperature=TEMPERATURE, top_k=TOP_K, top_p=TOP_P)`. Greedy text sits at the argmax and has systematically lower teacher-forced CE than top-10 sampled text, so the printed delta column of the GPT-2 row is not the same diagnostic as the VFE row. It is reached with `SELF_CE=True` and `BASELINE='gpt2'` under the shipped sampler settings (`GREEDY=False`, `TEMPERATURE=1.0`, `TOP_K=10`). The proposed fix is to give `HuggingFaceBaseline.generate` the same greedy, temperature, top_k, top_p and seed arguments and pass the module settings through; it would be falsified by a run with `GREEDY=False` in which the GPT-2 continuation differs between two `SEED` values. Reported by experiments; new.

### 18. grow_K_mup is still not muP and is unusable on the shipped scaling baseline (medium)

The locations are `scaling.py:444-465` and `scaling.py:1367-1375`. Executed on scaling's own baseline (projected_predictive), every `grow_K_mup` cell in both the `_fixed` and `_mup` arms is refused with "grow_K_mup varies e_q_mu_lr and requires a gradient belief update", and `e_q_mu_lr` is in `inactive_fields`. With `attention_mode='projected_gradient'` (default closed_form receiver) the guard admits the K40_mup cell while `e_q_mu_lr` is still inert. The live part of the arm scales only `m_p_mu_lr` by 20/K and `mu_init_std` by sqrt(20/K) (at K=100, `m_p_mu_lr` 0.001 and `mu_init_std` 0.0291), that is, the input embedding table, which muP for Adam keeps width-independent (Yang et al. 2022, Tensor Programs V, Table 3), while the K x K relational and block-MLP matrices that muP rescales keep fixed rates. It is reached when `CONFIG['routes']` contains `'grow_K_mup'` (documented route F1/EXP-6). The proposed fix is to scale `m_relational_score_lr`, `m_relational_content_lr` and `m_block_mlp_lr` by `anchor_k/K`, leave the embedding table rate and init fixed, drop `e_q_mu_lr` from the arm, and make the guard test `inactive_fields` instead of `relational_readout_mode`. It would be falsified by a `grow_K_mup` cell admitted on the shipped baseline with every scaled field live, or by a muP reference showing that input-table Adam rates scale as 1/width. Reported by experiments; previously reported as `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-10.md` item 14 (round-2 #18) and unfixed.

### 19. Base-run reuse as the evaluated (6,3) replicate is claimed by the plan but rejected by the launch, so TIER_B cannot start, and the reuse design keeps selected-run bias (medium)

The locations are `paper_arms.py:1014-1024`, `:1138`, `:1219-1227`, `:1547-1560`, `:1078` and `:1450-1466`. `build_plan` sets `evaluated.reused = [(int(base.cfg.seed), base.data_seed)]` and the note "seed pair (6, 3) is the base run itself and is reused, not retrained" on `is_complete(base.run_dir)` alone, without calling `reuse_mismatch`, and `format_plan` subtracts the reused pair from the GPU-hour total (`hours * (len(plan.seeds) - len(plan.reused))`). `base_protocol` sets `checkpoint_interval = max_steps`, and `REUSE_IGNORED_FIELDS = ("seed", "resume_from")` does not exclude it. Executed on k128_alibi, `reuse_mismatch(base run, evaluated plan)` returns "config differs from the plan in checkpoint_interval (run 90000, plan 120000)", and a replica of `run_plan`'s pre-launch check (`probe_block.py`) gives `blocked: ['Evaluated: ... (6,3): config differs from the plan in checkpoint_interval (run 90000, plan 120000)']` while `plan.reused: [(6, 3)]`, so `run_plan` raises `SystemExit`. Its advice, to move the stale run aside, would remove the base itself, which `load_base` reads, and because `compute_matched_config` waits for every evaluated pair (1450-1466) the compute-matched arm can never form its rule. The test fixtures write synthetic bases with `checkpoint_interval == max_steps` (`tests/test_paper_arms_20260928.py:53`), so they pass. If `checkpoint_interval` were simply ignored, the base run (a test-selected run on drifted code) would re-enter as one of three paired replicates and bias d_(6,3) toward the evaluated arm, and combined with the investigator's finding 1 it would also be accepted although it ran another configuration. The comment at 1453 saying the base fallback is not config-checked is stale, since `completed_run` already checks it (1222-1223).

It is reached with CONFIG arms `'TIER_B'`, `'TIER_AB'` or `None` on the `base_protocol` budget (the configured budget) for any base whose recorded `checkpoint_interval` differs from `max_steps`: 36.41 records 90000 against 120000, and 32.65 and 34.48 both record 75000. TIER_A, the current selection, is not affected. The proposed fix is to drop the evaluated-arm base-run fallback so every planned seed pair is retrained under the plan's config, removing `reused` from `build_plan` and `format_plan`; if reuse is kept, apply `reuse_mismatch` in `build_plan` and add the run-only cadence fields (`checkpoint_interval`, `log_interval`) to `REUSE_IGNORED_FIELDS`. It would be falsified by `run_plan` on k128_alibi with `arms='TIER_B'` passing its stale check without a code change, or by a real base recording `checkpoint_interval == max_steps`. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; previously reported as `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-09.md:51` and `:59` (the reused (6,3) replicate is a test-selected winner on drifted code, high), with the launch block from the 10-09 config-check remediation new.

### 20. Compute-matched baseline widens up to 8x with the base-width hidden learning rate and records no deviation (medium)

The locations are `paper_arms.py:941-947`, `:1476` and `:1500-1505`, and `vfe3/baseline.py:499-504`, `:1255` and `:1304`. Read from the code: `total_parameter_width` sets `hidden_lr_scale=base.hidden_lr_scale * base.embed_dim / chosen.embed_dim` (`baseline.py:1304`), and `_resolve_baseline` records the deviation "lr not tuned at this width" only under `options.get("total_matched")` (`paper_arms.py:941-947`). The compute-matched path builds `replace(base.with_width(chosen['width'], ...), max_steps=...)` (`baseline.py:1255`), and `with_width` changes only `embed_dim` and `n_heads` (`baseline.py:504`). The ladder runs to 8 x 128 = 1024 (`ladder_widths`, `paper_arms.py:1342-1345`), so the chosen arm can train at up to 8x width with the 3e-3 peak rate tuned at K=128 in a 6000-step sweep and with `hidden_lr_scale` 1.0, and its manifest row lists no declared deviation. In standard parameterization with Adam the effective hidden-layer update grows with width (Yang et al. 2022, Tensor Programs V, arXiv:2203.03466), so the two width-changed baselines follow inconsistent transfer rules and the compute-matched one is likely mistuned against the VFE. It is reached by the "Matched measured compute" arm (by label or via REST) once its width rule is computed. The proposed fix is to apply the same `hidden_lr_scale = s0 K0/K` in `width_ladder_rule` and the calibration configs and to append the "lr not tuned at this width" deviation to the compute-matched plan; it would be falsified by a frozen `width_rule.json` whose config has `hidden_lr_scale = 128/width`, or by a validation LR sweep at the chosen width showing 3e-3 is near optimal there. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; previously reported as `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-09.md:51` (`with_width` keeps lr, init and warmup across the up-to-8x ladder) and remediated for the total-matched arm only (`docs/2026-10-09.md:76`).

### 21. Paper sigma_schedule / S1 panel C 'weight decay alone' substitutes the logged Fisher step (lr_sigma) into the AdamW decay reference, overstating decay about 100x (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/paper/figures.py:306-369` (cross-check and substitution at `:349-366`), `vfe3/table_optim.py:181`, and `vfe3/train.py:4153-4164` and `:5045-5047`. In fisher mode the table decay is `p <- p(1 - lr_t*lambda)` with `lr_t = group['lr']`, the AdamW-scheduled `m_p_sigma_lr` (`decay = float(group["lr"]) * float(group.get("weight_decay", 0.0))` at `table_optim.py:181`), but after audit-2026-10-10-round2 item 37 train logs `lrs.update(step_table_fisher_lrs)`, so `lr_sigma` in metrics.csv is `m_table_fisher_lr * lr_t/lr_0`. `sigma_schedule` rebuilds `base * multiplier(s)` with base = `m_p_sigma_lr`, sees a mismatch of about `|m_p_sigma_lr - eta|/eta`, replaces the rates with the interpolated logged column, and feeds them to `decay_only_log_variance`. Two executed probes agree. A fake run (`m_p_sigma_lr=0.001`, eta=0.1, lambda=0.01, 200 updates, s0=log 5) reported `lr_crosscheck='mismatch: logged lr_sigma used'` with max relative error 0.99, and the curve ends at 1.4562 while the decay the optimizer applies ends at 1.6078 from s0 1.6094, so the drawn curve decays 95.3x too much. `probe_sigma_sched.py` on fisher run1 (`m_p_sigma_lr=0.0025`, `m_table_fisher_lr=0.1`, logged `lr_sigma` [0.001, 0.1, 0.075, 0.025]) reported `lr_crosscheck_max_rel_error` 1.5, `'mismatch: logged lr_sigma used'` and `lr_source` "metrics.csv lr_sigma interpolated between log rows", with rates [0.001, 0.001, 0.1, 0.075] fed to the decay reference, about 40x the decay rate actually applied.

It requires `m_table_update_mode='fisher'` (active) together with a nonzero sigma decay (`sigma_weight_decay > 0`, or `None` inheriting `weight_decay`); the active config sets `sigma_weight_decay=0`, so its curve is flat and correct. The proposed fix is, under fisher mode, to skip the `lr_sigma` cross-check or compare against `eta*multiplier` (or divide the logged value by `m_table_fisher_lr` and multiply by base) and always use `base*multiplier`, the rate `table_optim` applies decay with; viz-contracts alternatively proposes that train log the AdamW rate in a separate column such as `lr_sigma_decay`. It would be falsified if the fisher-mode curve tracks the reconstructed `m_p_sigma_lr` schedule on rerun, if a fisher-mode metrics.csv has `lr_sigma` equal to `m_p_sigma_lr` times the schedule, or if the table step applies decay at the eta-scaled rate. Reported by posthoc-paper and viz-contracts; new (a consequence of the audit-2026-10-10-round2 item 37 fix).

### 22. Methods-contract 'Optimization' field omits the Fisher table M-step and hardcodes clipping as per-role (medium)

The location is `vfe3/paper/tables.py:921-955`. `_optimization_items` prints "optimizer AdamW, decoupled weight decay" whenever every group has `decoupled_weight_decay` True (`:925-927`), prints `lr=initial_lr` for each group (`:929-930`), and reads as extra raw keys only `warmup_steps`, `max_steps`, `min_lr_frac`, `grad_clip`, `m_phi_update_mode`, `phi_mstep_max_matrix_norm` and `phi_frame_condition_max` (`:942-944`); `m_table_update_mode`, `m_table_fisher_lr`, `m_table_fisher_trust_radius` and `grad_clip_scope` are never read, and the clip text is the constant "per optimizer role, each role's L2 norm bounded by grad_clip" (`:947-951`), although `train.py:996` branches on `grad_clip_scope` and `table_optim.py:163` steps the tables with `eta=m_table_fisher_lr`. An executed probe on the tiny live route (`live_config`, K=8, 2 heads, 2 layers) found optimizer class `TableFisherAdamW`, groups `prior_bank.mu_embed` lr 0.025 `table_fisher='mu'` and `sigma_log_embed` lr 0.001 `table_fisher='sigma'`, and `grad_clip_scope='group'`; `_optimization_items` returned "optimizer AdamW, decoupled weight decay", "prior_bank.mu_embed: lr=0.025, wd=0" and "gradient clipping per optimizer role ... (roles: mu, phi, sigma)", with no item mentioning `m_table_update_mode`, while the realized table step is the natural-gradient step with eta=0.1 and trust radius 0.5. This is on the active config (`m_table_update_mode='fisher'` and `grad_clip_scope='group'`, `train_vfe3.py:278, :323`), reached through `methods_contract_table`, `paper_artifacts.py` and `assemble.py:148`. The proposed fix is to report `m_table_update_mode`, `m_table_fisher_lr` and `m_table_fisher_trust_radius`, label the table groups' lr as "schedule/decay rate", and read `grad_clip_scope` to print "per optimizer group" or "per role" accordingly. On a fisher-mode run with `grad_clip_scope='group'`, `methods_contract_items` listing the table natural-gradient step and a per-group clip would falsify it. Reported by posthoc-paper; new.

### 23. loss_curve.png labels the full training objective 'Training cross-entropy' and, on resumed runs, plots at steps 1..n with misplaced epoch boundaries (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:5409-5433`, `vfe3/train.py:4432-4434`, `vfe3/train.py:4739` and `vfe3/model/model.py:1314-1396`. `losses.append(step_loss)` records the optimizer loss, `ce if decode_objective is None else decode_objective`, plus the self-coupling, CG and minus-EM terms, and `_save_figures` draws it with `ylabel="train CE (nats/token)", title="Training cross-entropy"` at `list(range(1, n + 1))` under the comment "the 1-based step index IS the x-axis", with epoch boundaries from `list(range(steps_per_epoch, n + 1, steps_per_epoch))`. The training loop runs `range(start_step, n_steps)`, so after a resume `losses` holds only the continuation. Executed by viz-contracts: `probe_so.py` with `decode_second_order=True` gave losses [1.71013, 1.56915, 0.96505] against a `train_ce` column of [1.56556, 1.41983, 0.76696], so the plotted series equals `train_second_order_ce`; `probe_resume.py` (resume from `step_2.pt` of a 4-step run) produced 2 resumed losses plotted at "Training cross-entropy x = [1, 2]" while "Gradient norm x = [3.0, 4.0]" and the metrics.csv steps are [3, 4], and the epoch boundaries carry the same offset. The gap-checkpoint-resume-exactness investigator, reading the code, adds that a run resumed at step 4 of 8 returned 4 losses (the existing test asserts `len(losses_c) == 2`), that a run resumed from step 60000 of 120000 would draw its 60000 continuation losses at x = 1..60000 with `annotate_final` labeling the final point at the wrong step, and that `val_ppl.png` and `grad_norm.png` use the absolute `step` column (`_aligned`), so in one run directory the loss curve and the other trajectories disagree on the x axis.

The label error is reached with `decode_second_order=True`, `mstep_self_coupling_weight>0`, `cg_energy_weight>0` or `block_mlp_em_weight>0`; the axis error is reached by any `resume_from` run finalized through `finalize_run` or `finalize_validation_run` (figure worker). The proposed fix is to plot the `train_ce` column, or label the series "training objective" whenever an extra loss term is active, and to pass the resume offset (for example `artifacts.final_step - len(losses)`) or use the history's step column so the x axis is `start_step+1..n_steps` with epoch boundaries at absolute multiples of `steps_per_epoch`. It would be falsified by losses equal to `train_ce` under `decode_second_order`, by a resumed run whose loss_curve x axis starts at the checkpoint step, or by a `losses` list that includes the pre-resume steps. Reported by viz-contracts and gap-checkpoint-resume-exactness, who disagree on severity (medium and low respectively, the latter reporting only the resume axis); new (audit-2026-10-12-round2 item 12 fixed the CSV columns, not this figure).

### 24. numerical_health checks only layer 0's belief and mixes it with the last layer's routing capture in numerical_trust.png (medium)
