The locations are `vfe3/viz/extract.py:505-542`, `vfe3/viz/extract.py:619`, `vfe3/viz/figures.py:4535-4540` and `vfe3/viz/report.py:950-951`. `out = _snapshot_sequence(snapshot.layer_converged[0])` (`extract.py:527`) supplies `nan_mu`, `nan_sigma`, `nan_phi` and `max_condition`, while `capture = snapshot.conditional_capture` supplies `nan_energy` and `nan_beta`, and the guard rows come from `readout_state`, which reads `snapshot.layer_converged[-1]` (`extract.py:619`). An executed probe (`probe_health.py`, tiny active route, `n_layers=3`) found `snapshot.conditional_capture['beta_pre']` equal to `layer_conditional_captures[2]` (True) and not to `[0]` or `[1]` (False), and `layer_converged[0].sigma` differing from `layer_converged[-1].sigma`. The figure's non-finite mu/Sigma/phi rows and its "largest covariance condition number" title therefore describe layer 0 only, the beta and energy rows describe the last layer, and no NaN or ill-conditioning in layers 1..L-2 can appear; the docstring's claim that the first layer's "captured routing score / weights" are read is also false. This is on the active config (`n_layers=4`) and runs on every finalize-time publication report via `generate_figures -> extract.numerical_health -> plot_numerical_trust`. The proposed fix is to read every layer, or at least `layer_converged[-1]` together with `layer_conditional_captures[-1]`, report per-layer maxima and summed counts with summed denominators, and name the layer scope in the figure title. It would be falsified by a build in which `snapshot.conditional_capture` is layer 0's capture and `numerical_health` aggregates over all of `snapshot.layer_converged`, or in which the probe shows `conditional_capture` equal to `layer_conditional_captures[0]`. Reported by viz-contracts; new.

### 25. beta_channel_decomposition figure and run log call content_channel 'content energy' and drop rope_position_channel (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/viz/figures.py:2218-2249`, `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:4334-4338` and `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:4503-4508`. The writer (audit 2026-10-10 item 5) emits `rope_position_channel` and `energy_includes_rope_position: rope_active`, and its own interpretation says `content_channel` is "CONTENT ... plus RoPE position when energy_includes_rope_position is true". The consumer plots only `("positional_channel", "positional prior"), ("content_channel", "content energy")` and never reads either new key, and the run log at `:4336` prints "positional prior %+.4f vs content energy %+.4f". An executed probe (run1, tiny active route) gave `delta_ce = {content_channel: -0.001254, positional_channel: -3.3e-05, rope_position_channel: -0.001266}` with `energy_includes_rope_position: true`, so the bar labelled "content energy" is almost entirely the RoPE rotation in this sample. It is on the active config (`rope_mode='comparison_invariant'`, so `rope_active` is True), rendered by `figure_worker._render_saved_probe_figures` and logged by `_log_mechanism_diagnostics` at every finalize. The proposed fix is, when `energy_includes_rope_position` is true, to draw the `rope_position_channel` bar and relabel `content_channel` as "routing cost (content + RoPE position)", or to plot `content_channel` minus `rope_position_channel` with the non-additivity stated, and to apply the same change to the `_log_mechanism_diagnostics` line. It would be falsified by `plot_beta_channel_decomposition` reading `energy_includes_rope_position` or `rope_position_channel`, or by a record from the active route with `energy_includes_rope_position` false. Reported by viz-contracts; new (follow-on to the audit-2026-10-10 item 5 writer change).

### 26. 32 MiB tile budgets split scorer moments and routed spread into 26 and 11 checkpointed tiles per layer, about 66% of dispatched ops (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/relational_scoring.py:23`, `vfe3/relational_scoring.py:230-250`, `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:23` and `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:85-95`. In `projected_moments`, `bytes_per_token = prod(batch)*roles*H*r*d*4` = 32*2*5*32*160*4 = 6,553,600 B, and `step = max(1, min(tokens, 32, _PROJECTION_TILE_BYTES // bytes_per_token))` = 5 tokens, giving ceil(128/5) = 26 tiles per call, each wrapped in `checkpoint(...)` and each about 1 GFLOP of GPU work (X = S W and G = X X^T). In `_centered_spread`, `per_row = B*H*d*4*N` = 2.6 MB, so 12 rows per tile and 11 checkpointed tiles. The op counts were executed with a `TorchDispatchMode` counter on CPU (torch 2.11.0+cpu) at the live per-layer shape with B=1 and both budgets scaled by 1/32 so the loops run their B=32 trip counts (`probe_ops.py`): the 4-layer forward+backward dispatched 12,664 forward plus 24,613 backward = 37,277 aten ops, against 5,460 + 7,249 = 12,709 with budgets equivalent to 256 MiB (4 and 2 tiles), and `projected_moments` alone dropped from 7,288 to 1,128 forward ops. In isolation at B=4 (`probe_tiles.py`), 26 tiles gave 1,874 forward and 4,361 backward ops against 290 and 687 for 4 tiles, and 1,204 ms against 309 ms CPU forward+backward; on CPU, a, G and dU^-1 were bitwise equal and dS (the comparison-map gradient summed over tiles) differed by 2.5e-7 relative. As context, the live run's `train_step_ms_mean` is 381-392 ms, so 37.3k ops is about 10 us per op, and the investigator's FLOP count of about 2.5 TFLOP per step is roughly 100 ms at realistic batched IEEE-fp32 throughput; both point to a dispatch-bound step. The estimated impact is up to about two thirds of per-step dispatch work, and the wall-clock gain was not measured because GPU runs were forbidden for this audit.

It is on the active config (K=160, H=5, d=r=32, N=128, B=32, L=4); at K=20 the projection fit in one tile (`docs/2026-09-23.md:199-200`), and the trip counts grow with B*H*r*K. The proposed fix is to make the projection and spread tile budgets device-aware, for example 256 MiB on CUDA (the 32-token cap still bounds a projection tile at about 210 MiB of transient X at B=32), and expose them as a toggle; it is numerics-changing (the float32 reduction order of the shared-map gradients changes, 2.5e-7 measured, and cuBLAS may choose different kernels per batch count), so the current 32 MiB should stay the default until a 5090 timing confirms the gain. A `torch.profiler` trace of one live-dict step on an idle 5090 showing the GPU continuously busy through these tiles, or a CUDA-event A/B with the larger budgets that moves `train_step_ms_mean` by less than 5%, would retract the wall-clock impact, while the op counts would stand. Reported by performance; new.

### 27. predictive_content broadcast matmul materializes a (B*N*H, d, K) copy of T: 412 MiB saved per layer at B=32 (medium)

The location is `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:788-791`. The line `values = (T.to(dtype) @ coordinates.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)` with T of shape (H,d,K) and coordinates (...,N,1,K) makes `torch.matmul` expand T to the (B,N,H) batch and reshape it into a contiguous copy for bmm, which autograd saves. An executed `saved_tensors_hooks` probe (`probe_content_values.py`, CPU, B=4, N=128, H=5, d=32, K=160) measured 51.6 MiB saved for the broadcast form against 0.4 MiB for `torch.einsum('hsk,...nk->...nhs', T, xi)`; scaled to B=32 that is about 412 MiB per layer and 1.6 GiB over 4 layers, about 12% of the measured 3.29 GiB per layer of saved activations (`probe_saved.py`). CPU forward+backward took 32.5 ms against 0.7 ms, and the backward also writes a same-sized expanded gradient before reducing it. The blockwise covariance update already computes the same contraction as one GEMM (`relational_operators.py:1440`), and the two forms differ at rounding level, 2.8e-7 max relative. It is on the active config (kernel predictive readout, `predictive_content` mean, called once per layer from `vfe3/gradients/relational.py:332`). The proposed fix is to replace the broadcast matmul with `torch.einsum('hsk,...nk->...nhs', T.to(dtype), canonical.to(dtype))`, gated per project practice because it changes float32 reduction order. It would be falsified by `torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated` on the live dict unchanged after the swap, or a CUDA memory snapshot showing no (B*N*H, d, K) buffer saved by `predictive_content`. Reported by performance; new (`audit-2026-10-12.md:220` reported a similar broadcast-matmul copy in `sequential_head_assembly`, a different site).

### 28. One certificate-flagged entry sends 131 checkpointed 32x1 pair tiles over every (batch, head) slice through triangular solves (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/relational_scoring.py:1172-1175`, `:1231-1246` and `:853-872`. `_tile_steps(element_size, r*r)` gives `pair_budget = 32 MiB // (4 * B*H=160 * 1024)` = 51, so q_step = 32 and k_step = 1, and every tile slices only the token axes, evaluating all 160 (b,h) slices before applying `torch.where(pair_rec, ...)`. An executed probe (`probe_recovery_tiles.py`) forced one flagged entry (b=0, i=5, h=0) at the live B=32, N=128, H=5, r=32 shape with the tile body stubbed and counted 131 tile calls of shape 32x1, covering 4,192 (i,j) pairs times 160 slices, about 671k pair solves, for 256 genuinely touched pairs. Under grad each tile is also checkpointed (recompute plus backward), and the working-dtype rebuild adds another full `_strict_cholesky(safe)` (factorization, certificate and `host_read`) per role, plus `bool(...)` reads in `_source_root`, `shifted_failed.any()` and the `flags.tolist()` transfer, at least 5 extra syncs per role per layer. For a diagonal P(G) the factors are diagonal, so the r x r `solve_triangular` is an elementwise division. It is reachable on the active config whenever condition recovery flags or fails any comparison entry; the live run's metrics.csv shows `source_recovery_elements` 0 to 1 through step 2400, so it is latent there, while `docs/audit-results_2026-10-15-instability.md` reports 883k recoveries in one 300-step bisection group and 13 to 30 s per step for concurrent arms. The proposed fix is to gather only the (b,h) slices that hold a recovered entry, as the GEMM-fallback branch already does with `index_select` (`relational_scoring.py:1187-1220`), size the pair tile from that slice count, and use an elementwise direct-difference tile for a commutant-diagonal factor. It would be falsified by a flagged entry producing 4 or fewer tile calls, or by `source_recovery_elements` staying at 0 across full live-dict runs, which would make the path practically unreachable at this config. Reported by performance; new.

### 29. pullback_group materializes the dense generator basis and runs the product-residual check densely in float64 on every backtracking attempt (medium)

The locations are `vfe3/gauge_optim.py:431`, `:240-253`, `:270`, `:316` and `:1725`. `stage_pullback_group_candidate` passes `group.generators` (`:431`), whose descriptor replaces the lazy `_ElementaryBlockBasis` or `_BlockLocalBasis` with the dense (n_gen, K, K) tensor. `_pullback_group_compact_blocks` is gated on `group.name == "block_glk"` (`:248`), so for every block-local group `_pullback_group_current_element` (`:270`) and `_pullback_group_product_residual` (`:316`) run `group.generators.to(dtype=torch.float64)`, a fresh float64 copy on each backtracking attempt inside the `for attempt in range(max_backtracks + 1)` loop, and then embed each active row as a dense K x K matrix and exponentiate it instead of H blocks of d x d. A spy on `_GeneratorTensor.__get__` at tiny scale (executed) recorded "dense reads: 1" for live `block_glk` with `pullback_group` (the source becoming `Tensor`), "dense reads: 2" for `block_sl` with `pullback_group`, and 0 under adamw. By arithmetic at K=160, H=10, d=16 with `block_sl`, the float64 basis copy is 2550*160*160*8 B = 522 MB per attempt, and the dense exp costs K^3/(H d^3) = 100x the block exp per row. This contradicts the `_BlockLocalBasis` contract (`groups.py:47-52`) that training paths read only the (m, d, d) local basis, and `:1725` reads `.generators.shape[-1]` just to obtain K. It is reachable with `m_phi_update_mode='pullback_group'` and any group: `block_glk` (dense read at `:431`) and every block-local group `block_so`, `block_co`, `block_sl`, `block_borel`, `block_unipotent` and `block_glmc` (dense reads at `:270` and `:316` too); the config budget admits d_head <= 16 for gl-sized local algebras, and it is not the active config (adamw). The proposed fix is to extend the compact-block residual and current-element branches to any group with `certified_block_local_basis()` or `head_block_generators()` (embedding through the local basis), pass a lazy-aware basis to `pullback_group_direction`, and use `generator_shape[-1]` at `:1725`. It would be falsified by a spy on `GaugeGroup.generators` recording zero dense reads, with `group._generator_source` still a `_BlockLocalBasis`, after a `block_sl` `pullback_group` train_step. Reported by gauge-groups; partly previously reported as audit-2026-10-08 hotspot-4#5 (P2-22), which removed the float64 dense copy from the `block_glk` residual check only, while the block-local groups (admitted 2026-10-07 F47 and 2026-10-16 Plan C) and the `:431` read remain.

### 30. No test checks the pushforward against Monte Carlo in the capped regime, and no test builds a model under fully_gauge_covariant (medium)

The locations are `tests/test_audit_20261012_pushforward.py:38-150`, `tests/test_block_norm_covariance_20261010.py:304-330` and `tests/test_fully_gauge_covariant_20261014.py:57-110`. Read from the tests: in `test_audit_20261012_pushforward.py` the only Monte Carlo test, `test_sphere_bound_holds_for_the_layernorm_output`, checks the trace bound, and the capped-regime model test `test_model_with_pushforward_stays_finite_at_large_sigma_init` (sigma_init=15, mu_init_std=0.01) asserts only finiteness, so nothing compares the capped shape with Monte Carlo, which is where the previous findings show the approximation is uncorrelated with the truth. The whole-model finite-difference test perturbs a single coordinate in a regime it does not show to be capped. `test_fully_gauge_covariant_20261014.py` contains no `VFEModel` construction; its functions are `test_overrides_reach_the_gauge_facet...`, `test_a_covariant_configuration_gets_no_overrides`, `test_combinations_without_a_covariant_reading_are_refused`, `test_launcher_switch_defaults_off` and `test_ablation_switch...`, and `docs/2026-10-14.md` states that no training run was made with the switch on, so the pure path's numerical degeneracy described above is not exercised by any test. This concerns the test suite covering the active pushforward path and the pure-path switch. The proposed fix is to add a float64 Monte Carlo comparison of diagonal and full pushforward variances at Sigma/s^2 of about 1e3 to 1e4, and a tiny live-route forward/backward under `fully_gauge_covariant_overrides(live_values())` that checks layer-input covariance conditioning. It would be falsified by an existing test that compares the capped pushforward elementwise with Monte Carlo, or one that builds and steps a model under the fully_gauge_covariant overrides. Reported by gradients-tests; new.

## Low-severity findings

### 31. fully_gauge_covariant switch emits configurations the validator refuses on projected_gradient and expected_likelihood_chunked (low)

In `vfe3/gauge_covariant.py:56-86`, the non-self-covariant branch always sets `block_coordinates='reference'`, and the family branch rewrites only `diagonal_chunked` to `full_chunked`. The switch therefore emits configurations that `VFE3Config` refuses, including routes where a covariant configuration exists. This was executed on `live_values()`: `projected_gradient` with `use_block_mlp=False` and `norm_type_block='none'` gives overrides `{..., 'block_coordinates': 'reference'}`, and `VFE3Config` raises "block_coordinates='reference' ... requires 'projected_predictive'". The same configuration with `block_coordinates='local'` has `gauge_covariance_failures == []`, and the end-to-end probe confirms it is covariant: dlogits of 5.95e-15 / 2.22e-14 under a per-position local gauge, against 4.79e-02 / 1.19e-01 for the beliefs-only negative control. With `decode_mode='expected_likelihood_chunked'` and the diagonal family, the switch moves to `gaussian_full` but leaves the decoder alone, and the result is refused as rank-incompatible. The switch also forces `use_cg_coupling=False` whenever the MLP is not `canonical_frame`/`gauge_gate`, even with the block MLP off, where tied CG in local coordinates is covariant (executed: `so_n` shared + CG + `canonical_frame` gives dlogits 1.2e-14).

This is reachable from `train_vfe3.py` or `ablation.py` with `fully_gauge_covariant=True` and `attention_mode='projected_gradient'` (any block settings), or with `decode_mode='expected_likelihood_chunked'`; it fails closed at `VFE3Config` construction. The proposed fix is to emit `block_coordinates='reference'` only when an active norm or MLP needs it and the route admits it (keeping `'local'` when nothing frame-dependent runs in the block), and to map `expected_likelihood_chunked` to a reference-admitted decoder or raise a targeted error. The finding is falsified if `VFE3Config(**{**vals, **fully_gauge_covariant_overrides(vals)})` constructs without error for `vals = live_values(attention_mode='projected_gradient', predictive_covariance='passthrough', attention_residual=False, rope_mode='none', use_block_mlp=False, norm_type_block='none', block_norm_covariance='passthrough')`. Reported by gauge-groups; new.

### 32. Paper arm 'Head-block family, frame-free Cholesky table' cannot be built from a base run with m_table_update_mode='fisher' (low)

At `paper_arms.py:404-407` the arm sets `{'family': 'gaussian_block', 'prior_covariance_table': 'block_cholesky', 'gauge_transport': 'off'}` and coerces only `GAUGE_OFF_COERCED`, while the validator at `vfe3/config.py:1932-1942` refuses any non-diagonal table under `m_table_update_mode == "fisher"` (lines 1939-1942). Executed: `paper_arms.rebuild` from `VFE3Config(**train_vfe3.config)` raises "m_table_update_mode='fisher' needs prior_covariance_table='diagonal' (got 'block_cholesky')"; every other non-inapplicable arm built except the two pushforward arms of the previous finding. This affects any paper base trained from the current launcher (`m_table_update_mode='fisher'`); the archived bases use adamw tables and build. The fix is to give the arm the companion override `m_table_update_mode='adamw'`, declared in its `coerced` list, or to mark it inapplicable on a fisher-table base; a successful `paper_arms.rebuild` for this arm on a fisher-table base would falsify the finding. Reported by config-wiring; new.

### 33. fully_gauge_covariant override log prints the launcher literal, showing omitted keys as 'None -> None' (low)

At `train_vfe3.py:560-562` the log line is built as `", ".join(f"{key}={config.get(key)!r} -> {value!r}" for key, value in overrides.items())`, but `fully_gauge_covariant_overrides` decides the overrides from `config.get(key, getattr(VFE3Config(), key))` (`gauge_covariant.py:46`). Keys absent from the launcher dict are therefore logged as `None`. Executed on the current `train_vfe3.config`: the logged line contains `sigma_max=None -> None`, while `VFE3Config(**config).sigma_max == 100.0`, so the real change is 100.0 -> None; `config.md` receives the correct overrides dict in its header. This fires whenever `fully_gauge_covariant=True` and an override key is missing from the launcher, which the WIP launcher's removal of `sigma_max` makes the current case. The fix is to log the resolved pre-override value, for example `getattr(VFE3Config(**{**config, 'seed': seed}), key)`, or to reuse the defaults lookup; a logged `sigma_max=100.0 -> None` on the current launcher would falsify the finding. Reported by config-wiring; new.

### 34. WIP launcher dropped predictive_covariance_floor_mode and leaves categorical keys without valid-values comments (low)

Commit 322843b (2026-10-16) added `predictive_covariance_floor_mode = "prior",  # "prior" ... | "relative" ...` to `train_vfe3.py`, but the working-tree launcher no longer has it (`git diff HEAD -- train_vfe3.py` shows the line removed between `predictive_covariance_floor` and `predictive_covariance_ceiling`, near `train_vfe3.py:160-162`). AGENTS.md (rule added in 5f5d484, 2026-10-12) requires every new `VFE3Config` field in the dict at its default with a valid-values comment, and `decode_av_precision = "fp32",` (`train_vfe3.py:331`) and `decode_ranking_fp64_escalation = False,` (`train_vfe3.py:340`) carry none. Executed: 39 `VFE3Config` fields are absent from the launcher, and `predictive_covariance_floor_mode` is the only one introduced after the rule; `tests/test_live_route_profile.py` passes because the omitted field equals its default `'prior'`, so there is no runtime effect. The fix is to restore the line at `'prior'` with its comment and add `"fp32" | "fp64"` and `True | False` comments to the two keys. The finding is falsified if the removal is an explicit user decision recorded in `docs/2026-10-16.md`, which would make it intentional working state. Reported by config-wiring; new.

### 35. paper_arms.load_base reports migrated fields (projected_factorization, rope_mode) as 'defaulted' (low)

At `paper_arms.py:557-561`, `present = set(raw) | set(pinned)` counts only literal keys and pins, while `config_from_serialized` merges `projected_cov_condition_recovery`/`projected_factor_route` into `projected_factorization` and `pos_rotation`/`rope_space` into `rope_mode`. Executed: `load_base('k128_alibi').defaulted` contains `projected_factorization` and `rope_mode`, though both were derived from keys the record carries; the same list correctly carries `block_norm_covariance` and `predictive_covariance_floor_mode`, so the plan mixes truly defaulted fields with migrated ones. This affects every plan built from a pre-2026-10-12 base, including the configured `k128_alibi`. The fix is to add the surviving names of the merged selector groups (`projected_factorization`, `rope_mode`, `frame_pair_escalation_tol`, `attention_mode`) to `present` when any of their source keys is in `raw`; the finding is falsified if `load_base('k128_alibi').defaulted` contains neither field. Reported by config-wiring; new.

### 36. inactive_fields() does not mark decode_av_precision or decode_ranking_fp64_escalation inert under expected_likelihood_chunked (low)

In `vfe3/config.py:2308-2311` the two fields are marked with `mark(("decode_av_precision", "decode_ranking_fp64_escalation"), ...)` only inside `if self.decode_mode == "linear":`, yet `_decode_expected_likelihood_chunked` (`vfe3/model/prior_bank.py:2436-2482`) computes `-0.5 * (d ** 2 / s + torch.log(s)).sum(-1) / tau_eff` with no a_v reduction and no ranking guard; the guard is read only at `prior_bank.py:2210` and `:2335`, in the diagonal and full decodes. An executed probe with `live_config(decode_mode='expected_likelihood_chunked')` showed that flipping `decode_av_precision` to `'fp64'`, or `decode_ranking_fp64_escalation` to True, left the loss, every parameter gradient and the eval logits `torch.equal`, while `cfg.inactive_fields()` returned None for both; under `diagonal_chunked` the same fp64 flip changed the loss. The toggle is reachable and affects the launcher's "Inactive launcher settings" log and the ablation dead-sweep guard. The fix is to mark both fields from the decode registration (any decoder without the a_v reduction or ranking guard). It is falsified by a read of `pb.decode_av_precision` or `pb.decode_ranking_fp64_escalation` on the expected-likelihood decode or its fused CE, or a probe in which flipping either changes the loss, gradients or ranking metrics there. Reported by dead-code; new.

### 37. Exact spectral (Daleckii-Krein) Psi route is documented as opt-in but no config field enables it (low)

`vfe3/geometry/phi_preconditioner.py:100-116` describes `"spectral"` as the exact Daleckii-Krein closed form and calls it opt-in, with `_PHI_GROUP_DIFFERENTIAL_MODE: str = "series"`; the only writer is `set_phi_group_differential_mode` (`phi_preconditioner.py:404-414`), whose callers are three test files and none in `vfe3/` or the root scripts. A grep over `config.py` and `train_vfe3.py` finds no field or launcher key mentioning `differential_mode`, yet `GaugeManifoldAdamW` still snapshots and checkpoints the mode (`vfe3/gauge_optim.py:1493`, `1534-1555`), persisting state for a setting no run can change. This evidence was read, not executed. It matters only under `m_phi_update_mode='pullback_group'` or `'fisher_group'`, and then only through a process-global Python setter. The fix is to expose it as a validated `VFE3Config` field (default `'series'`) applied by `build_optimizer`, or to document it as a test-only switch; a production or launcher call of the setter, or a config field that sets it, would falsify the finding. Reported by dead-code; new.

### 38. so_n tensor-power irreps are not exactly skew and are built outside the single-thread guard used for su_n (low)

`_tensor_power_irrep` (`vfe3/geometry/irreps.py:237-250`, used at `irreps.py:625-644`) returns `Q.T @ _leibniz_action(G_def[a], p) @ Q` with no antisymmetrization, whereas the su path does `rho = 0.5 * (rho - rho.transpose(-1, -2))  # exactly skew` (`irreps.py:674`) under a `torch.set_num_threads(1)` guard, adopted after the 2026-10-16 log found that a threaded matmul changed the last bit of the (2,2) images and hence the fingerprint. Executed: max|G + G^T| is 2.2e-16 for `so_n` N=4, 5 and 6 at l2, and 4.4e-16 for N=4 l3, and exactly 0 for the SO(3) ladder and wedge blocks, contradicting `vfe3/geometry/groups.py:715-718` ("every embedded generator is exactly skew"). The load check at `vfe3/model/model.py:578-592` compares sha256 bytes exactly (`587-592`), so any last-bit change from threading, BLAS dispatch or a torch upgrade in this pinv/Gram-Schmidt/matmul path refuses the checkpoint as trained in a different `so_n` irrep basis. Per the 2026-10-16 log, `so_n` towers up to N=7 l3 were measured bit-identical across thread counts; cross-version portability is not measured.

This is reachable with `gauge_group='so_n'`, `group_n != 3` and an `'l<p>'` block with p >= 2, and the fingerprint is checked on every checkpoint load. The proposed fix is to run `_tensor_power_irrep` under the same single-thread guard and either antisymmetrize its images (which changes existing `so_n` N >= 4 fingerprints and needs a migration note) or correct the docstring, and to consider fingerprinting a rounded basis or comparing allclose against a stored float64 copy. The finding is falsified if max|G + G^T| is exactly 0 for `get_group('so_n')(9, group_n=4, irrep_spec=[('l2',1)], dtype=torch.float64).generators`. Reported by gauge-groups; partly previously reported in `docs/2026-10-16.md` (su_n M2), which records the thread-count fingerprint dependence and fixed it for su_n only.

### 39. SPD repair rebuild differentiates through the Lorentzian-damped eigh, giving wrong gradients inside degenerate unclipped eigen-clusters (low)

At `vfe3/geometry/retraction.py:438-465` the repair runs `eigenvalues, eigenvectors = _eigh_damped(repair_input, _rel_gap_eps(repair_input))` (line 439) and rebuilds `eigenvectors * clipped @ eigenvectors^T` (lines 450-457), so autograd goes through `_EighDamped.backward`, where F = delta/(delta^2 + gap_eps) zeroes the in-cluster term. Executed probe p13 (float64) used Sigma = Q diag(5e-7, 5, 5, 5) Q^T with eps=1e-6 and direction and readout E = W = q1 q2^T + q2 q1^T inside the 5-cluster; the repair was counted (`public_spd_repair_elements()=1`), the autograd directional derivative was 0.0852, and the central finite difference was 2.0000000023, matching the exact Loewner divided difference of 1 for that pair, which gives 2. The path requires `attention_mode='projected_gradient'`, `relational_receiver_update='euler'` and `family='gaussian_full'` (`retract_spd_full -> _certify_public_spd`); only rows touching the eps floor or the sigma_max ceiling are repaired, and the error needs exactly degenerate unclipped eigenvalues there (most likely near the isotropic sigma_init start). It is not on the active config, since `projected_predictive` does not call the retraction. The fix is to differentiate the clip-and-rebuild through the Loewner divided-difference adjoint (for example a per-row-bound clip kind of `_SymmetricSpectralMap`). It is falsified if, after the change, p13 gives an autograd derivative within about 1e-6 of 2.0, or by a demonstration that `_certify_public_spd`'s backward never reaches such a row on any reachable config. Reported by transport-geometry; previously reported as `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-07.md` F34 / consolidated item 27, whose Loewner-adjoint part is still unapplied while the repair counter and clearance ladder were adopted.

### 40. pullback_group is refused at the active head width although its group_log closed form and an off-strip eigenbasis solver exist (low)

`_pullback_structure_problem` (`vfe3/config.py:2478-2507`, applied at `config.py:1477-1479`) applies the gl(d) structure-constant budget to both retractions, and `strict_structure_bytes(1024, 32)` is 24576 MiB against 512 MiB. Under `group_log`, however, structure constants are needed only for the fallback rows of `pullback_group_log_direction` (off the strip, or max_h kappa_2(U_h) > 1e3), which call `pullback_group_direction` (`vfe3/gauge_optim.py:646-675`, specifically 652-663). Executed probe p11 called the stager at K=64, H=2, d=32: on-strip rows succeeded through the closed form (`fast=[True, True]`, `rejected=[False, False]`), and pushing one block 3.2 rad off the strip raised `ValueError: ... 1024 generators on a 32 x 32 block need 24576 MiB`. Off-strip rows can already be solved without structure constants by `uspace_ambient_gradient(offstrip=True)` (`_spectral_psi_inverse`, `vfe3/geometry/phi_preconditioner.py:283-287`, `1301-1345`), which `belief_fisher_group_direction` uses at `gauge_optim.py:747`; probe p3 checked that solve at 1.5e-10 relative error.

This concerns `m_phi_update_mode='pullback_group'` (either retraction) at d_head=32 (K=160, 5 heads), which the config refuses at construction; `fisher_group` remains reachable at this width. The proposed improvement is, under `group_log`, to solve off-strip rows with `uspace_ambient_gradient(offstrip=True)` and xi = U^{-1} G_bar, reject rows that fail the kappa or Psi gate instead of falling back to the series, and skip the budget check for that retraction. It is falsified by a row class at d=32 that only the certified series can handle (for example rows with |Psi| < 1e-8 where the series certificate still passes), or by a measured prohibitive cost of the eigenbasis route at the active width. Reported by transport-geometry; the budget was raised in `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12.md` ("Decision for the user"), and the point that the `group_log` route depends on it only through its fallback is new.

### 41. fp64 covariance escalation rewrites whole sequences, so one future token's fp32 failure changes earlier positions' logits (low)

In `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:1286-1319`, `rows = bad.any(dim=-1).nonzero().flatten()` (line 1293) selects every sequence with a failing token, and `return sigma_plus.index_copy(0, rows, sigma_wide.to(sigma_plus.dtype))` (line 1319) overwrites every token of those sequences with the float64 recompute. Executed on the live tiny route (K=8, H=2, N=8, L=1) with an injected fp32 failure (variance -1) only at the last token of sequence 0: logits at positions 0..N-2 of that sequence, which cannot see the last token, changed by up to 1.526e-05, the healthy sequence changed by 0.0, and `covariance_escalation_elements` reported 8 tokens recomputed for one failing token. Since each receiver's update reads only its own beta row and sources j <= i (`_predictive_covariance_update_blockwise`), writing back only the failing rows is exact and strictly causal. This is on the active config (`predictive_covariance='moment_matched'`, `predictive_covariance_fp64_escalation=True`) whenever any token's fp32 update is not PD. The fix is to write back only the failing receiver rows, for example `torch.where(bad[rows][..., None], sigma_wide, sigma_plus[rows])` before `index_copy`, optionally restricting the fp64 pooled/spread computation to those rows to cut cost. Re-running the injection probe after the fix should leave positions 0..N-2 bitwise unchanged; if the current code already gives 0.0 there, the finding is wrong. Reported by attention; related to `docs/2026-10-15.md:23` (the per-sequence escalation that replaced whole-call recompute after the K=160 run fell from about 4 to 1 it/s), with the causality coupling new.

### 42. Shifted CholeskyQR3 shift uses eps and ||X||_F^2; claimed validity to kappa ~ 1/eps is about 2.5 decades too high (low)

The shift at `vfe3/numerics.py:638-656` (used from `vfe3/relational_scoring.py:96-98` and `792-796`) is s = 11(mr + r(r+1)) eps ||X||_F^2, which is up to 2r times the Fukaya et al. (SISC 42 (2020) A477) shift s = 11(mn + n(n+1)) u ||X||_2^2; a larger shift leaves kappa(C1^-1 X) ~ sqrt(s)/||X|| * kappa(X) above the CholeskyQR2 limit u^-1/2 sooner. Executed (torch 2.11.0+cpu, r=32, m=160, fp32), the failure rate of `cholesky_qr2(X, shift=True)` on a log-spaced spectrum was 0.00 at kappa=1e4, 0.70-0.78 at 1e5, and 1.00 at 3e5 and 1e6, against 0.09-0.12 at kappa=1e5 for Fukaya's shift (u = eps/2, 2-norm); on a flat spectrum with one small singular value at kappa=3e4 the rates were 0.50 against 0.00. Even the theoretical shift gives a range of about 1/(u sqrt(11(mn+n(n+1)))) ~ 6e4 here, not the 1/eps = 8.4e6 the docstring claims. Where the factorization succeeds the error is the same (2.4e-3 vs 2.8e-3 at kappa=1e5), and failures fall back to float64 QR, so the cost is extra fp64 work, not silent error. It is reached under `gram_certified`, or uncertified factor-route recovery, when `rope_mode` is not `comparison_invariant` or a non-rotating block of size > 1 exists, and not on the active diagonal commutant, where m == r returns only plane deviations. The fix is to use u = eps/2 with a 2-norm estimate (a few power iterations on X X^T, or ||X||_F^2 / r as a lower proxy with the true bound checked) and change the docstring to kappa(X) <~ 1/(u sqrt(11(mr + r(r+1)))). A run at r=32, m=160 where the repo shift's fp32 failure rate at kappa=1e5 matches Fukaya's would falsify the finding. Reported by numerics; new.

### 43. Covariance ceiling calls bare eigh and the raising cholesky, outside batch-safe and failure-masked conventions (low)

`_SpectralCeiling.forward` (`vfe3/model/covariance_ceiling.py:62`) calls `torch.linalg.eigh(W)` directly instead of `vfe3.numerics.safe_eigh`, so a CUDA call with more than about 23,325 clamped matrices of size <= 32 hits the cuSOLVER batched-eigensolver ceiling documented at `numerics.py:370-379`, and `_dense_ceiling` (`covariance_ceiling.py:99`) factors the anchor with the raising `torch.linalg.cholesky` rather than `cholesky_ex` plus a mask. This was read; the adjoint itself was executed and is correct (float64 gradcheck passes, the degenerate-spectrum directional derivative matches central FD to 6.6e-11, and an eigenvalue exactly at c takes the right derivative). It applies when `predictive_covariance_ceiling` is set (off on the active config) with `gaussian_full` or `gaussian_block`; the diagonal family takes the elementwise min. The fix is to use `safe_eigh` and `cholesky_ex` with a failure verdict; a CUDA call clamping more than 23,325 head blocks of size 32 that completes would falsify it. Reported by numerics; new.

### 44. Full-Gaussian per-coordinate KL forms delta - log1p(delta) directly, and the x_minus_log1p band edge of 1e-3 is narrower than its series allows (low)

`vfe3/families/gaussian.py:116-118` forms delta - log1p(delta) directly, and `x_minus_log1p` in `vfe3/families/base.py:29-33` switches to its series only below |x| = 1e-3. Executed (torch 2.11.0+cpu, fp32, maximum relative error of delta^2 + 2(delta - log1p(delta)) against float64): at delta ~1e-4 the error is 3.6e-4 as written against 1.1e-7 with `x_minus_log1p`, and at delta ~1e-5 it is 4.5e-3 against 1.5e-7; just above the 1e-3 band edge the wide branch still carries 5.7e-5 relative error, while the series truncation x^4/3 relative would be 3e-9 at x = 1e-2. Absolute errors are around 1e-12 or smaller, so KL values are not materially affected. The direct form is reached on `gaussian_full` / `gaussian_block` with `lambda_alpha_mode='state_dependent_per_coord'`, and `x_minus_log1p` is on every diagonal KL. The fix is to use `x_minus_log1p(delta)` in `_full_gaussian_kl_coordinate_terms` and widen the series band to |x| < 1e-2 with one more term; a per-coordinate alpha computation whose output changes by more than rounding under the cancellation-free form would falsify the finding. Reported by numerics; new.

### 45. Logged Dong rank-one residual is computed on local-coordinate means, so it is frame-dependent and overstates token spread (low)

At `vfe3/model/model.py:1921-1922`, `metrics.rank_one_residual(snapshot.layer_outputs[layer].mu.double())` (`vfe3/metrics.py:633-651`) reads mu_i = U_i xi_i, which includes the per-token frame U_i and is not invariant under the passive gauge action, while the router mixes the invariant reference means xi_j = U_j^{-1} mu_j. Executed at init (K=16, H=2, N=16, L=4, phi_scale=0.3, B=4), r(mu)/r(xi) at layers 0-3 were 0.725/0.634, 0.689/0.547, 0.652/0.512 and 0.661/0.526 on the live route, and 0.770/0.623, 0.720/0.482, 0.702/0.426 and 0.690/0.382 with no MLP and no norm, so frame-induced diversity in local coordinates hides the collapse of the mixed content the diagnostic is meant to detect (Dong et al. 2021). It is always logged (`relational_rank_one_residual` in diagnostics and `metrics.csv`) on every projected route. The fix is to also log, or replace with, `rank_one_residual` of xi = U^{-1} mu through the realized relational frame; if r(xi) and r(mu) agree within noise on trained checkpoints, the distinction does not matter in practice. Reported by attention; related to `docs/2026-10-11.md:31` (where the diagnostic was added) and `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-09-round2.md:31` (the per-token write path through U_i), with the frame dependence new.

### 46. 'Canonical free energy' diagnostic weights label sectors by tau_h relative to the self term, so it is not the negative ELBO of the v2 record joint (low)

`canonical_routing_rows` returns belief row = sum_j beta*_ij D_ij and entropy row = `taus[h]` * sum_j beta*(log beta* - log pi) (`vfe3/free_energy.py:652-653`), and `vfe3/model/model.py:2139-2143` assembles total = self_coupling + lambda_beta*(belief_coupling + attention_entropy). Per head this is tau_h * [KL(beta||pi) + tau_h^{-1} sum_j beta D] = tau_h * F_i^(h), where F_i^(h) is the row free energy of the declared record joint (`GL(K)_supplementary_v2.tex:944`, eq. rg_bridge_row); the supplement adds at `:948` that multiplying a row objective by tau_h preserves its optimizer but does not license rescaling sectors of one global evidence lower bound, and the main text calls the objective tau_h times the label free energy (`GL(K)_attention_v2.tex:1098`). The beta* stationarity and envelope value are correct (measured envelope residual 4.4e-16 and dense-KL residual 8.9e-16, p1), but the reported total and `canonical_total_delta` mix a unit-weight self sector with label sectors scaled by 5.657 (and by lambda_beta), so neither is a bound or an ELBO change; in the tiny live run (p7), layer 1 had self_coupling 3.185, attention_entropy 2.842 and `canonical_total_delta` -0.861. The evidence kind is recorded as read. The code and figures call it a diagnostic, not an ELBO, so this is a reading hazard rather than a wrong number. It applies with `diagnose_canonical_free_energy=True` on any route; on the live config tau_h = kappa_beta * sqrt(32) = 5.657. The fix is to also log the unit-temperature record form, self + sum_h [KL(beta*||pi) + tau_h^{-1} sum_j beta* D], or to state in the docstring and figure note that the total is the tau-tempered free energy, an ELBO only at tau_h = lambda_beta = 1. A declared normalized joint in the manuscripts whose negative ELBO has label sector tau_h KL(beta||pi) + sum_j beta D and an unscaled self sector would falsify it. Reported by variational; new.

### 47. Importance-sampled evidence with the mean-field q as proposal is heavy-tailed: per-receiver errors of several nats at 64 draws (low)

The proposal at `vfe3/model/minimal_agent.py:1492-1497` is the readout's q(X) = N(xi^+, C^+), used by the estimator at `minimal_agent.py:1124-1200`; under the mean field C^+ omits the between-source spread, so q is narrower than the target mixture and the weights w_s = p(X,o)/q(X) are heavy-tailed. An executed one-head probe (H=1, where `res.evidence` is exact) with source covariances scaled by exp(2 z), N=6, K=3, d=2 and 360 receivers gave IS(64) minus exact with mean -0.041, min -7.25 and max +1.41 nats; 25.8 percent of receivers were off by more than 0.1 nats and 2.2 percent by more than 1 nat. On the worst receiver the error was -7.25, -3.55, -0.79 and -1.11 nats at 64, 1024, 16384 and 131072 draws, far slower than n^{-1/2}, and with an ELBO gap of 7.32 nats the IS gap read 0.07. With coupled heads (H=2, compared against `tests/test_audit_20261012_round2_evidence._enumerated_evidence` over 100 receivers), IS minus enumerated had mean -0.078, min -4.26 and max +2.21 nats. The existing test `test_importance_evidence_converges_to_the_enumeration_with_coupled_heads` uses homogeneous source covariances, where convergence looks like n^{-1/2}.

This is an opt-in eval-cadence diagnostic (`diagnose_agent_elbo=True` with `predictive_readout='minimal_agent'`), never in the loss. The fix is a defensive proposal, for example the exact per-head component posteriors as a mixture or C^+ inflated by the label spread, plus an effective-sample-size or Pareto-k diagnostic beside `is_evidence`, and reporting the exact evidence where the heads are uncoupled. The finding is falsified if, on heterogeneous-covariance instances, the 64-draw estimate stays within about 0.1 nats of the enumerated evidence with error shrinking as n^{-1/2}. Reported by gap-opt-in-readout-and-link-routes; new (the estimator itself was added for `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12-round2.md` M1).

### 48. Expected-likelihood Triton kernel still rounds lse = m + log l to the ulp of the logits; gradient error 6-18x the torch route's (low)

In `vfe3/kernels/_expected_likelihood_triton.py:83-91`, `119-121`, `134` and `179`, `el_fwd_kernel` stores `lse = m + tl.log(l)` and `CE = lse - lt`, and `_pair_weights` uses `p = tl.exp(logit - lse[:, None])`; at |logit| around 1e3 (small tau) lse is rounded to ulp(m) and every softmax weight in the row inherits the error. `kl_ce_fwd_kernel`/`kl_ce_bwd_kernel` avoid this by keeping m and log l apart (`kl_decode.py:118-124`; `docs/2026-10-12.md:137` measured up to a 20x gradient-error inflation). An executed A/B under the Triton interpreter ($CUDA_PYTHON, torch 2.10.0.dev+cu128, triton 3.5.1, CUDA hidden, TRITON_INTERPRET=1, numpy fp32 so libdevice and approx ops are excluded) compared the shipped kernel, a scratch copy keeping (m, log l) apart, and the torch fp32 route against float64, as max relative gradient error over mu_q, sigma_q, mu_v and sigma_v (shipped / patched / torch): K=8 tau=0.1 5.0e-6 / 8.1e-7 / 7.1e-7; K=8 tau=0.01 3.4e-5 / 3.5e-6 / 3.0e-6; K=20 tau=0.1 1.3e-5 / 2.0e-6 / 1.4e-6; K=20 tau=0.01 8.8e-5 / 4.8e-6 / 4.8e-6; K=64 tau=0.1 2.3e-5 / 1.3e-6 / 7.6e-7; K=64 tau=0.01 2.6e-4 / 3.1e-5 / 9.4e-6. In a separate case (K=6, tau=0.01, sigma_q around 31) the shipped sigma_q gradient error was 8.5e-3 against 4.8e-5 for torch. The repo interpreter test runs only at `decode_tau=0.9` (`tests/test_expected_likelihood_kernel_20261010.py:43,128`), where the shipped kernel measures 2.1e-7 against 1.4e-7, so it cannot see the effect.

The route is `decode_mode='expected_likelihood_chunked'` on CUDA tensors, which has no selector and always runs this kernel (`prior_bank.py:1988`); the active config uses `diagonal_chunked`, so it is not the live path. The fix is to store the running max and log l in separate (S,) buffers, write CE = (m - lt) + log l, pass both to `_pair_weights` with `p = exp((logit - m) - log_l)` as `kl_ce_bwd_kernel` does, and add a tau=0.01 case to the interpreter test. A compiled-GPU or interpreter run at tau <= 0.1 where the shipped kernel's error is within 2x of the torch route's, or where the separated variant gives no improvement, would falsify it. Reported by gap-live-decode-triton-kernels; new (the same defect was fixed for the KL kernel only, `docs/2026-10-12.md:137`).

### 49. KLDecodeCE.backward is not once_differentiable, so create_graph=True silently returns wrong second derivatives (low)

`KLDecodeCE.backward` (`vfe3/kernels/kl_decode.py:126-168`) builds its result with torch ops on saved inputs (`g_lhs.addmm_(G, rhs_c)`, `torch.mm(G.t(), lhs_one, ...)`), but G comes from an in-place Triton write autograd does not track, so under `create_graph=True` the first gradient requires grad while its graph omits every dependence through G. An executed interpreter probe (float64, S=3, V=7, 2K=4) matched first-order gradients but gave a sum of |d(sum dCE/dlhs)/dlhs| of 134.23 for the fused Function against 0.6156 for the autograd reference, with no error raised; the expected-likelihood Function returns a first gradient that does not require grad and so fails loudly. Nothing in `vfe3` differentiates the decode CE twice today (`create_graph` appears only at `vfe3/model/edge_links.py:766` on an edge energy, and there is no `torch.func` or hessian use), so only a future second-order use of the live `decode_ce_triton` route would hit it. The fix is to decorate `KLDecodeCE.backward`, and `ExpectedLikelihoodCE.backward` for consistency, with `torch.autograd.function.once_differentiable`; a double backward through `kl_decode_ce_rows` matching the reference, or a PyTorch version that raises on this pattern itself, would falsify it. Reported by gap-live-decode-triton-kernels; new.

### 50. fisher_group completion still damps Fisher-visible rotations; no rotation weight recovers the belief-Fisher natural gradient on its support (low)

`completion_coefficients` (`vfe3/geometry/phi_fisher.py:64-66`, used at `69-80`) returns (1 + λ/2, 1 - λ/2), so M(X) = C^{-1}X(c1 C + ξξ^T) + c2 X^T adds λ||asym Z||² on every antisymmetric Z rather than only on the stabilizer {asym Z : Zη = 0}. The knob has since been relabeled (`config.py:760`: completion weight lambda on every rotation, raising Fisher-visible rotation metrics a -> a + 2 lambda; `train_vfe3.py:276`), but the kernel-only completion or pseudo-inverse route proposed earlier does not exist. Executed: the λ=0 operator reproduces the pullback Fisher (2KL/t² goes 27.1518, 27.1618 toward the formula value 27.1630 as t goes from 1e-3 to 1e-4), so the uncompleted metric is right and only the completion choice is at issue. It is opt-in under `m_phi_update_mode='fisher_group'`. The fix is a completion mode that penalizes only P_perp asym(Z) P_perp (the projection in `stabilizer_leak`), so the solve equals the belief-Fisher natural gradient on the Fisher-visible subspace; a `rotation_weight` for which `fisher_type_solve`'s Fisher-visible component matches the pseudo-inverse solution to about 1e-6 with a bounded kernel component would falsify it. Reported by info-geometry; previously reported as `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-07.md` item 34 (F24/F63), relabeled since without the kernel-only option.

### 51. With an enabled GradScaler and clipping off, train_step publishes no role norms, silently disabling the norm-spike monitor (low)

In `vfe3/train.py:947-955`, `974-985`, `1017-1043` and `1137-1144`, an enabled scaler makes `explicit_grad_check` False on a silent step, so `_flags=[]` and the fused transfer block that fills `set_norm_values` is skipped, and with clipping off the clip block that would compute them never runs. An executed probe (`tiny_config`, CPU GradScaler `init_scale` 1024) gave role_grad_norms `{'mu': 0.4276, 'sigma': 0.0, 'phi': 0.004}` with clip=None and scaler off, `{}` with clip=None and scaler on, and the full dict with clip=1.0 and scaler on. `_DivergenceMonitor.observe` then sees no norms, leaving the norm-spike test and the `grad_norm_*_interval_*` columns blank, and a loss-triggered `_spike_attribution` reads gradients that were never unscaled (`need_unscale` is False), so its norms carry the loss scale; the `train_step` docstring says `status_out` computes the set norms even with clipping off. This is opt-in (`amp_dtype='fp16'` with `grad_clip` None or 0); the active config is fp32 with clip 1.0. The fix is, when `status_out` is requested under an enabled scaler, to call `unscale_` (or divide the set norms by the scale); non-empty, unscaled role norms under an enabled scaler with `grad_clip=None` would falsify it. Reported by training; new.

### 52. guard_sigma_floor_frac and guard_sigma_ceil_frac test guards inactive on the moment-matched route (low)

The diagnostics at `vfe3/model/model.py:1795-1799` call `guard_saturation(..., eps=cfg.eps, sigma_max=cfg.sigma_max)` (`vfe3/metrics.py:865-924`), but on the active launcher config `inactive_fields()` reports `sigma_max=100.0` as inactive ("no sigma E-step runs on projected routes..."; executed). The ceiling column therefore counts belief variances that happen to fall within 1e-3 relative of 100, and the floor column tests eps=1e-6 while this route's floors are the additive f*Sigma_p (f=1e-4, sigma_init=5) and the LayerNorm pushforward floor, so both read near zero by construction and say nothing about whether the route's real clamps bind (those are `covariance_ceiling_elements`, `layernorm_trace_cap_elements` and the kl_max fractions). This is on the active config (`projected_predictive`, `moment_matched`, `gaussian_diagonal`) and also feeds `val_guard_sigma_*` through `_VAL_GEOMETRY_KEYS`. The fix is to emit NaN for `guard_sigma_ceil_frac` when `sigma_max` is inactive and either drop the eps floor fraction on projected routes or point it at the route's own floor; `sigma_max` being read by an active clamp on this route would falsify the finding. Reported by training; new.

### 53. Width-transfer selection uses primary_val_ppl (best of periodic and final), not the final validation PPL its comments describe (low)

The `LR_WIDTH_TRANSFER` comment (`ablation.py:290`) says "the one with the lowest final validation PPL" and the fit docstring says "by final validation PPL", but the code at `ablation.py:4567-4569` reads `ppl = _as_float(row.get("primary_val_ppl"))`, which `run_single` defines (`ablation.py:2187`) as the min of any periodic best and the final validation PPL. A high-LR cell that is healthy at an early periodic eval and blows up late keeps a finite primary PPL, is scored as non-diverged and can win, defeating the stated purpose of screening instability. This was read, not executed. It matters for any `lr_width_transfer` run whose `eval_interval` is below `LR_WIDTH_TRANSFER['max_steps']`; the current `BASELINE_CONFIG` has `eval_interval=300000` against 1500 steps, so primary equals final today. The fix is to read the `final_val_ppl` column (already in the CSV schema at `ablation.py:2410`) and count a non-finite final as diverged; the finding is falsified if `primary_val_ppl` equals `final_val_ppl` for every cell whenever periodic evals ran. Reported by recent-diff; new.

### 54. Self-CE reference tokens get a full seq_len of context while generated tokens get only the prompt (low)

`held_out_token_ids` (`generate_text.py:328-369`) returns slices `stream[(i+1)*step - warmup:(i+1)*step + n_tokens]` with warmup = max(seq_len, baseline_seq_len), so every scored reference token has at least seq_len tokens of context, while `score_streams` (`generate_text.py:373-399`, `500-507`) scores generated tails with prefix = prompt ids ("The history of the city", about 5 tokens), so the first ~seq_len generated tokens see 5 to seq_len tokens of context. The docstring (`generate_text.py:23-25`) says the reference slices carry an equal window of preceding text. Because CE falls with context length, the generated-minus-reference delta is biased upward, toward the "not a preferred mode" reading. This was read and applies with `SELF_CE=True`. The fix is to give each reference draw a prefix as long as its prompt (warmup = len(prompt ids)) or to score both only from position seq_len onward; a measurement showing the model's per-token CE is flat in context length over 5 to 128 tokens would falsify it. Reported by experiments; new.

### 55. Frontier-collapse F-test counts identical runs once per nested route and essentially cannot reject (low)

`_collapse_identical_points` (`scaling_analysis.py:1516-1529`) deduplicates only the pooled fit, LOF and bootstrap, while `ancova_frontier_collapse` (`scaling_analysis.py:1288-1407`) receives `fit_param_points`, in which width K{k} equals width_depth K{k}_L6 and depth L{d} equals width_depth K160_L{d} (`scaling.py:410-421`). An executed simulation of 150 replicates of one shared offset law (5 widths x 4 depths, 3 seeds, sd 0.01) with the duplicated route set had df=(6,20) offset and (4,23) log-log, n_total 29 for 20 distinct runs, and rejected the null 0/150 for both forms, while a disjoint route set gave 13/150 (0.087) under the log-log form. This applies when `CONFIG['routes']` includes width, depth and width_depth together (the standard set); shipped scaling uses `['width']` only, where the test is not run. The fix is to run the collapse test on disjoint routes (assigning each run to one route) or to refuse it when routes share `effective_run_keys`; a simulation under a shared law where the duplicated design rejects near 0.05 would falsify it. Reported by experiments; new (audit-2026-10-08 reported the duplication for LOF and bootstrap, and its fix did not cover the frontier test).

### 56. parameter_optimization freezes kl_max at the baseline's 8K for every candidate (low)

`_resolved_model_config` (`parameter_optimization.py:496-507`) returns `asdict(VFE3Config(**config))`, and `__post_init__` writes kl_max = 8*embed_dim in place (`vfe3/config.py:1004-1005`), so the swapped-in `ablation.BASELINE_CONFIG` carries `kl_max=160.0` into every candidate (`parameter_optimization.py:510-521`, `661-679`). Executed with `block_norm_covariance` patched in memory to get past an earlier crash: `_candidate_config({'embed_dim':40,'n_heads':2})` gives kl_max 160.0 where 8K=320, `ablation._cell_cfg_dict` under `_ablation_baseline_scope` gives 160.0, and a plain ablation cell at K=128 gives 1024.0. kl_max is live on this baseline, and this contradicts ablation's documented rule that `kl_max=None` resolves to 8 * embed_dim in every cell. It is latent on the shipped catalog and reached by any search parameter or `requires` entry that changes `embed_dim`. The fix is to swap in the user's raw config dict, or reset resolved-default fields such as kl_max to None, instead of the asdict of the constructed config; a candidate with embed_dim=40 resolving kl_max=320 would falsify it. Reported by experiments; new.

### 57. Scaling exponent CI and lack-of-fit test treat init-only seed spread under a shared data order as full replicate error; docstring misstates non-embedding share (low)

The pooled pure-error s^2 that drives the offset-law bootstrap and the F(m-p, n-m) lack-of-fit test (`scaling_analysis.py:954-1020`, `1181-1262`) comes from seeds that share `data_seed=3` (`scaling.py:89`), so it omits data-order variance, and nothing in `scaling_summary.json` or `SCALING_ANALYSIS.md` says so, while `multiseed_analysis` prints its CAVEAT. Separately, the module docstring (`scaling_analysis.py:12-14`) says `n_non_embedding_params` is ~99% of `n_params`, whereas `run_artifacts.py:3543-3545` and `scaling_analysis.py:1626` state that the lookup tables are ~99%, so non-embedding parameters are ~1% (K=160, H=5: tables about V(2K+K^2/5) = 273M against a few million context parameters). A supporting executed simulation of the offset bootstrap with 5 sizes x 3 seeds found 80% coverage (20/25) when the true E sits on its 0 bound and a median CI width of 0.61 in alpha when E=3. This applies to the shipped scaling `CONFIG` with `data_seed=3`. The fix is to record data-order status in the summary, print the lower-bound caveat beside the CI and LOF p-value, and correct the docstring to ~1%; a scaling run with `data_seed='model'` whose fixed-size seed spread matches the shared-order spread would falsify it. Reported by experiments; new.

### 58. Protocol check compares nominal amp_dtype, so baselines and the Linear-decoder arm run bf16 while the base ran effective fp32 (low)

`BASELINE_PROTOCOL_FIELDS` includes `"amp_dtype": "amp_dtype"` (`paper_arms.py:470`), and `check_baseline_arm` (`paper_arms.py:675-680`) compares field values only. The 36.41 base records `amp_dtype='bf16'`, but its own `inactive_fields` reports "effective precision float32: no contraction on this route runs under autocast (only decode_logits_amp on the linear decode and use_cg_coupling consume amp_dtype)" (executed, `probe_reuse.py`); the completed baseline runs record `amp_dtype bf16` (`70.02_tf_matched_params_s6_d3/config.json`), and `BaselineTransformer._amp_context` wraps the blocks in `torch.autocast(dtype=torch.bfloat16)` (`vfe3/baseline.py:860-864`). The dry plan for the "Linear decoder" arm (`paper_arms.py:419-421`) notes that `amp_dtype='bf16'` and `decode_logits_amp=True` are inert on the base and live in this arm, so it changes decode precision as well as decoder family, yet the check reports both as matched; under compute matching, bf16 also gives the baseline higher calibrated throughput, and so more steps, than an fp32 baseline would get. This affects every baseline arm on the `k128_alibi` base (the current TIER_A launch) and the "Linear decoder" VFE arm. The fix is to compare effective precision (amp_dtype only where the matched config's `inactive_fields` does not mark it inert) and to declare or pin `decode_logits_amp=False` in the Linear-decoder arm. The finding is falsified if the VFE projected-predictive route on the base runs some contraction under bf16 autocast, or if the baseline's blocks run fp32 under `amp_dtype='bf16'`. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; new.

### 59. Retired train_window_offset is dropped silently, making fixed-window and per-epoch-random runs indistinguishable to reuse and fingerprints (low)

The defect spans `vfe3/config.py:2629-2632`, `paper_arms.py:1178-1180`, `multiseed_analysis.py:102-107` and `vfe3/paper/runs.py:296`. `_RUNTIME_POLICY_FIELDS = ("divergence_abort", "fill_uninitialized_memory", "train_window_offset", ...)` carries the comment "None changes the model, so any saved value is dropped silently". The window origin decides which training windows are seen, so it changes the training data even though it leaves the architecture alone. An executed probe (`probe_two.py`) loaded the 36.41 record through `vfe3.paper.runs.load_config` once with `train_window_offset='fixed'` and once with `'per_epoch_random'`. Both reported `strict dropped: {}`, and the serialized configs were identical (True). As a result `reuse_mismatch`, the multiseed `_semantic_config` (which records `retired:` keys only from the lenient path) and `check_homogeneity` all treat the two protocols as one. The older K128 bases' `config.json` files have no `train_window_offset` key, so those runs used fixed windows.

This is reachable in any comparison or aggregation that mixes runs saved before 2026-10-12 14:20 (fixed windows, for example the 32.65 and 34.48 bases and the `D:/DATA_KEEP/10-5-2026_Multiseed` group) with later runs. The proposed fix returns the dropped runtime-policy value in the `dropped` mapping on the strict path, or records the effective window mode in provenance, so that reuse and homogeneity compare it. The finding would be falsified if training windows were identical under the two offset modes, or if provenance already recorded the window mode and the comparators read it. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; new.

### 60. Among duplicate runs of one seed pair, the lexicographically first folder (prefixed by test PPL) is selected and the decision rule never sees duplicates (low)

At `paper_arms.py:1121-1127` and `paper_arms.py:1212-1216`, `complete_runs` returns matches from `sorted(arm_dir.iterdir())`, and `completed_run` returns the first accepted one. Run folders are named `<test PPL>_<slug>_s<seed>_d<data seed>`, so the choice depends on the result string and not on the run. An executed probe with three complete runs of (23, 23) produced the order `['100.20_evaluated_s23_d23_2', '35.90_evaluated_s23_d23_3', '36.80_evaluated_s23_d23']` and selected `100.20_...`. Only the selected run enters the manifest, which bypasses the duplicate-pair check in `vfe3/paper` (`tables.py:378-379`). It is reachable whenever an arm directory holds two complete runs of the same `(seed, data_seed)`, for example after a manual rerun or copy. The fix is to refuse, or report as stale, more than one complete run per seed pair, or to select by recorded completion time and never by name order. It would be falsified if run directories were unique per seed pair by construction or if sorting used a result-independent key. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; new.

### 61. A finished arm run with nonfinite test CE counts as never run, so a relaunch silently re-rolls that seed pair (low)

At `paper_arms.py:1111-1118` and `paper_arms.py:1579-1592`, `is_complete` requires `math.isfinite(test_ce)`, and an executed probe gives `is_complete(...) == False` for `{"test_ce": NaN}`. `run_plan` trains every pair whose `completed_run` is None, and neither `build_manifest` nor the stale list records the nonfinite attempt. A diverged replicate therefore disappears and is retrained, and on nondeterministic GPU kernels the retry can succeed. This is survivorship over seeds: the arm's paired statistics then include only replicates that did not diverge. It is reachable for any VFE or baseline arm replicate whose final evaluation is nonfinite, since `divergence_abort` no longer stops training. The fix is to classify a run that wrote `summary.json` with a nonfinite `test_ce` as a recorded failure that blocks retraining and is reported in the manifest row, and to the decision rule as a failed pair. It would be falsified if `run_plan` or `build_manifest` recorded such a run under some status, or if a relaunch skipped it. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; new.

### 62. Multi-seed digest never states whether seeds shared a data order; JSON status lumps per-seed orders with mixed designs (low)

At `multiseed_analysis.py:1008-1018` and `multiseed_analysis.py:1077-1120`, `_write_md` writes `test PPL = mean +/- sd` and the single-seed noise floor, then a fixed CAVEAT ("Data-order sharing is reported only for one explicit common data_seed; data_seed=None means ..."). It never writes `request_design['data_order']`, and it never says that under a shared order the SD is an init-plus-optimization lower bound. Only the noise-band figure caption (`vfe3/viz/figures.py:5757-5761`) and the JSON carry the status. Executed on the existing group `D:/DATA_KEEP/10-5-2026_Multiseed`, the status resolves to `data_order: {'status': 'shared_explicit', 'data_seed': 3}`, which the markdown does not mention. Separately, `_data_order_status` returns `'nonshared_or_mixed'` both for per-seed orders (`data_seed == seed`) and for a group in which some seeds share an order, whereas `vfe3/semantics/multiseed.py:288-320` separates `'distinct'` from `'partially_shared'`.

This affects every published multiseed digest, and since `train_vfe3.py` ships `DATA_SEED = 3`, every group shares one order. The fix writes the data-order status and its lower-bound sentence into `MULTISEED_ANALYSIS.md` beside the headline and splits `'nonshared_or_mixed'` into `'distinct'` and `'partially_shared'`. It would be falsified by a `MULTISEED_ANALYSIS.md` for a `DATA_SEED=3` group that contains the shared-order or lower-bound statement. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; new.

### 63. Five of six paper_arms BASES entries point at directories that no longer exist (low)

In `paper_arms.py:161-175`, `vfe3_runs/32.65_wikitext-103_K128_diag-chunked_exp` and `vfe3_runs/34.48_wikitext-103_K128_linear-exp` are absent and now live under `D:/DATA_KEEP/10-10-2026_experiments/`; `D:/DATA_KEEP/39.77_...` is now under `9-2026_Older_Experiments/`; `D:/DATA_KEEP/52.91_...` and `vfe3_runs/Full_Gaussian_mom-match_Template_routing/55.07_...` were not found (executed check). `load_base` reads `(run_dir / "config.json")` without a guard, so selecting any base other than `k128_alibi` in `CONFIG['bases']` crashes with `FileNotFoundError` before any plan is printed. The fix updates the paths to the moved locations or has `load_base` report a missing base as unavailable rather than raise. It would be falsified if the listed paths resolve on the user's machine, for example through a different mount. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; new.

### 64. Inference-bank decode callers never pass encoded_sigma, so decode_covariance='encoded' breaks the logit lens and disables the report's full-vocabulary figures (low)

Two investigators found the same omission at two call sites. In `vfe3/semantics/contextual.py:370-380` (used at `:627` and `:639`), `_readout` calls `model._decode_belief_with_context(mu, sigma, canonical_frame=canonical_frame)` without `encoded_sigma`, and in `vfe3/viz/extract.py:217-249`, `_decode_inference_record` makes the same call while the bank record stores no encoded covariance. `_decode_covariance` at `vfe3/model/model.py:853-855` then raises `ValueError("decode_covariance='encoded' needs the encoded covariance ...")`. The semantics investigator observed that `forward_beliefs` decodes with `encoded_sigma=beliefs.sigma` (`model.py:1125`), the same object stored as `diagnostic_capture["initial_belief"] = beliefs` (`model.py:1068`) and returned as `layers[0]` by `_forward_layers`. On a tiny model (V=24, K=4, 2 heads, 2 layers, `projected_predictive`, `diagonal_chunked`, untied), `C._readout(...)` raised the ValueError under `'encoded'`, and with `encoded_sigma=layers[0].sigma` passed explicitly the readout reproduced the forward logits with max|diff| = 0.0, as under `'belief'` and `'constant'`. Under `'encoded'`, `_attempt` records the whole logit_lens step as failed, which also drops the Σ-dynamics Spearman, the lens headline and the multiseed `contextual/lens_ce_*` metrics. The viz-contracts probe (`probe_enc.py`) gave "bank CE matches model(tokens): True" for belief and constant and "belief_ce_vocab_stats raised: ValueError ..." for encoded; in `generate_figures` (`vfe3/viz/report.py:917-965`) the `_safe` wrapper then logs a warning and sets `decode_stats`, `ce_bank` and `vstats` to None, which skips `vocab_probability_heatmap`, `vocab_calibration`, `vocab_confusion`, `sigma_stratified_error`, `sigma_ce_scatter` and the bank reliability diagram. Both probes were executed.

The defect is reachable whenever `decode_covariance='encoded'` is used with a prior-bank decoder; that value is documented as valid in the `train_vfe3.py` config dict and `docs/2026-10-15.md` ran it as a live setting, while the active config uses `'belief'` and is unaffected. The two investigators propose slightly different fixes: semantics would pass `encoded_sigma=layers[0].sigma` from the `_forward_layers` output through `_readout`, and viz-contracts would store the stack-input sigma (`forward_beliefs`' `_encoded_sigma_out`) in each inference-bank record and pass it in both `_decode_inference_record` and `semantics._readout`. The finding would be falsified by a case where `initial_belief.sigma` differs from the `beliefs.sigma` passed as `encoded_sigma` (for example a reassignment between `model.py:1068` and `:1125`), or by `belief_ce_vocab_stats` returning CE equal to `model(tokens)` under `'encoded'`. Reported by semantics and viz-contracts; `docs/2026-10-11.md:124` records that analysis code "cannot supply the encoded covariance and refuses 'encoded'", and the new evidence is that the capture does carry it and supplying it reproduces the forward logits exactly (viz-contracts lists it as new).

### 65. Calibration text audit reports same_text=True even when a segment failed its in-order match (low)

In `vfe3/posthoc/calibration.py:201-216`, `same = matched == word_chars and unmatched_bpe == 0` (line 216) ignores `mismatched_segments`. A segment rejected by the wildcard window is re-matched piecewise from `cursor` rather than `cursor+pending` (lines 206-208), and its characters still count as matched. Executed: `text_audit('The cat', ' The <unk> cat\n')` returned `same_text` True with matched 6 of 6 and `unmatched_bpe` 0, although the `<unk>` word has no counterpart in the BPE text; W then counts a word the BPE stream lacks, and `calibration_for_run` would still mark the row `'ok'` (line 327). This is reachable through `posthoc_eval.py` with `calibration=True` (the default) on any WikiText run with a complete test pass. The fix requires `mismatched_segments == 0` and zero unmatched wildcards for `same_text`; the finding is falsified if `text_audit` on that pair returns `same_text` False. Reported by posthoc-paper; new.

### 66. Row 5 'stored covariances -> identity' leaves the untied decode bank's trained covariances in place (low)

In `vfe3/posthoc/interventions.py:250-257`, `stored_covariances_identity` zeroes only `prior_bank.sigma_log_embed`, but with `untie_decode_bank` the Gaussian decode reads its own `decode_sigma_log_embed` (`vfe3/model/prior_bank.py:997-998`, see also `903-909`), which stays trained. The manuscript row reads "Stored covariances Sigma_p^c replaced by the identity" (`GL(K)_attention_v2.tex:1828`), written for the linear decoder that has no decode covariance, so on the active route (`untie_decode_bank=True`, `decode_mode='diagonal_chunked'`) the row measures the encode-side table only; its record names the table, but the label does not. This was established by reading. The fix either also swaps `decode_sigma_log_embed` (or splits encode-side and decode-side rows) or labels the row "encode-side stored covariances" whenever the decode bank is untied. It would be falsified if the `diagonal_chunked` decode with `untie_decode_bank=True` did not read `decode_sigma_log_embed`. Reported by posthoc-paper; new.

### 67. M2 and the decision-rule output do not surface a shared data order or config violations in the rendered table (low)

At `vfe3/paper/tables.py:278-298, 316-320, 576-585` and `vfe3/paper/runs.py:560-574`, `check_homogeneity` computes `data_seed_varied` (`runs.py:573`), but neither `main_results_table` nor `decision_rule` reads it. The caption says "mean ± SD (ddof = 1) over seeds" (`tables.py:316`) whether or not the data order varied; with one shared order that SD is an init-plus-optimization lower bound, and the paired SE inside the margin `max(2SE, 0.03)` shrinks with it. Config violations reach the LaTeX only as a `%` comment above the row (`tables.py:291-293`), invisible in the compiled table, whereas `multiseed_analysis` refuses or captions such panels. This was read, not executed. It is reachable for an arm built from `train_vfe3.py` multi-seed launches (`DATA_SEED=3`, `train_vfe3.py:88`); `paper_arms.py` varies the data seed with the model seed and is unaffected. The fix adds a visible marker (a dagger and the caption clause "one shared data order") to the M2 row and the decision clause when `data_seed_varied` is False, and renders homogeneity violations visibly. It would be falsified by an M2 row from single-data-seed runs carrying a visible shared-order caveat in the LaTeX output. Reported by posthoc-paper; new (the shared-order caveat itself is documented at `train_vfe3.py:83-88`).

### 68. Reproduction check labels an fp32 'highest' replay of a TF32 'high' run as matched arithmetic with the tight tolerance (low)

At `vfe3/posthoc/report.py:721`, `matched = loaded.amp_dtype == cfg.amp_dtype and (cfg.amp_dtype is None or device.type == 'cuda')` ignores the matmul policy, while `load_model` replaces `'high'` with `'highest'` under `precision='fp32'` (`vfe3/posthoc/evaluate.py:109`). The check then applies `REPRODUCTION_TOL_MATCHED=1e-3` and the arithmetic label "matched" although the replay changed TF32 to IEEE fp32; the change appears in `report['notes']` but not in the check. This was read. It is reachable with `posthoc_eval.py precision='fp32'` on CUDA for a run trained with `fp32_matmul_precision='high'` (the active driver value) and `amp_dtype=None`. The fix includes `fp32_matmul_precision` equality in `matched`, so a changed policy gets the fp32-replay tolerance and label; it would be falsified by a replay at a different matmul policy recorded with arithmetic "fp32 replay". Reported by posthoc-paper; new.

### 69. M5 table and forest plot hide aligned-subset scoring and hardcode the bootstrap unit (low)

At `vfe3/posthoc/report.py:904-909, 409-426` and `vfe3/posthoc/figures.py:330`, when an arm scores a different target set the row CE is `arm_nll.mean()` over the aligned subset while the baseline row shows `base.ce` over all targets; `n_targets_dropped` is stored but not printed in `interventions_table`, so CE_arm minus CE_base need not equal the printed ΔCE. `plot_interventions` also labels the axis "95% article-bootstrap interval" even when the unit is `block_4096` (`streams.py:436-439`). This was read. It is reachable for full-covariance decodes, which can exclude degenerate positions per arm, and for non-WikiText datasets that fall back to 4096-token blocks; on the active diagonal decode `decode_degenerate_positions` returns None and no targets are dropped. The fix prints `n_targets_dropped` (or the aligned-subset baseline CE) and passes the resampling unit into the plot label; it would be falsified by a table showing the aligned baseline CE whenever targets are dropped. Reported by posthoc-paper; new.

### 70. Registered Gaussian decoder expected_likelihood_chunked is classified 'unsupported' by a hard-coded name set, so its decode bank is never analyzed (low)

At `vfe3/semantics/run.py:59` and `vfe3/semantics/run.py:467-472`, `_KL_DECODE_MODES = frozenset(("diagonal", "diagonal_chunked", "full", "full_chunked"))`, and `_decode_kind` returns `"unsupported"` for anything else; `'diagonal'` and `'full'` are no longer registered decode names (the registry holds `diagonal_chunked`, `full_chunked`, `expected_likelihood_chunked`, `linear`). For `expected_likelihood_chunked`, `decode_mu`, `decode_log_var` and `tau_eff` raise through `_require_decode("gaussian")`, so the `decode_mu` view, the Σ-section decode-bank battery and the next-token CKA are skipped, although the bank is the same `(decode_mu_embed, decode_sigma_log_embed)` Gaussian table; only `decode_natural_causal`, linear in λ(q) for the KL decode alone, truly does not apply. This was read. It is reachable for any run with `decode_mode='expected_likelihood_chunked'` (registered at `prior_bank.py:2436` and listed as valid in the `train_vfe3.py` config comment), not the active `diagonal_chunked` config. The fix derives the decode kind from the decode registration, or adds a `'gaussian_bank'` kind that enables `decode_mu` and the decode-bank battery and skips only the natural-parameter view, and drops the retired names. It would be falsified by a loaded `expected_likelihood_chunked` run whose `decode_kind` is `'gaussian'` or whose sigma metrics contain `banks.decode`. Reported by semantics; new.

### 71. Semantic significance counts and marks across many relations, pairs and scorers carry no multiplicity control (low)

The locations are `vfe3/semantics/frames.py:1882-1885`, `vfe3/semantics/report.py:1328-1331`, `vfe3/semantics/vectors.py:937-947`, `vfe3/semantics/vectors.py:2236` and `vfe3/semantics/gaussians.py:1050`. The phi headline counts `sum(1 for v in ok_rel if v['p'] is not None and v['p'] < 0.01)} / {len(ok_rel)}` over about 10 relations; concept directions use `significant = np.abs(np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0)) >= DIRECTION_Z` (|z| >= 2, about p = 0.046 two-sided) on all R(R-1)/2 pairs and report `n_significant_pairs`; the offsets heatmap marks `P < OFFSET_ALPHA` (0.05) for every relation and view; and the Gaussian-similarity figure fills a marker when the unadjusted paired Δρ CI excludes 0 across 8 scorers and 2 benchmarks. A grep of `vfe3/semantics` finds no Holm, Bonferroni, BH or FDR, and with 45 direction pairs at |z| >= 2 about 2 are expected to be marked under the null. This was read, and it applies to every full analysis on the active config. The fix reports Holm- or BH-adjusted p-values or counts next to the raw ones per family (relations within a view, direction pairs within a view, scorers within a benchmark). It would be falsified by downstream text or figures that already state the per-family error rate, or by a family small enough that adjustment changes no mark. Reported by semantics; new.

### 72. vocab_for_run ignores the cache_dir override and reads the vocabulary sidecar from the default cache (low)

At `vfe3/semantics/run.py:546` and `vfe3/semantics/vocab.py:221-251`, `load_semantic_run` accepts `cache_dir` and uses it for counts and token streams (`load_cached_tokens(dataset, "train", cache_dir=cache_dir)`) but calls `vocab_for_run(dataset, selection_identity, V)`, which has no `cache_dir` parameter and reads `WordVocabulary.load(vocabulary_path(dataset, tag="word"))`, falling back to `default_cache_dir()`. The token ids are then decoded with a sidecar from a different cache, giving either `FileNotFoundError` or a different table that only has to pass the vocabulary-size check. This was read. It is reachable through `load_semantic_run(..., cache_dir=<non-default>)` on a word-level dataset (`wikitext-103-word` or `-word-eos`) without an explicit vocab; BPE runs are unaffected. The fix threads `cache_dir` into `vocab_for_run` and `vocabulary_path(dataset, tag="word", cache_dir=cache_dir)`; it would be falsified by a call path where a word dataset's vocab is built with the same `cache_dir` as its token streams. Reported by semantics; new.

### 73. frames and gaussians share one RNG stream across sub-steps, so budgeting one step reshuffles later steps' draws (low)

In `vfe3/semantics/frames.py:1712` and `:1753-1776`, `rng = np.random.default_rng([int(ctx.seed), 0x70E1])` is consumed in order by `commutator_diagnostics` (`_random_pairs(ids.size, n_pairs, rng)`), `_canonical_commutators`, `group_analogies` (`gen.choice(Q, size=int(max_rerank_questions))` picks the rerank subset) and `relation_transports` (derangements and `_cap`), so changing `frame_commutator_pairs`, or an earlier step failing, changes the rerank subset and the transport nulls. In `vfe3/semantics/gaussians.py:1336`, one `rng = np.random.default_rng(ctx.seed)` feeds the frequency Spearman, the entailment cluster bootstrap and the similarity bootstraps of the prior bank and then the decode bank. `vectors.py:91-94` avoids this with per-step `_STEP_STREAM` ids ("so each stochastic step draws the same numbers whichever others ran"). This was read; it touches every phi and sigma section and matters when comparing analyses with different options (multiseed `option_differences`, quick versus full presets). The fix gives each sub-step its own stream, `np.random.default_rng([seed, section_id, step_id])`, as vectors and contextual do.
