It would be falsified by two phi runs that differ only in `frame_commutator_pairs` yet select the same rerank subset and transport null draws. Reported by semantics; new.

### 74. REPORT.md gauge paragraph says holonomy is identically the identity without the content-link qualification (low)

At `vfe3/semantics/report.py:1135-1137`, `if run.frames_active: extra.append("with the flat transport Ω_ij = U_i U_j⁻¹ telescopes, so holonomy is identically the identity and is not reported")` remains unqualified. `frames.py:1738-1742` was narrowed to "its loop holonomy" and adds a content-link clause only when `content_link_edges != 'off'`, while the 2026-10-08 finding noted that on the projected routes the content links L_ij have nonzero triangle holonomy (`posthoc/holonomy.py`) even with content links off. This was read; it affects every REPORT.md with frames active, including the active `projected_predictive` config. The fix restricts the sentence to the frame cocycle Ω ("the frame transport's loop holonomy is the identity") and points to `posthoc/holonomy.py` for content-link holonomy on projected routes. It would be falsified if `posthoc/holonomy.py` reported identity triangle holonomy for the content links of an active-config checkpoint. Reported by semantics; previously reported as `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-08.md` gauge-theorist#6 (fixed in `frames.py` only).

### 75. Valence-axis projections score two axis-defining words in-sample (low)

In `vfe3/semantics/resources/relations.json` (`axes.valence`) and `vfe3/semantics/vectors.py:1126-1146`, the valence pairs include `['hate','love']` and the words list starts with `['love','hate', ...]`; an executed probe of the resource file reports `valence ... overlap ['hate', 'love']`. `semantic_projection` builds `u = diff.mean(axis=0)` from all pairs, including hate to love, and scores `Xp[wrows] @ u`, so the scores of "love" and "hate" are in-sample. The other five axes have no overlap. This affects the semantic-projection tables and figures for every view. The fix removes the two words from the valence list or scores pole words on the leave-one-pair-out axis; it would be falsified if no axis shared a word between its pairs and its words. Reported by semantics; new.

### 76. phi notes and summary claim the barycenter uses the 'most frequent' frames even when counts are unavailable (low)

At `vfe3/semantics/frames.py:1713-1714, 1722-1726`, `vfe3/semantics/run.py:655-660` and `vfe3/semantics/report.py:1300-1302`, `ref_ids = ids[:n_ref]` and `_candidate_order` returns `np.sort(ids)` when counts is None, so the reference set is the lowest token ids, yet the note reads `f"m = bi-invariant barycenter of the {n_ref} most frequent candidate frames"` and `_phi_summary` says "the barycenter over the N most frequent frames". This was read. It is reachable only for runs without a unigram table and without a cached training stream (`counts_source='unavailable'`), not the active config, whose `decode_unigram_prior=True` provides counts. The fix uses "the first N candidates (id order; counts unavailable)" when `ctx.counts` is None; it would be falsified if `_candidate_order` returned a frequency order with counts None. Reported by semantics; new.

### 77. Divergence-monitor state (EMAs, warmup counts, cooldown, accumulators) is not checkpointed, so spike detection is not resume-exact (low)

`train()` always builds a fresh `divergence = _DivergenceMonitor()` (`vfe3/train.py:4414`, class at `vfe3/train.py:4038-4141`, use at `4997-5029`), whose `__init__` sets `_loss_ema=None`, `_loss_seen=0`, `_norm_ema={}`, `_norm_seen={}`, `_last_spike=None` and calls `reset_interval()`. The `save_checkpoint` payload (`vfe3/run_artifacts.py:2252-2273`; keys step, model_state, optimizer_state, ..., data_state, numerics_counters, wall_clock_s) has no monitor entry and `load_checkpoint` restores none. With `DIVERGENCE_EMA_WARMUP=50` (`train.py:3871`), no spike can be detected for 50 steps after a resume, the cooldown restarts, and the EMA (rho = 0.98) differs for roughly another 50 steps. The training investigator noted that the weights still match bitwise (a separate probe gave max |straight - resumed| = 0.0 under the fisher route). The gap-checkpoint-resume-exactness investigator executed `probe_divergence.py` on the live route at tiny scale (K=8, N=4, B=2, L=2, V=7) for 12 steps with a checkpoint at 6 and lowered thresholds (`DIVERGENCE_EMA_WARMUP=1`, `DIVERGENCE_LOSS_SPIKE_MARGIN=0.0`, `DIVERGENCE_SPIKE_COOLDOWN=1`): losses after the resume were identical, but spike steps after step 6 were [8, 12] continuous and [8, 9, 12] resumed, `interval_steps` was 8.0 versus 2.0 at step 8 and 12.0 versus 6.0 at step 12, and the resumed run wrote a forced row at step 9. Under the live cadence (`checkpoint_interval=60000`, a multiple of `log_interval=1200`) the interval columns still agree because the checkpoint step is a log row, but EMA, warmup and cooldown differ regardless. The neighboring host counters `skipped_steps` and `consecutive_skips` (`train.py:4410-4411`) also restart, so the "%d of %d total so far" warning at `train.py:4782-4784` pairs a continuation-only skip count with the absolute step.

This is reachable on every resume of a persisted run on the active config, including the `train_vfe3.py` launcher; parameters, optimizer, RNG and data are unaffected, and only warnings, `divergence_spikes.jsonl`, forced metrics rows and the `interval_*` and `divergence_spike` columns change. The fix serializes the monitor's state (`_loss_ema`, `_loss_seen`, `_norm_ema`, `_norm_seen`, `_last_spike`, `steps`, the `_RunningStat` accumulators, `_clipped`, `_binding`) as a tolerant diagnostic checkpoint entry, like `numerics_counters`, and restores it in `train()` after `load_checkpoint`. It would be falsified by a resumed run flagging the same spike steps within the first 50 post-resume steps, by `probe_divergence.py` giving equal spike lists and `interval_steps` values, or by a checkpoint key that carries the monitor state. Reported by training and gap-checkpoint-resume-exactness; new.

### 78. Code identity counts the launcher toggle fully_gauge_covariant as executable code, so flipping it discards best-model selection on resume (low)

At `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:379`, `_DECLARATIVE_DRIVER_NAME = re.compile(r"^(?:config|[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*)$")` decides which top-level assignments `_driver_logic_bytes` (`run_artifacts.py:382-415`, `403-415`) strips before hashing root drivers into `code_identity_sha256` via `_package_code_identity`. `fully_gauge_covariant = False` (`train_vfe3.py:101`, `ablation.py:314`) does not match, and an AST scan of all nine root drivers found it is the only lowercase click-to-run setting. ablation.py's own runner hash lists it as declarative (`_DECLARATIVE_ABLATION_NAMES = {"BASELINE_CONFIG", "fully_gauge_covariant", ...}`, `ablation.py:132-143`), and `docs/2026-10-14.md:11` states that flipping it does not change the runner-source hash, so the two declarative rules disagree. Executed probes: flipping only that line changes `_driver_logic_bytes` for `train_vfe3.py` (True) and for `ablation.py` (True), an edit of `DATA_SEED = 3` to 4 does not (False), and ablation's runner identity does not change on the same flip (False). A changed `code_identity_sha256` makes `_validate_best_model_mapping` (`run_artifacts.py:437-466`, consumer at `1191-1197`) raise `_BestModelContractMismatch("... does not match the active executable-code identity")`, so `_preflight_best_selection` returns None and selection restarts at inf/None with the "model selection restarts" warning.

It is reachable on every `train_vfe3` and ablation run after such an edit, even when the config is already fully covariant and the overrides are empty. The data-io investigator adds that provenance mislabels the run as drifted or `mixed_generation`, and that under `STRICT_CODE_IDENTITY=True` the live kernel refuses new runs and finished ablation cohorts lose reuse. The fix treats `fully_gauge_covariant` as declarative in `_is_declarative_driver_statement`, preferably through one explicit name set shared with `ablation._DECLARATIVE_ABLATION_NAMES` so the filters cannot drift apart. It would be falsified if `_driver_logic_bytes` returned identical bytes for both values of the toggle, or if a resume after the flip kept `best_val_ppl` and `best_step` without the warning. Reported by data-io and gap-checkpoint-resume-exactness; data-io lists it as new (the filter came from audit 2026-10-08 P2-2), and gap-checkpoint-resume-exactness relates it to audit-2026-10-08 P2-2 / general-B-training-config#2, whose fix did not cover this lowercase setting.

### 79. A train stream with fewer windows than batch_size makes train() die with a bare StopIteration (low)

`make_dataloader` accepts any stream with T >= L+1 (`usable = self.tokens.numel() - seq_len - 1; if usable < 0: raise`, `vfe3/data/datasets.py:794-797`) and builds a `drop_last=True` train loader, while `train()` checks only `if steps_per_epoch < 0: raise` (`vfe3/train.py:4259`). The step loop (`vfe3/train.py:4622-4634`) then does `except StopIteration: epoch += 1 ... it = iter(loader); tokens, targets = next(it)`, and the second `next()` raises StopIteration out of `train()`. An executed probe with `tiny_config(batch_size=4, warmup_steps=0)` on a 12-token stream at N=3 gave 3 windows, `len(loader)=0`, and `StopIteration ''` with no message; a second probe with per-epoch origins at N=128, B=32 found that every T in [129, 4223] yields 0 batches per epoch, and the banner (`coverage_lines`) prints `epochs=nan` without stopping the run. This is reachable through the documented `train_vfe3.py` `MAX_TOKENS` smoke-run toggle (`train_vfe3.py:428-436`) in [129, 4223] at the live N=128, B=32, and through ablation and scaling `max_tokens` or any library caller with a short stream. The fix raises a ValueError in `train()` when a sized loader has `len(loader) == 0`, stating the minimum stream length (about B*N + N tokens with per-epoch origins), possibly also in `make_dataloader` when `drop_last` and `len(ds) < batch_size`. It would be falsified by a run with `MAX_TOKENS=4000` at N=128, B=32 that trains or stops before step 0 with a descriptive error. Reported by data-io; new (related to a different, fixed short-stream item in `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12-round2.md` item 16 / line 172).

### 80. Word-cache builder writes neither the vocabulary digest nor the provenance manifest the loader checks (low)

The loader binds a word cache to its id order only `if "vocabulary_sha256" in meta` (`vfe3/data/datasets.py:432-434`), but the builder's meta dict (`build_wikitext103_word_cache.py:258-283`: schema_version, dataset_id, ..., vocab_size, n_tokens, n_chars_source, tokens_per_char, tokenization_code_sha256, tokenizer_config_sha256, optional eos) has no such key. Grep shows the only writer of the key is a test fixture (`tests/test_audit_20261008_config_train.py:243`), and the on-disk `~/.cache/tokenized_cache/wikitext-103-word-eos_validation_word_tokens.pt.meta.json` lacks it. Likewise `_cache_tokenizer_provenance` (`datasets.py:193-235`) verifies `<cache>.provenance.json`, which nothing in the repo writes; executed, `cache_source_identity('wikitext-103','validation')` returns `tokenizer_provenance_status 'filename_inferred_unverified'`, and the finished 36.41 run's `selection_data_identity` carries the same status. The vocabulary check matters only for the word datasets after an interrupted FORCE rebuild with a reordered table, while the manifest status affects every cache including the live wikitext-103 BPE cache. The fix adds `"vocabulary_sha256": word_vocabulary_sha256(vocabulary.words)` to each split's meta and publishes a `<cache>.provenance.json` with the seven fields `_cache_tokenizer_provenance` requires after the sidecar is saved. It would be falsified by a freshly built word cache whose meta contains `vocabulary_sha256`, or by any repo code path that writes a `.provenance.json`. Reported by data-io; previously reported in `docs/2026-10-08.md:50`, `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-08.md` gap-data-lifecycle#6 and `docs/audits/tamper-sweep-2026-09-27.md:72`.

### 81. Terminal checkpoint never carries a current wall clock, so a resume loses or undercounts pre-resume wall time (low)

`artifacts.wall_clock_state` has one writer, `artifacts.wall_clock_state = timer.elapsed_wall_clock_s() if timer is not None else None` (`vfe3/train.py:5023`), inside the periodic-checkpoint branch. Before the terminal callback, `train()` refreshes only `artifacts.numerics_counters_state` (`train.py:5311-5316`), and `finalize_validation_run` then calls `artifacts.save_checkpoint(...)` (`vfe3/run_artifacts.py:4879-4883`), which writes `"wall_clock_s": getattr(self, "wall_clock_state", None)` (`run_artifacts.py:2272`). The terminal checkpoint is therefore None under every sweep, where periodic checkpoints are forced off and the timer is usually None, and stale if a caller combines periodic checkpoints with a terminal callback; `load_checkpoint` maps that to `resumed_wall_clock_s=None`, and `_cumulative_wall_time` (`run_artifacts.py:3915-3928`) reports only the continuation. This was read. It is reachable for terminal-callback runs (`ablation.run_single`, scaling cells, which force `checkpoint_interval=0` at `ablation.py:1956` and `scaling.py:714`) when a user later resumes from their terminal `step_N.pt`; the `train_vfe3.py` launcher is unaffected. The fix sets the wall clock next to `numerics_counters_state` before the terminal callback, measured from a `train()`-start `perf_counter` plus the resumed offset. It would be falsified by a terminal `step_N.pt` from an ablation cell whose `wall_clock_s` is finite and equal to the cell's training wall time. Reported by gap-checkpoint-resume-exactness; related to audit-2026-10-10 item 51, whose fix covers periodic checkpoints only.

### 82. readout_diagnostics.png can never draw its canonical-F panels (wrong columns passed, missing 'total' key) (low)

At `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:5386-5406`, `readout_history = _hist_subset(readout_keys + ("attn_entropy", "attn_entropy_min", "effective_rank", "belief_cond_median", "belief_cond_median_layer_max"))` with `readout_keys` the keys starting with `relational_`, while the panels in `vfe3/viz/figures.py:2775-2786` read `total`, `canonical_total_output`, `self_coupling`, `self_divergence`, `belief_coupling`, `attention_entropy`, `canonical_beta_tv` and `canonical_self_weight`, and the CSV writes `d['total']` as `inner_alignment_energy_total`. In executed probe run2 with `diagnose_canonical_free_energy=True`, `canonical_beta_tv` [0.532, 0.669, 0.601, 0.561] and `self_coupling` were finite, yet a spy on `_history_dashboard` showed the "Routing and readout diagnostics" live panels without "Canonical F at the captured beliefs (diagnostic)" or "Model router against the canonical router". The per-layer figure, whose dict carries `total`, is unaffected. The panels are dead on every route under `diagnose_canonical_free_energy=True` (default off). The fix adds the missing columns and `CANONICAL_DIAGNOSTIC_KEYS` to the readout subset and maps `total` to `inner_alignment_energy_total` on the history dashboard; it would be falsified by `readout_diagnostics.png` showing the canonical panels on such a run. Reported by viz-contracts; new.

### 83. Canonical free-energy figures correlate training-batch F with held-out val_ce; held-out F, phi and vertex columns are never plotted (low)

At `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:5502-5528` with `vfe3/viz/figures.py:1710-1762`, `fe_keys = (self_coupling, belief_coupling, attention_entropy, val_ce)`; the first three are train-batch diagnostics from the log step and `val_ce` is the validation split, so the Pearson r(F, CE) in the title pairs two populations. `_val_diagnostics` (`vfe3/train.py:3405-3410`) already writes `val_self_coupling`, `val_belief_coupling`, `val_attention_entropy` and `val_inner_alignment_energy_total` on the same eval rows; executed run2 gave `val_self_coupling` ['', 2.268, '', 2.211] against `self_coupling` [2.378, 2.601, 2.294, 2.265]. Separately, `_save_figures` (`run_artifacts.py:5559-5561`) adds `val_phi_matrix_norm_p95/p99/max` and `val_vertex_cond_p99` to the validation-sanity subset, but grep shows no figure reads them (`figures.py:2975-3003`). The first part needs `diagnose_canonical_free_energy=True`; the unplotted held-out geometry columns are on the active route. The fix builds the canonical figures from the `val_*` F columns or states the train-batch population in the title, and adds held-out chart-radius and vertex panels or stops selecting those columns. It would be falsified by the canonical figure reading the `val_*` F columns or by a panel series referencing `val_phi_matrix_norm_*`. Reported by viz-contracts; new.

### 84. Report-mode worker runs strict generate_figures before the lenient history re-render, so the lenient loader never helps old runs (low)

In `vfe3/viz/figure_worker.py:590-633`, mode `'report'` makes `main()` call `generate_figures(run_dir, ...)` first, which calls `_load_config(run_dir)`, the strict `config_from_serialized` (`vfe3/viz/report.py:783`), and only afterwards `_render_persisted_run_figures(run_dir, _load_worker_config(run_dir), None, logger)`. A strict failure propagates out of `main()`, so the history dashboards are never rebuilt; finalize mode has the opposite order but always runs on the current build. An executed probe on a copy of run1's `config.json` with `lambda_alpha_mode='constant'` gave `_load_worker_config -> SimpleNamespace` but `load_run_config -> ValueError serialized config from ...` (`vfe3/viz/run_loading.py:17-29`). This is reachable through `make_figures.py` in report mode on any run that saved a value this build refuses. The fix renders the persisted history and probe figures first, or inside a try that survives a `generate_figures` failure, as finalize mode does; it would be falsified by report mode producing `metrics.csv` dashboards for a run whose config fails `config_from_serialized`. Reported by viz-contracts; previously reported as audit-2026-10-12-round2 item 10 (fix incomplete for the on-demand path).

### 85. plot_scaling_law prints R^2 as testable for the offset law at 4 sizes, contradicting metric_contracts' 5-size rule (low)

At `vfe3/viz/figures.py:4757-4762`, `testable = int(fit["n_distinct_sizes"]) >= SCALING_MIN_TESTABLE_SIZES` (4) regardless of `with_offset`, while `vfe3/metric_contracts.py:11-19` sets `SCALING_MIN_TESTABLE_SIZES_OFFSET = 5` because 4 sizes leave the 3-parameter law one residual degree of freedom, and the analyzer itself uses `scaling_min_testable_sizes(True)` (`scaling_analysis.py:1943-1945`). An executed probe with 4 sizes, 3 seeds and `with_offset=True` produced the legend "fit $\alpha$=0.253, $R^2$=0.992, E=2.973, offset power law" while `scaling_min_testable_sizes(True)` = 5. This is reachable with the live `scaling_analysis` `CONFIG['with_offset']=True` (`scaling_analysis.py:100`) and exactly 4 distinct sizes. The fix uses `scaling_min_testable_sizes(fit['form'] == 'offset_power_law')` for the gate and its "not testable" label; it would be falsified by the legend reading "not testable" for an offset fit at 4 sizes. Reported by viz-contracts; new.

### 86. Latent producer/consumer contract lies: lr_grid baseline None crashes plot, bank copies drop block_diagonal, summary.json writes null estep_final_* keys (low)

Three latent contract mismatches were executed by probe. First, `lr_grid_heatmap_kwargs(..., baseline: Tuple[float, float])` (`vfe3/viz/sweep_adapters.py:354-395`) receives `tuple(meta["grid_baseline"]) if meta.get("grid_baseline") else None` from `ablation.py:5283` and always stores `grid['baseline']`, while `plot_lr_grid_heatmap` (`vfe3/viz/figures.py:4441-4443`) does `if "baseline" in grid: bx, by = grid["baseline"]`; the probe printed "baseline key present: True None" then "TypeError cannot unpack non-iterable NoneType object". This needs a grid sweep declared without `grid_baseline`, which none is today. Second, `_cpu_bank_value` (`vfe3/viz/extract.py:55-59`) rebuilds `CanonicalFrameContext(forward=..., inverse=...)` without `block_diagonal`; with `decode_coordinates='reference'` the live capture was (2, 8, 2, 2, 2) with `block_diagonal=True` and the bank copy had `block_diagonal=False`, and decoded logits still matched (diff 0.0) only because `prior_bank.py:1116` infers `compact = canonical_frame.block_diagonal or inverse.dim() == mu_q.dim() + 2`, so the defect is currently masked. Third, `finalize_run` (`vfe3/run_artifacts.py:4205-4207`) writes `estep_final_iterate_f_per_token`, `estep_final_f_per_token` and `estep_iterate_evidence` from `results.get(...)`, which nothing sets, so they are always null (run1 `summary.json`); `scaling_analysis.py:554` copies `estep_final_f_per_token` and `scaling_analysis.py:1056` builds `f_seeds` from it, which is therefore always empty, on every `finalize_run`.

The proposed fixes are to type `baseline` as Optional and omit the key or guard the plot when it is None, to carry `block_diagonal=value.block_diagonal` in `_cpu_bank_value`, and to drop the three always-null summary keys and the `scaling_analysis` reader or publish them as explicit unavailable records. The respective falsifiers are `plot_lr_grid_heatmap` succeeding with baseline None, a bank copy with `block_diagonal` True, and a code path that assigns `results['estep_final_f_per_token']`. Reported by viz-contracts; new (item (c) is in the class of audit-2026-10-12 item 17).

### 87. block_trace_vectors builds the dense elementary block_glk basis (524 MB at K=160, H=5) under pos_phi_project_slk (low)

The defect is in `vfe3/geometry/groups.py:319-335`. `GaugeGroup.block_trace_vectors` has a block-local branch but no elementary branch, so on canonical per-head `block_glk` it falls through to `_block_trace_vectors(self.generators, self.irrep_dims)`. That call forces the lazy `_ElementaryBlockBasis` into the dense `(H d^2, K, K)` tensor. `generator_traces` (`groups.py:308-317`) already has the elementary branch. The investigator ran a descriptor spy on the tiny live route with `pos_phi='learned'` and `pos_phi_project_slk=True` and saw "after step: Tensor dense reads: 1", with the traceback `model.py:731 -> lie_ops.py:787 -> groups.py:335`; `block_glmc` with the same toggles gave 0 reads. At K=160, H=5, d=32 the dense basis is 5120*160*160*4 B = 524 MB of resident float32. The path runs on every forward through `VFEModel._pos_phi_right -> project_phi_to_slk` when `pos_phi` is `'learned'` or `'frozen'` with `pos_phi_project_slk=True` on per-head `block_glk`; it is not active under the current `pos_phi='none'`.

The proposed fix is to add the elementary branch `V[h, h d^2:(h+1) d^2] = eye(d).reshape(-1)`, as `generator_traces` does. The finding is falsified if the same spy shows `group._generator_source` still an `_ElementaryBlockBasis` after a forward with `pos_phi_project_slk=True` on `block_glk`. Reported by gauge-groups; new.

### 88. About 11 host syncs per silent live-dict step (2 per layer), plus torch-internal reads in the content-chart matrix_exp (low)

The locations are `vfe3/relational_scoring.py:1094`, `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:1293`, `vfe3/geometry/transport.py:1355`, `vfe3/contracts.py:37`, `vfe3/train.py:1022-1029` and `vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:225-228`. An executed sync-proxy probe (`probe_syncs.py`) patched `Tensor.item/tolist/__bool__/__int__/__float__/nonzero` and `torch.unique` on a tiny live-route model with L=2. The forward read the host at `contracts.py:66/62` (the scorer host read, once per layer), at `relational_operators.py:1293` (`bad.any(dim=-1).nonzero()`, the fp64-escalation check, once per layer) and at `transport.py:1355` (`torch.unique`). Two further reads were probe artifacts that a real run avoids: `prior_bank.py:1323` is skipped through `train._transfer_targets`, and `prior_bank.py:1039` fires only while the unigram table is unset. At L=4 that gives 4x2 per-layer reads plus the unique, the deferred-scope close and the fused finite gate (`train.py:1022-1029`), so about 11 per step against the 7 reported on 10-12; the increase of 4 comes from the 10-15 escalation check. The probe did not count the shared content chart, which runs `torch.linalg.matrix_exp` on an `(L,K,K)` float64 stack in the forward and again at 2K x 2K in the backward, and the repository's own measurement (`transport.py:1268-1269`) says CUDA `matrix_exp` copies its squaring counts to the host. This is reachable on the active config at every training step.

The proposed fix is the optimistic whole-forward check from audit 10-12 item 22: queue the scorer and escalation verdicts and replay the forward in float64 only if any layer flags. Optionally, the content-chart exponential could use the existing fixed-schedule `_t18` kernel with a device-side norm bound. Removing the per-layer scorer read is part of finding 3; the escalation read stays as documented. The finding is falsified if `torch.cuda.set_sync_debug_mode('warn')` around one live-dict `train_step` on CUDA reports 7 or fewer synchronizations. Reported by performance; previously reported as `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12.md` item 22 and `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12-round2.md` item 19 (count now 11, up from 7).

### 89. Diagonal LayerNorm pushforward always builds a float64 shadow graph (~45% of its ops) (low)

At `vfe3/model/block_mlp.py:356-364` the code computes `high = _layernorm_diagonal_pushforward_at(mu.double(), sigma.double(), ...)` and then `torch.where(pushed <= 0, high.to(dtype), pushed)` unconditionally, so the where keeps the fp64 branch in the autograd graph even where it is not selected. An executed op count on `block_norm_covariance_pushforward` at the live shape (`probe_ln.py`, CPU, B=1, N=128, K=160) gave 170 forward ops, 79 of them producing float64, and 387 backward ops, 170 of them float64. Over 4 layers that is about 1,000 fp64 ops per step, about 2.7% of the 37k dispatched ops, on a device with 1/64-rate FP64; the values are memory-bound O(BNK), so the cost is mostly dispatch. This is reachable on the active config (`block_norm_covariance='pushforward'` on `gaussian_diagonal`), once per layer.

The proposed fix is to evaluate the bracket in a cancellation-free float32 form (sigma_k(1-a_kk)^2 plus the off-diagonal sum), or to compute the float64 recompute under `no_grad` and attach it only where selected through a masked custom backward. Either changes rounding, so it should be gated. A 5090 profile in which the float64 pushforward kernels take under 1% of step time would falsify the finding. Reported by performance; previously touched at `docs/2026-10-11.md:67` (item 57 made the fp64 select sync-free; the unconditional shadow cost was not measured).

### 90. Stale comments on recently changed fields, including the _HISTORICAL_DEFAULT_PINS claim that routing_kl_max_reference is 'now row_min' (low)

The locations are `train_vfe3.py:350`, `ablation.py:563`, `vfe3/config.py:3044`, `vfe3/config.py:833` and `tests/test_predictive_covariance_fp64_escalation_20261015.py:5-6`. The launcher comments at `train_vfe3.py:350` and `ablation.py:563` still say `predictive_covariance_fp64_escalation ... (counted in source_recovery_elements)`, but since 2026-10-15 finding 8 the escalation has its own counter, `covariance_escalation_elements` (`relational_operators.py:104-125`), and `source_recovery_elements` stays 0 for it, as `test_nonpositive_float32_variance_is_recomputed_in_float64` asserts. The WIP pin comment at `config.py:3044` reads `routing_kl_max_reference "absolute" at e0a3bb4 (2026-10-08), now "row_min"`, while the dataclass declares `routing_kl_max_reference: str = "absolute"` at `config.py:833`; config-wiring executed a per-commit default history over all 99 `config.py` commits and found only `'absolute'` since e0a3bb4. Since this comment is the record readers use to decide which pins are needed, it misleads, although loading is unaffected because the pin value equals the current default. The escalation test module docstring still says the update is "recomputed whole in float64", while since aa7e88f only the failing sequences are recomputed. Recent-diff's evidence was read; config-wiring's history check was executed. None of this has runtime effect.

The proposed fix is to change the launcher comments to "counted in covariance_escalation_elements", to revert the pin row to 'now "absolute"' or drop it since the default never flipped, and to update the test docstring to "failing sequences". The finding is falsified if any commit or the working tree declares `routing_kl_max_reference = "row_min"`, or if the escalation still increments `source_recovery_elements`. Reported by recent-diff and config-wiring; new.

### 91. BCH composition stack/registry and GaugeGroup.gram_pinv are stranded with no production caller, and their closure guard carries the same false positive (low)

The code is at `vfe3/geometry/lie_ops.py:64-410` (including `:86-127` and `:194-319`), `vfe3/geometry/lie_ops.py:583-742` and `vfe3/geometry/groups.py:391-408`. An executed grep of `vfe3/` outside `lie_ops.py` found zero references to `compose_phi`, `compose_bch`, `get_compose`, `register_compose`, `embed_phi`, `lie_bracket_coords` or `_bch_dynkin_correction`, and `GaugeGroup.gram_pinv()` is never called, although its docstring still calls `compose_bch -> extract_phi` "the BCH positional-composition hot path". Outside `lie_ops.py`, `compose_phi` appears only in a docstring at `positional_phi.py:67` and `compose_bch` only in a docstring at `groups.py:396`; the registry key `"bch"` appears in production only at its decorator (`lie_ops.py:583`) and in one test file, and no `VFE3Config` field names a compose rule. `require_basis_closed`, `_bch_dynkin_correction`, `_fail_if_bch_residual_exceeds`, `_from_equal_diag_blocks`, `_spans_matrix_algebra`, `_bracket_closure_residual` and `lie_bracket_matrix` have no reference outside `lie_ops.py`, and the stack has 26 test references. Live positional composition is the exact product exp(X)exp(Y) through `right_phi`. In audit-2026-10-07 the chain's only caller was `retract_phi`; a grep now finds no `retract_phi`, `_retract_core` or `phi_retract_mode`, so roughly 480 lines of callee chain were left behind. The guard `require_basis_closed` uses `den = brak.norm() + eps` with eps=1e-12, an absolute floor independent of generator scale. Executed, it raises "not closed" on the closed `su_n` towers `[('pq2_2',1)]` (residual 7.9e-01 by bracket norm) and `[('pq3_0',1)]`, and on `so_n` N=4/5 `'l2'` and N=4 `'l3'`, all of which have homomorphism residual at most 6e-15; it passes on `pq1_0+pq1_1` and every block-local group. `CompactBlockElement` (`lie_ops.py:413`) is live and not part of this finding.

Gauge-groups proposes deleting the composition registry, its closure guard and `GaugeGroup.gram_pinv` (or moving them to a test helper), and, if any part is kept, measuring closure relative to ||G_a||_F ||G_b||_F as in the pullback fix. Dead-code proposes retiring the registry and its BCH helpers with their tests, or labeling them test-only, keeping `CompactBlockElement`. The finding is falsified by a production import or call of `compose_phi`, `compose_bch`, `get_compose`, `require_basis_closed` or `GaugeGroup.gram_pinv` under `vfe3/`, by a config field that selects a composition rule, or by `require_basis_closed` accepting `get_group('su_n')(27, group_n=3, irrep_spec=[('pq2_2',1)], dtype=torch.float64).generators`. Reported by gauge-groups and dead-code; the stranded chain was previously reported at `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-07.md:404-472` (`phi_retract_mode -> retract_phi -> compose_bch` unreachable) and is partly remediated since, because the callers were removed but the chain was not; the closure-guard false positive is new.

### 92. Retired shared free-energy leftovers: test-only F row evaluators with a stale 'single typed boundary' comment, always-empty E-step trace field, a figure panel that never draws (low)

The locations are `vfe3/free_energy.py:204-519` (including `:339-347` and `:349-509`), `vfe3/metrics.py:277-369`, `vfe3/inference/e_step.py:401-404`, `vfe3/model/model.py:1607-1610`, `vfe3/viz/figures.py:2121-2131` and `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:4290-4303`. The comment at `free_energy.py:345-346` says the rows and the evaluator are "the SINGLE typed boundary the diagnostics route their belief assembly through", but the only caller of `_belief_free_energy_rows` and `hierarchical_free_energy_terms` is `metrics.free_energy_terms` (`metrics.py:343-360`), which a grep finds only in `tests/test_metrics.py`; the live canonical diagnostic sums its rows by hand (`model.py:2142-2143`). Inside `free_energy_terms` the reported `attention_entropy` uses `torch.log(beta.clamp(min=eps))` (`metrics.py:346`) while its total goes through the xlogy rows (`free_energy.py:500-501`), the floored form that `free_energy.py:328-330` says breaks the Gibbs-envelope derivative, though for beta >= 1e-12 the value difference is below 1e-10. A transitive reachability scan from the entry scripts and the `register_*` decorators (executed) finds `free_energy.log_partition`, `reduced_free_energy`, `free_energy`, `hierarchical_free_energy_terms`, `_belief_free_energy_rows`, `_reduce_row`, `BeliefFreeEnergyRows`, `HierarchicalFreeEnergyTerms` and `metrics.free_energy_terms` unreachable from production; only `tests/test_free_energy.py` and `tests/test_metrics.py` reach them. `e_step.py:403` runs `state_record.update(sequence_index=..., beliefs=[belief], free_energy=[], ...)` and nothing appends to it; the probe measured `snapshot.trace_free_energy.numel() == 0` on all four routes, so in `plot_iterate_trajectory` (registered as `'estep_convergence'`) the branch `if fe.size and trace.get("free_energy_available", True):` at `figures.py:2126` never draws panel A. `collect_estep_depth_sensitivity` ignores tokens, targets and depths and returns `_conditional_unavailable_probe("collect_estep_depth_sensitivity", cfg=model.cfg)`. All of this is unreachable under every valid config (projected routes only).

Variational proposes dropping the "single typed boundary" sentence or routing `_canonical_free_energy_diagnostics` through `hierarchical_free_energy_terms`, and using xlogy for the reported entropy in `free_energy_terms`, or retiring the four test-only oracles together. Dead-code proposes either labeling the F evaluators as test-only references or removing them together with the `free_energy=[]` trace field, `DiagnosticSnapshot.trace_free_energy` and panel A in one change. The finding is falsified by a production caller outside `tests/` of `metrics.free_energy_terms`, `_belief_free_energy_rows`, `hierarchical_free_energy_terms` or `free_energy()`, or a production path that appends to `state_record['free_energy']`. Reported by variational and dead-code; previously reported at `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-07.md:122` and `:1291` (`free_energy()` has no production caller), `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12.md` item 17 (depth-sensitivity stub), the round-1 dead-code item on the test-only metric registry (`metrics.py:2049-2190`), and `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12-round2.md` Dead code, all still open; the stale comment is new.

### 93. Leftovers of retired divergence_family/renyi_order: non-KL functionals, alpha != 1 kernels, unselectable PriorBank/CG knobs (low)

The code is at `vfe3/families/base.py:574-719`, `vfe3/families/gaussian.py:316-345`, `vfe3/families/gaussian.py:680-800`, `vfe3/model/prior_bank.py:731-732`, `vfe3/model/prior_bank.py:791-796`, `vfe3/model/prior_bank.py:2386-2424`, `vfe3/model/cg_coupling.py:47-65`, `vfe3/free_energy.py:110-183` and `vfe3/config.py:2582-2587`. `config.py:2584` pins `"divergence_family": "renyi", "renyi_order": 1.0` in `_RETIRED_PINNED_FIELDS` and refuses any other saved value (`tests/test_config_retired_fields_20261002.py:30`), and `VFEModel` builds `PriorBank` (`model.py:359-372`) without either argument, so `pb.divergence_family` is always `'renyi'` and `pb.renyi_order` always 1.0; every production `self_divergence*` call keeps the defaults. The keys `'squared_hellinger'`, `'bhattacharyya'` and `'jeffreys'` appear in production only at their registrations (`families/base.py:702-719`) and the re-export list (`divergence.py:44-46`), with 2 to 3 test files each. An executed probe wrapped every `_FUNCTIONALS` and `_FUNCTIONALS_PER_COORD` entry and `DiagonalGaussian/FullGaussian.renyi_closed_form/renyi_per_coord`, then ran forward, backward, `diagnostics()` and `build_diagnostic_snapshot` on four routes (live `diagonal_chunked`, live `gaussian_full/full_chunked`, and the default test profile with diagonal and with full family) and recorded zero calls. The `'renyi'` key at alpha=1 (KL) is live through `self_divergence_for_alpha` (`model.py:1344, 2129`); the rest is reachable from tests only.

Per the standing rule the investigator proposes leaving it and reporting it; if it is removed, the three non-KL functionals, the alpha != 1 branches and the PriorBank and CG `divergence_family`/`renyi_order` parameters should go with their tests in one change. Any config, launcher or production call that passes a divergence other than `'renyi'`, or a Rényi order other than 1, would falsify it. Reported by dead-code; new (the retirement itself is recorded in commit 2f2772f, 2026-10-02).

### 94. Single-entry transport registry leaves dead metadata branches and constant summary keys named after regime_ii_covariant (low)

The locations are `vfe3/inference/e_step.py:41-107`, `:109-121`, `:150-180`, `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:5026-5058`, `:5270-5271` and `vfe3/model/model.py:493-497`. There is one registration, `@register_transport("flat", ...)` (`transport.py:513`), which sets none of `needs_mu`, `needs_sigma`, `batch_independent`, `state_builder` or `runtime_exactness_key`; `transport_mode` is a retired pinned field (`config.py:2586`), and `model.py:493` hard-codes `get_transport_registration("flat")` and raises if it has a `state_builder`. As a result the `registration.batch_independent` strip (`e_step.py:98`), the `needs_mu`/`needs_sigma` gates (`:173-176`) and the runtime-exactness branches in `run_artifacts` (`:5033-5058`) cannot run in a model, and every summary writes `"regime_ii_covariant_exact": True` and `"regime_ii_covariant_exactness": "not_applicable"`. `runtime_exactness_key` appears only in `transport.py` and `run_artifacts.py`, in 0 test files; an executed probe wrapping `e_step._transport` recorded 0 calls on the four routes, and the `_can_fuse_flat` docstring (`e_step.py:113-116`) still cites the retired `glk`, `so_k` and `tied_block_glk` groups. The metadata branches run only with transports registered inside `tests/test_cleanup_geometry.py`. The proposed fix keeps the registry as the extension seam but renames the two summary keys to transport-neutral names (or drops them) and updates the docstrings that cite retired groups. A second production transport registration, or a config path that selects a key other than `'flat'`, would falsify it. Reported by dead-code; new.

### 95. GEMM-fallback slice counter is incremented but never read, logged or reset (low)

At `vfe3/relational_scoring.py:1190` the code runs `_GEMM_FALLBACK_SLICES += len(fallback_slices)` when a slice goes to the direct tiles (`relational_scoring.py:63-85`, `:1186-1190`), but `gemm_fallback_slices()` and `reset_gemm_fallback_slices()` have no caller in `vfe3/` or the root scripts and are referenced only by `tests/test_round2_projection_numerics_20261009.py`, which is also the only reference to `_GEMM_EXPANSION_TOL`. Every other per-device counter (source recovery, SPD repair and ceiling, covariance ceiling, fp64 escalation, LayerNorm trace cap, mu-trust and decode-logdet fallbacks) is imported, read and reset by `train.py` (`train.py:83-93, 2110-2125, 4421-4425`). Because this one is a process-global int with no reset, in-process sweep cells would also accumulate it across cells. The evidence was read, and the path is reachable through the opt-in `pair_kl_gemm_tol` (`config.py:838`; `None` on the launcher). The proposed fix adds `gemm_fallback_slices` to the run-total counter columns in `train.py` and resets it with the others at train start; a production read of the counter or an equivalent metrics column would falsify the finding. Reported by dead-code; new.

### 96. Duplicated launch helpers with drifted _banner copies; run_training and rename aliases are dead (low)

The locations are `train_vfe3.py:357-387`, `vfe3/train.py:5660-5734`, `train_vfe3.py:453-464`, `train_baseline.py:223-233`, `vfe3/experiment.py:36-76` and `train_vfe3.py:527-530`. After stripping docstrings and annotations, the AST is identical for `train_baseline._reserve_directory`, `train_vfe3._reserve_directory` and `experiment.reserve_directory`, and `experiment.rename_run_by_test_ppl` duplicates `train_vfe3._rename_run_by_test_ppl`. The banners have drifted: `vfe3/train.py:5660` prints the `'[live/dead probe failed]'` probe_note but no auxiliary block-MLP rate, while `train_vfe3.py:357` prints `'auxiliary M-LRs: block_mlp=...'` and the `full_corpus_tokens` coverage line but drops probe_note. `_rename_run_by_ppl` and `_rename_run_by_validation_ppl` (`train_vfe3.py:529-530`) have zero references in `vfe3/`, `tests/` or the scripts, and `run_training` (`train.py:5696`) is called only from `tests/test_train.py` and `tests/test_cuda_zero_device_gates_20260929.py`. The evidence was read; `train_vfe3._banner` is live, `vfe3/train._banner` is reachable only through `run_training`, and the aliases are unreachable. The proposed fix has `train_vfe3` and `train_baseline` import `vfe3.experiment.reserve_directory` and `rename_run_by_test_ppl`, and deletes `run_training` with its private `_banner` and the two aliases; an importer of the aliases or a production caller of `run_training` or `vfe3.train._banner` would falsify it. Reported by dead-code; previously reported as `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-10.md` item 63 and in the `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12.md` Simplification section, still open.

### 97. Hygiene: unused imports and locals, undefined annotation name, ignored parameters, stale launcher comment (low)

An executed `ruff --isolated --select F401,F841,F821` reports 8 problems. The unused imports are `warnings` (`ablation.py:79`), `typing.Union` (`vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:6`), `List` (`vfe3/paper/assemble.py:23`), `Mapping` (`vfe3/posthoc/evaluate.py:31`), `List` (`vfe3/posthoc/feature_probes.py:26`) and `dataclasses.field` (`vfe3/posthoc/report.py:39`); the unused local is K in `H, K = len(self.irrep_dims), self.input_dim` (`relational_operators.py:1111`); and `'torch.Tensor | GaugeGroup'` at `vfe3/geometry/lie_ops.py:763` names `GaugeGroup` with no import or `TYPE_CHECKING` block. The `relational_operators` items are present in HEAD, so they predate the WIP. `group` is accepted positionally by `e_step` and `e_step_iteration` (`vfe3/inference/e_step.py:198`, `:337`) and never read, although `e_step_shared_kwargs` says it holds "exactly the configuration the projected receiver reads". `VFEModel.diagnostics` and `diagnostics_per_layer` (`vfe3/model/model.py:2494`, `:2555`) take `log_likelihood`, documented as accepted for call-site compatibility and unused, and a grep for `log_likelihood=` outside `free_energy.py` and `metrics.py` finds no caller passing it. The launcher comment at `train_vfe3.py:350` says escalations are counted in `source_recovery_elements`, but they are counted in `_COVARIANCE_ESCALATION_COUNTS` and reported as `covariance_escalation_elements` (`relational_operators.py:107-124`; `train.py:5272`). This applies on every config. The proposed fix removes the unused imports and local, imports `GaugeGroup` under `TYPE_CHECKING` in `lie_ops.py`, corrects the launcher comment, and drops the ignored parameters when those call sites are next edited; ruff no longer reporting these codes, or a caller that passes `log_likelihood` or reads `group` inside `e_step`, would falsify it. Reported by dead-code; new.

### 98. Stale group lists and claims in docstrings, config comments and README after the 2026-10-16 group buildouts (low)

The locations are `vfe3/geometry/groups.py:829-831`, `vfe3/geometry/irreps.py:8-16`, `vfe3/config.py:373-374`, `train_vfe3.py:225`, `README.md:33`, `README.md:119`, `README.md:517` and `vfe3/model/cg_coupling.py:74-75`. `_build_su_n` says "built so far are the trivial pq0_0 and the adjoint pq1_1", but M2 builds every (p, q) with p + q <= 6 (the probes built `pq2_2`, `pq3_0` and others); it also calls the result "the irrep of highest weight (p, q)", which for N >= 4 is the family p w_1 + q w_{N-1} and not every SU(N) irrep (it misses Lambda^2 C^4, for example). The `irreps.py` module docstring lists only the `'so:l'` family although `'so:w/wp/wm'` and `'su:pq'` are registered. `config.py:373-374` says shared `block_glk` "refuses pullback_group", but an executed tiny shared `block_glk` model with `pullback_group` builds (`pullback_capability='pullback_per_block'`) and its `train_step` moves phi (dphi 9.3e-4). `train_vfe3.py:225` says "shared: block_glk, so_n only", but `su_n` is shared-native. README:119 (gaussian_block frames) omits `block_sl`, `block_borel`, `block_unipotent` and `su_n`, all of which declare `gaussian_block`; README:517 limits `phi_frame_condition_max` to `block_glk` and `block_glmc`, while `FRAME_CONDITION_GROUPS` (`gauge_optim.py:1121`) includes `block_sl`; README:33 says the covariant path is "without RoPE", but comparison-space RoPE (`comparison_invariant`) passed the end-to-end covariance probe at 1e-11. The `CGCoupling` signature comments say "N of SO(N)" and `'so'`, but `su` is supported. This is documentation only. The proposed fix updates these lines to the current registry, M2 irrep coverage, the shared-tying pullback admission and comparison-RoPE covariance; config refusing `pullback_group` for `gauge_tying='shared'` `block_glk`, or `su_n` refusing labels other than `pq0_0`/`pq1_1`, would falsify it. Reported by gauge-groups; new.

### 99. Damped-eigh docstrings are stale (retract_spd_full backward is the exact Loewner adjoint) and the gap-eps computation is dead work (low)

The locations are `vfe3/geometry/retraction.py:96-102`, `:281-282`, `:334-335` and `:669-672`. `_SymmetricSpectralMap.forward` and `_SymmetricSpectralMapPair.forward` call `_eigh_damped(symmetric, _rel_gap_eps(symmetric))` inside a custom Function forward, which runs with autograd disabled, and their backward computes `_loewner_adjoint` from the saved eigenpairs, so the Lorentzian damping never acts. The `retract_spd_full` docstring nevertheless says it uses the gap-regularized `_eigh_damped` "so the unrolled backward stays finite on a degenerate spectrum", and `_eigh_damped`'s docstring calls itself the drop-in "on the full-cov SPD retraction paths". In executed probe p12, with `_EighDamped.backward` wrapped by a counter, a backward through `retract_spd_full` at Sigma = I (fully degenerate) made 0 calls and the gradient was finite; gradcheck at Sigma = I also passed (p1). The `_rel_gap_eps` reduction (`abs().amax` over the whole `(B*N, K, K)` stack) is computed and discarded on every forward. This is reached on every call of `retract_spd_full` (projected_gradient + euler + gaussian_full) and every `_symmetric_spectral_map` caller. The proposed fix calls `safe_eigh` directly in the two forwards and rewords the docstrings to credit the exact Daleckii-Krein (Loewner) adjoint with its repeated-eigenvalue limit. A call path where the spectral map's eigh output carries an autograd graph (for example a double backward that re-enters `_EighDamped`) would falsify it. Reported by transport-geometry; new.

### 100. README lists gaussian_block as a projected_gradient family, but the config refuses it (low)

`README.md:665-667` says `projected_gradient` takes `family` in {`gaussian_diagonal`, `gaussian_full`, `gaussian_block`}, but in executed probe p10 `tiny_config(family='gaussian_block', attention_mode='projected_gradient', relational_receiver_update='euler')` raised "family=gaussian_block keeps one SPD covariance block per head ... got attention_mode=projected_gradient (requires projected_predictive)" from the check at `vfe3/config.py:1291-1294`, while the same call with `gaussian_full` was accepted; `e_step.py:250-256` also restricts `full_link` to `gaussian_diagonal` and `gaussian_full`. The fix is to drop `gaussian_block` from that list or state that it is refused there; a config with `family='gaussian_block'` and `attention_mode='projected_gradient'` that constructs without error would falsify it. Reported by transport-geometry; new.

### 101. Positional frames are labeled 'BCH positional encoding' but compose as the exact group product (low)

`vfe3/model/model.py:514-517` says "BCH positional encoding (default-off): a learned per-position Lie-algebra element table composed into the gauge frame", and the launcher group header at `train_vfe3.py:232` reads "BCH gauge-frame PE (pos_phi)". The executed composition is the exact right product U_i = exp(X_v) exp(Y_n): `build_factored_transport` takes `right_phi` and forms `exp_blocks @ right_exp` and `right_inv @ inv_blocks` (`vfe3/geometry/transport.py:1570-1607`, specifically `:1597-1604`). A read-only grep finds `compose_phi` and `compose_bch` (`vfe3/geometry/lie_ops.py:583-742`) called only from tests (`tests/test_lie_ops.py`, `tests/test_perf_equivalence.py`, `tests/test_p1_compact_phi_block_transport_20260711.py`, `tests/test_audit_20261007_mimo_tier.py`). The order-4 BCH chart is O(||X||^6) accurate, while the shipped composition has no truncation error. This describes every path with `pos_phi` set to `'learned'` or `'frozen'`. The fix renames the comments to "exact right-factor positional frame exp(X) exp(Y)" and marks `compose_bch` as a test and diagnostic utility; a model path routing positional composition through `compose_phi` with `mode='bch'` would falsify it. Reported by transport-geometry; new.

### 102. rope.py docstring wrongly says R(theta) is a group element only where the head image is full SO(d) (low)

The module docstring at `vfe3/geometry/rope.py:15-19` says R(theta) coincides with a structure-group element ONLY on blocks where the irrep image is full SO(d) (`block_glk` heads; the l1 defining rep of SO(3)). The `block_glmc` builder (`groups.py:970-973`) states, and the identity cI + sJ = realification of e^{i theta} confirms, that each RoPE block lies in U(1)^m within GL(m,C), whose image does not contain SO(d); `block_so`, `block_co` and `block_sl` also contain the pair rotations but are missing from the examples, while `block_borel` and `block_unipotent` do not, as their traits (`contains_pair_rotations=False`) record. The evidence was read; it concerns `rope_mode` `'frame'` / `'frame_value'` with the block-local groups. The fix rewords the sentence to "R(theta) lies in the group exactly when the head image contains the coordinate-pair rotations" and points to the `contains_pair_rotations` trait; the `block_glmc` realified basis failing to span cI + sJ on each coordinate pair would falsify it. Reported by transport-geometry; new.

### 103. Pivot-ratio conditioning proxy is documented as a lower bound on 1/cond but is an upper bound (low)

At `vfe3/families/gaussian.py:34-35`, `:57-62` and `:92-95` the docstring calls the pivot ratio "a free lower bound on 1/cond(L L^T)" and the comment calls it a "lower bound on 1/cond(Sigma)". An executed check (torch 2.11.0+cpu, K=20, 2000 random SPD matrices per condition number) gave min(pivot_ratio * cond) = 4.01, 5.46 and 8.4 at cond 1e2, 1e4 and 1e6 (median 10.8, 34.6, 70.9), so min(L_kk)^2/max(L_kk)^2 >= 1/cond always, which follows from lambda_min <= L_kk^2 <= Sigma_kk <= lambda_max. The calibration table at `:38-46` has the correct direction (ratio 5.69e-5 at true 1/cond = 1e-6), and the `gaussian_full` / `gaussian_block` KL fp64 escalation trigger (`_FULL_COV_KL_COND_FLOOR`) is therefore sufficient but not necessary for cond > 1e4. The fix changes the wording to "upper bound on 1/cond (a lower bound on cond)" in the docstring and both comments; an SPD matrix with min(L_kk)^2/max(L_kk)^2 < 1/cond would falsify it. Reported by numerics; new.

### 104. README claims extra E-steps change nothing under closed_form at rho = 1, false for every accepted alpha mode (low)

`README.md:646-649` says that with `relational_receiver_damping` in (0,1], "1 is the minimizer; then extra E-steps change nothing", and `vfe3/config.py:610` says only "1 = the minimizer" (see also `config.py:604`). The code refreshes alpha*(q) = c0/(b0 + D) at every iteration (`iterate_context = full_link_context_at(_full_link_context, belief.mu, belief.sigma)`, `e_step.py:285`), so rho = 1 is one majorize-minimize step on c0 log(b0+D) + G, not the minimizer. On the tiny live route switched to `projected_gradient` (executed probe p10), `closed_form` gave loss 1.9779487, 1.9901704, 1.9979 and 2.0204961 at `n_e_steps` = 1, 2, 3 and 8, approaching the `closed_form_profiled` value, which was 2.0225294 at all four depths; in a direct context probe (p2), one MM step reached profiled energy 5.9136 while the profiled root reached 2.7365. The `e_step_iteration` docstring (`e_step.py:236-238`) and `train_vfe3.py:164` ("1 = the frozen minimizer") state this correctly. It is reachable with `attention_mode='projected_gradient'`, `relational_receiver_update='closed_form'` and `n_e_steps > 1`, and applies to every alpha mode since the state-dependent modes are the only ones accepted after constant alpha was removed.

The proposed fix changes the README parenthetical and `config.py:610` to say that rho = 1 minimizes the energy with alpha frozen at the current iterate, that further E-steps continue the MM iteration toward the `closed_form_profiled` fixed point, and that only `closed_form_profiled` makes extra E-steps idempotent. A `projected_gradient` model with `closed_form`, rho = 1 and `lambda_alpha_mode='state_dependent'` giving bitwise-identical loss at `n_e_steps` = 1 and 2 would falsify it. Reported by variational; new.

### 105. Table-Fisher docstring states the KL/(r^2/2) ratio without its factor 2 (low)

The docstring at `vfe3/table_optim.py:23-26` says that along +Δl the exact ratio KL/(r^2/2) is (e^x - 1 - x)/x^2 with x = √2 r, equal to 1.049 at r = 0.1 and 1.158 at r = 0.3. Along +Δl, KL(p+||p) = ½(e^x - 1 - x) and r²/2 = x²/4, so the ratio is 2(e^x - 1 - x)/x²; executed, (e^x-1-x)/x² at r=0.1 is 0.5244, while the factor-2 form gives the quoted 1.049 and 1.158, and at the live trust radius r=0.5 (`m_table_update_mode='fisher'`) the worst-case ratio is 1.284. An executed step on a tiny bank matched the formula (mu err 6e-8, l err 3e-8), with realized rows at Fisher norm 0.5000 and exact KL ratio 0.912 to 1.063. The fix writes the ratio as 2(e^x - 1 - x)/x^2 and optionally adds the r=0.5 value (1.284). Reading the docstring's KL under a different normalization, such as 2 KL, would falsify it. Reported by info-geometry; new (the numbers came from audit 2026-10-10 round 2 item 36; the formula typo was not reported).

### 106. ALiBi slope comment gives the power-of-two formula, wrong for H=5; capped-row comment ignores ALiBi (low)

The comment at `vfe3/config.py:479-480` says the realized head slopes are `alibi_slope * 2^(-8h/H)`. Executed, `_press_slopes(5, 1.0)` returns [0.25, 0.0625, 0.015625, 0.00390625, 0.5], while the formula gives [0.330, 0.109, 0.0359, 0.0118, 0.0039]; the code (`vfe3/attention_prior.py:42-47`) follows the Press et al. interleaved schedule for non-power-of-two H, which is correct, and at H=4 the two agree. `config.py:831` says a fully capped row under the absolute reference "routes uniformly", but under `causal_alibi` it routes by softmax of the ALiBi prior, because the prior is added after the capped cost is divided by tau (`free_energy.py:194-201`). This applies to the active config (`beta_attention_prior='causal_alibi'`, `n_heads=5`). The fix states that the slopes follow the Press reference schedule (2^(-8h/H) only for power-of-two H, interleaved otherwise) and that a fully capped row routes by the prior pi; `_press_slopes(5, 1)` returning 2^(-8h/5) would falsify it. Reported by attention; new.

### 107. Manuscripts say the tied/minimal-agent model is not implemented, but vfe3 implements and validates it (low)

`Research/manuscripts/Attention/GL(K)_attention_v2.tex:1302` says "The tied variant is neither implemented nor evaluated", and `Research/manuscripts/Attention/minimal_agent_model.tex:47` says the model "is declared and has not been implemented or evaluated", with the same claim at `:59` and `:271`. The code builds the tied record (`vfe3/model/relational_operators.py:406-419`, `:992-1009`): `raw_score_q` is S_Q, T is both key and content, `channel_log_noise` holds n_h, there is no O (`relational_operators.py:671-685`), and it uses exact Gaussian conditioning through `minimal_agent_update`. The investigator ran three variants causally on tiny models (`mean_field`, `exact`, `spread_corrected`; max past-logit change 0.0), and config validation wires them; the route is `predictive_readout='minimal_agent'` (projected_predictive, equal heads), selectable from `train_vfe3.py:151-153`. The fix changes the scope sentences to say the model is implemented as `predictive_readout='minimal_agent'` and to state what has or has not been trained or evaluated. If "implemented" in the manuscripts deliberately means "implemented in the evaluated run", the sentences only need to say so explicitly. Reported by attention; new.

### 108. Manuscript M5 table rows disagree with implemented rows (row 2 wording, missing 'prior flattened' row) (low)

Code row 2 at `vfe3/posthoc/interventions.py:890-891` keeps the trained log-prior ("Routing costs removed; positional prior retained"; `_routing_arm` `no_energy` keeps `log_prior`), and under the active `causal_alibi` prior that prior is not uniform, while the manuscript row at `Research/manuscripts/Attention/GL(K)_attention_v2.tex:1826` reads "Routing costs removed; uniform causal prior retained". Code row 3, "Positional prior flattened on the causal support", has no row in the manuscript table (`tex:1825-1835`), which lists 11 rows against the code's 12 (`interventions.py:888-910`), although the supplementary text reports a flattened-prior number. The evidence was read and concerns the `interventions_table` output pasted into `tab:placeholder_interventions`. The fix aligns the manuscript table to the 12 implemented rows and the code's row-2 wording; a manuscript revision whose M5 rows match `INTERVENTIONS` row for row would falsify it. Reported by posthoc-paper; new.

### 109. channel_noise_mstep docstring justifies the n -> 0 boundary with an 'unbounded below' case the readout cannot produce (low)

The docstring at `vfe3/model/minimal_agent.py:794-801` says a direction with v_jk = e_jk = 0 makes F unbounded below (m_j log n -> -inf). The code forms E_j = sum_i beta_ij (A_i^+ + (a_i^+ - b_j)(a_i^+ - b_j)^T) (lines 822-829) with A_i^+ = S_h C_i^+ S_h^T positive definite, so e_jk >= sum_i beta_ij q_k^T A_i^+ q_k > 0 whenever m_j > 0, a term with m_j = 0 is identically 0, and when v_jk = 0 with m_j > 0 the term e/(v+n) -> +inf dominates; F is therefore bounded below on n > 0 for every state the readout passes in. The behavior itself is sound (NaN plus a flag when U = 0 or best == 0, lines 867-869, because a constrained optimum at n ~ 0 has no log-n representative); only the stated reason is wrong. The evidence was read, and this is a diagnostic of the mean-field minimal agent under `diagnostics=True`. The fix states that F is bounded below because A_i^+ is positive definite and that the boundary flag marks a constrained optimum at, or within 1e-12 U of, n = 0. A readout state in which some m_j > 0 has a direction with e_jk = 0, which requires a singular posterior query covariance A_i^+, would falsify it. Reported by gap-opt-in-readout-and-link-routes; new (the boundary flag itself answers `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12-round2.md` M2).

### 110. parameter_report counts a parameter with an all-zero gradient as live (low)

At `vfe3/train.py:5583-5586` `parameter_report` computes `dead_names = [name for name, p in named if p.requires_grad and p.grad is None]` and `live = sum(... p.grad is not None)`, so a parameter whose gradient is connected but identically zero counts as live. Executed on the `projected_gradient` + `gaussian_full` + pushforward config, the layer-1 `raw_score_q/k` came back with all-zero `.grad` tensors rather than None, so the banner reports them live; the same holds, benignly, for `gauge_gate` `fc1` and the low-rank edge maps at their zero-factor initialization. The documented `phi_embed` incident is the class this misses whenever autograd connects the parameter but the contribution cancels, and `parameter_motion_report` catches a frozen parameter only at the end of the run. This runs in every run's banner (`_banner` in `train_vfe3.py` calls `parameter_report`). The fix reports parameters whose probe gradient is exactly zero as a separate "zero-gradient at probe" list beside `dead_names`. A probe config where a structurally gradient-dead parameter comes back with grad None, or evidence that `parameter_report` is not used for dead-parameter diagnosis, would falsify it. Reported by gradients-tests; new.

### 111. Live-route gradient test passes if any single parameter gets a nonzero gradient (low)

The only backward test on the tiny launcher route (`live_config`), at `tests/test_live_route_profile.py:48-56`, collects `grads = [p.grad for p in model.parameters() if p.grad is not None]`, asserts `grads and all(finite)`, and asserts `any(bool((g != 0).any()) for g in grads)`, so a dead table, a severed detach or a wrong custom backward on the live route would all pass. An executed directional central-difference check of every parameter on the same `live_config` model in float64 (`fd_variants.py`) runs in about 1 s, with worst relative error 1.4e-5 at eps=1e-5 once the LayerNorm-gamma amax tie is broken and 1.0e-6 on the pure-path overrides. The fix asserts a non-None gradient for every `requires_grad` parameter and adds a float64 directional-FD check per parameter with the block-norm gamma moved off its all-ones tie; another test that already checks per-parameter gradients or FD on `live_config` would falsify it. Reported by gradients-tests; related to `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-12.md` item 24 (live-route profile), which added the profile but not a gradient check.

### 112. Two 'launcher accepts' tests are tautological: they overwrite train_vfe3.config and assert the values they set (low)

`test_launcher_accepts_mode_and_covariance_controls` (`tests/test_gauge_block_mlp_integration.py:197-206`) runs `monkeypatch.setattr(train_vfe3, "config", local)` with `local = config_values(block_mlp_mode="coordinate", ...)`, then asserts `selectors <= set(train_vfe3.config)`, which is always true since `config_values` holds every field, and that `test_config(**train_vfe3.config)` returns the values it set. `test_launcher_accepts_projected_depth` (`tests/test_predictive_multilayer.py:24-30`) does the same with `config_values(**asdict(_depth_cfg(layers)))`. Neither reads the real launcher dictionary, so neither can fail on a launcher change; in the current working tree the launcher omits 39 `VFE3Config` fields, among them `predictive_covariance_floor_mode`, `kl_max` and `sigma_max`, and both tests still pass. The evidence was read. The fix asserts against `tests.profiles.launcher_values()` (the real dict) or drops the two tests, since `test_live_route_profile` already covers the launcher route; either test failing after a selector key is removed from the real `train_vfe3.config` would falsify it. Reported by gradients-tests; new.

### 113. Known red test test_launchers_leave_kl_max_to_its_eight_k_resolution raises KeyError on the working-tree launcher (low)

The investigator ran `python -m pytest tests/test_audit_20261012_round2_config.py tests/test_block_mlp_launchers.py tests/test_config_markdown_20261007.py` with the thread caps and got `FAILED ...::test_launchers_leave_kl_max_to_its_eight_k_resolution`, raised at `assert launcher["kl_max"] is None` (`tests/test_audit_20261012_round2_config.py:55-61`) with `KeyError: 'kl_max'`. The uncommitted `train_vfe3.py` dict (`train_vfe3.py:126-345`) no longer carries `kl_max` (git diff line 255 removes it), and an AST comparison counts 39 `VFE3Config` fields missing from it, contrary to the AGENTS.md rule that every field appears there. The launcher dict is the user's WIP and is not judged here; the test-suite state on the CPU lane is. The fix is either to restore the keys to the launcher at their defaults, as AGENTS.md requires, or to make the test read `config.get('kl_max')` with the default as the documented fallback; the test passing on the current working tree would falsify it. Reported by gradients-tests; previously reported in `docs/2026-10-15.md` (CPU lane failures from "the launcher kl_max key").

## Improvements and optimizations (info)

### 114. Improvement: log the kernel router's own label free energy -tau log Z of its clamped cost (info)

This one sits in `vfe3/model/model.py:2245-2276` and `vfe3/free_energy.py:229-251`. The active route never reports the free energy of the router it actually uses. `_relational_diagnostics` sets `self_coupling`, `belief_coupling`, `attention_entropy` and `total` to NaN unless `diagnose_canonical_free_energy` is on (`:2264-2276`). That diagnostic scores a different router, the flat-alignment D^h in reference coordinates, and not the S_Q/S_K comparison KL with its P(G) projection and `kl_max` clamp. Its beta* also differs from the model's beta (`canonical_beta_tv` 0.237 and 0.610 on the tiny live run, p7). The capture already holds the router's clamped cost (`score_energy_pre=score.detach()`, `relational.py:341`), the log-prior and tau. So `reduced_free_energy(score, tau=routing_temperature, log_prior)` would give, per head and row, -tau log sum_j pi_ij exp(-D_ij/tau). That is tau times the surprisal of Theorem thm:probabilistic_status item 1 (`GL(K)_attention_v2.tex:1098`), the objective whose exact minimizer is the beta the model uses. The cost is one O(BHN^2) logsumexp over tensors already in memory. Separately, the investigator executed a check that the `reduced_free_energy` identity holds to 1.4e-14 with an ALiBi prior and per-head tau (p1). The rest of the evidence comes from reading the code. The proposal is a diagnostic on the active route (`projected_predictive`, kernel readout): add per-layer columns such as `relational_routing_free_energy`, computed as `reduced_free_energy(cap['score_energy_pre'], tau, snapshot.log_prior)`, averaged over scorable rows and summed over heads. The finding would be falsified by an existing `metrics.csv` column on the kernel route that already reports logsumexp_j(log pi - D/tau) of the router's clamped cost. Reported by variational; new.

### 115. Fused backward routes d_bias through a TF32 GEMM instead of an fp32 reduction; CUDA parity test never runs under the live 'high' policy (info)

The locations are `vfe3/kernels/kl_decode.py:23-26`, `vfe3/kernels/kl_decode.py:138`, `vfe3/kernels/kl_decode.py:161-166` and `tests/test_kl_decode_triton_20261012.py:342-368`. The module docstring says the backward GEMMs run under the caller's policy "as the torch route's autograd backward does". That is true for d_lhs and d_rhs. For d_bias it is not: the torch route gets d_bias from the `sum_to_size` reduction of the `+ bias` broadcast in fp32. The fused route instead takes it as the last column of `torch.mm(G.t(), lhs_one)`, where `lhs_one = [lhs, 1]` has width 2K+1 = 257, a leading dimension that is not 16-byte aligned. The investigator emulated TF32 input rounding on CPU (S=2048, V=512, K=16, tau=0.1, round-to-nearest; this was executed). The d_bias relative error was 7.9e-8 for the fp32 sum against 1.2e-4 for the TF32 GEMM, and 4.5e-4 with truncation. The decode-table gradients did not change beyond noise (d_mu_v 3.8e-4 against 3.3e-4, d_sigma_v 3.9e-4 against 3.5e-4). The reason is that the TF32 error in d_rhs (2.0e-4), which both routes share, dominates. Under emulated TF32, query-side gradients carry about 5e-4 to 1e-3 relative error on both routes. `tests/conftest.py:86-96` forces IEEE matmul, so `test_cuda_fused_route_matches_float64_like_the_torch_route` measures none of this. Nothing was verified on GPU, and the alignment cost of N=257 was not measured.

The path is reachable under the active config, which has `decode_ce_triton=True` and `fp32_matmul_precision='high'`, so on CUDA the backward GEMMs run in TF32. The forward cross term is pinned to IEEE by `_cross_term`. The proposed fix computes `g_bias[v0:v1] = G.sum(0)` and `g_rhs[v0:v1] = G^T lhs` (width 2K, aligned) in place of the ones-column GEMM, and adds one CUDA parity case under `torch.set_float32_matmul_precision('high')`. A GPU measurement under 'high' would falsify the finding if it showed the fused d_bias error equal to the torch route's, or if it showed the 2K+1-wide GEMM no slower than a 2K-wide GEMM plus a column sum. Reported by gap-live-decode-triton-kernels; new. The TF32 backward of the pair-KL GEMM was accepted in `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-09-round2.md` item 32, but that item does not cover the decode backward.

### 116. fisher_group trust radius bounds the Frobenius norm of the body generator, not the Fisher norm, leaving per-step KL uncontrolled across rows (info)

At `vfe3/gauge_optim.py:916-917` (with `vfe3/config.py:759`) the trust step is `right_norm = torch.linalg.vector_norm((learning_rate * xi).reshape(rows, -1), dim=-1); trust_scale = (trust_radius / right_norm.clamp_min(tiny)).clamp(max=1.0)`. Here xi = U^{-1} W U is the body generator, so the bound is extrinsic. The direction itself is the completed belief-Fisher natural gradient. This was verified: the `fisher_type_solve` residual is about 1e-16 at λ = 0.7, 1, 2 and 3, and the generalized spectrum equals `fisher_spectrum_bounds`. The investigator ran `completed_fisher_operator` at s = (0.1, 1, 3, 10), mu = 0. Unit-Frobenius generators E_jk had completed Fisher norm² ranging from 0.02 (E_30) to 200 (E_03), a ratio of 1e4. A fixed Frobenius radius therefore admits per-row KL changes that differ by four orders of magnitude. The table step, by contrast, bounds its rows in the Fisher norm (`table_optim.py:166-175`). This is opt-in only, through `m_phi_update_mode='fisher_group'` with either retraction; the live config uses adamw. The proposed fix scales each row by min(1, R / sqrt(eta² <W, M(W)>)) using the operator that is already formed, so the trust radius has the same KL meaning as `m_table_fisher_trust_radius`. Either of two things would falsify the finding: code elsewhere in the fisher_group staging that rescales xi by its Fisher norm before `stage_group_log_candidate`, or a spectrum showing the Fisher/Frobenius ratio bounded near 1 for trained row statistics. Reported by info-geometry; new.

### 117. LR width-transfer reruns the identical x1.0 baseline cell once per rate (12 of 60 cells duplicated) (info)

In `ablation.py:1262-1288` and `ablation.py:294-303`, `_lr_width_transfer_arms` emits `{embed_dim, n_heads, field: base*multiplier}` for every (width, field, multiplier) combination. At multiplier 1.0, every field's arm is the same as the width's baseline config. The investigator counted the cells under the working-tree `LR_WIDTH_TRANSFER` and `BASELINE_CONFIG` (executed): 60 cells but only 48 distinct configs, so 20% of the 1500-step runs repeat a config that already exists. Because `deterministic=False`, the repeats are also independent noise draws of the reference cell, which means each rate's best multiplier is chosen against a different replicate of x1. This happens whenever `LR_WIDTH_TRANSFER['enabled']=True` and 1.0 is in `'multipliers'`. The current WIP lists [0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0] over 3 widths and 5 rates. The proposed fix emits one shared x1 cell per width (labeled, for example, 'K=..,H=..,baseline') and has `fit_lr_width_transfer` read it as multiplier 1 for every field. The finding would be falsified if `run_sweep` deduplicates cells by effective config hash, so that only 48 trainings actually run. Reported by recent-diff; new.

### 118. Baseline LR sweep adopts a grid-edge winner without a flag; ablation analysis reports no paired differences despite shared seeds and data order (info)

At `train_baseline.py:333-364` and `train_baseline.py:484-491`, `lr_sweep` returns `min(finite, key=best_val_ppl)` and `main` sets `cfg.lr` to that value. The only edge check is a comment ("a winner at the top of the grid ... means the grid should be extended"), while `fit_lr_width_transfer` already computes `at_grid_edge`. At `ablation.py:4625-4654`, `analyze_sweep` prints only per-arm mean, SD and CV. Every arm is trained on identical (seed, `DATA_SEED`) pairs, so a per-seed paired difference against the baseline arm would have much smaller variance than an unpaired comparison of two SDs. The evidence comes from reading the code. It applies to `train_baseline.py` with `LR_SWEEP` set (shipped: 7e-4 to 6e-3) and to any multi-seed ablation sweep. The proposed fix records `at_grid_edge` in `lr_sweep.json` with a warning, and adds a paired-difference table (mean, SE, t-interval on n-1 df) against the baseline label for seeded sweeps. An `lr_sweep.json` whose winner is the largest grid value and carries an edge flag would falsify the first half. Reported by experiments; related to `docs/audits/audit-2026-10-09.md` (baseline lr 3e-3 at the grid edge).

### 119. Improvements: aggregate divergence indicators across seeds; compute the seed design once (info)

At `multiseed_analysis.py:936-943`, `summary.json` carries `skipped_steps` and `accepted_update_frac` (36.41 summary keys), but `SCALAR_KEYS` leaves them out. Under the default headline key `best_val_ppl`, a seed that diverged after its best step still contributes a normal pre-divergence value, and nothing in the digest shows the divergence. Separately, at `multiseed_analysis.py:1208-1217`, `main` calls `_requested_seed_design` once directly, once per scalar key through `aggregate_scalar` (about 20 keys), once for curves and once for per-layer. Each call re-reads every config and rebuilds `VFE3Config` for the fingerprints. The evidence comes from reading the code, and it applies to `multiseed_analysis.main` on any group. The proposed fix adds `skipped_steps`, `accepted_update_frac` and final_val_ce minus the best val CE to `SCALAR_KEYS`, and passes one precomputed design into the aggregators. The finding would be falsified if the digest already flags a seed with nonzero `skipped_steps`, or if the per-call cost is negligible on real groups. Reported by gap-paper-arms-and-multiseed; new.

### 120. Two M5 rows (routing covariances -> I, M4 decomposition) cannot be measured on the active route because post-hoc scorers skip the comparison-space rotation (info)

The locations are `vfe3/posthoc/interventions.py:335-337, 595-597` and `vfe3/posthoc/routing.py:179-181`. The investigator executed a tiny live-route probe (comparison_invariant, moment_matched, pushforward, diagonal_chunked, untied bank). Rows 2, 3, 5, 7 and 9 of M5 ran. Row 4 was N/A because `attention_residual` is False, and row 8 was N/A because there are no positional frames. Row 6 was N/A with the message "comparison rope_mode: this counterfactual scorer does not apply the comparison-space rotation". Rows 10, 11 and 12 were N/A for moment_matched, pushforward and diagonal_chunked. The M4 decomposition also refuses comparison rope (`routing.py:179-181`). The audit of 2026-10-08 chose refusal over rotating the recomputed moments. As a result, the covariance-channel row and the dot-product-reduction figure have no value on the evaluated route, which uses the active `rope_mode='comparison_invariant'`. The proposed fix applies the operators' comparison-space rotation R_i to the projected moments inside `_identity_covariance_score` and `logit_components`, as `relational_score` does, and drops the refusal. A comparison-rope model on which row 6 and `routing_decomposition` return status ok would falsify the finding. Reported by posthoc-paper; previously reported as audit-2026-10-08 general-C-analysis-tests#1 and gauge-theorist#3, which were fixed there by refusing.

### 121. Logit-lens summary calls the bank 'held-out' although it defaults to the checkpoint-selection split (info)

In `vfe3/semantics/contextual.py:72` the default is `"contextual_split": "validation"`, and at `vfe3/semantics/contextual.py:1847-1848` `contextual_summary` writes `f"(logit lens on {...n_tokens} held-out tokens)"`. `SemanticRun.token_stream` identity-checks exactly the selection split, so the final-layer lens CE is measured on the same data that chose the checkpoint. The selection bias is small, but the label overstates independence; the test split is the untouched held-out set. The evidence comes from reading the code. It applies whenever the contextual section runs with the default split on runs whose `best_model.pt` was selected on validation, which is the usual selection split. The proposed fix either defaults `contextual_split` to 'test', or writes "validation (selection split) tokens" in the summary when the bank split equals `selection_identity['split']`. A `selection_data_identity` whose split is not 'validation' for the analyzed runs would falsify the finding. Reported by semantics; new.

### 122. Test split is first opened after training, so a missing test cache fails after the whole training budget (info)

At `train_vfe3.py:654-656`, `_run_once` builds only the train and validation loaders before `train()`. The calls `test_loader = _select_loader(DATASET, cfg, split="test")` and `_tokens_per_char(DATASET, "test")` run inside `if artifacts is not None:` after training. The same pattern is at `vfe3/experiment.py:203` and `train_baseline.py:318`. A `FileNotFoundError` or identity error at that point arrives after max_steps=120000 steps, and `summary.json` and `test_results.json` are never written. Recovery requires a resume at step == max_steps, which needs a checkpoint at the final step. That holds at the current checkpoint_interval=60000 but not in general. The evidence comes from reading the code. It applies to `train_vfe3` with `RUN_ROOT` set (live), to `experiment.run_vfe` and to `train_baseline`; `scaling.py` already opens test up front (`scaling.py:1263`). The proposed fix preflights the test cache at launch when persistence is on, for example with `cached_token_count(DATASET, 'test')` or `cache_source_identity(DATASET, 'test')` next to the validation loader build. Any launch-time access to the test cache before `train()` is called in `train_vfe3._run_once` would falsify the finding. Reported by data-io; new.

### 123. Resumed run directory holds only post-resume history while summary best_step stays run-wide (info)

The locations are `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:1880-1886`, `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:1920-1923` and `vfe3/run_artifacts.py:2747-2771`. The investigator ran `probe_resume.py` with eval and logging every 2 steps (executed). Run A's `metrics.csv` had steps [2, 4, 6, 8]. Run B, resumed from A's step_4, had [6, 8], and run C had [8]. All three restored best_val_ppl=5.940 at best_step=4, a step that does not appear in B's or C's `metrics.csv`, `val_ppl.png` or `diagnostics/*.csv`. `RunArtifacts` starts with `history=[]`, and the only link back to the source run is the `checkpoint_path` in `resume_provenance.json`. This is the documented design, with a new run_dir per resume, but the figures and the pure-path report cover only the continuation.
